# Implementation Notes

How the app works today, by topic: decisions and their reasons, endpoint and protocol discoveries, quirks, and open issues. It describes the current state, not history (git log has that). Facts about external services carry the date they were observed. Update the relevant section when a change makes something here untrue.

## Architecture & storage

### Targets & platform
- iOS-only SwiftPM app: the `StupidSocialMac` target and `.macOS` platform were removed from `Package.swift`; `@main StupidSocialApp` is the single entry point; no `os(macOS)`/`NS*` branches. `targetEnvironment(simulator)` `#else` branches in `SettingsView`/`KeychainCredentialStore` are intentional. Bare host `swift build`/`swift test` target macOS and don't build; verify with `stupid-app build`
- Reusable code lives in `StupidSocialCore` so unit tests link without the app's `@main`. Bundle id `tech.stupid.StupidSocial`
- The chat framework is the `StupidChatKit` package, a dependency from its own repository (see Chats & chat screen § StupidChatKit); `StupidSocialCore` and `StupidSocialPush` depend on it
- iOS minimum `.v18`. `Info.plist`: `UIDesignRequiresCompatibility=false`; `UIDeviceFamily` `[1, 2]` (with `[1]` iPad runs phone-boxed and always `.compact`)
- Network badge PNGs live in `Resources/` (made with ImageMagick) and are listed explicitly under `resources:` in `stupid-app.yml`, because standalone imagesets weren't copied into the bundle

### Composition & protocols
- `AppContainer` (app layer) is the composition root for stores, clients, sources, `FeedService`, `FeedViewModel`, `SettingsViewModel`; `ContentView` owns only the container, loads the cached feed during setup and starts the initial story-bar fetch once the container exists. Cleanup history/justification: `docs/ARCHITECTURE_REFACTOR_PLAN.md`
- `NotificationSource.swift` defines capability protocols (notification fetching, account validation, profile fetching, target details, stories, posting, relationship mutation, activity); `FeedService` receives capabilities directly. No source-level unread counts. Rule: capability methods must be protocol requirements, not only protocol-extension helpers, or calls through an existential hit the default implementation
- Source protocols inherit `Sendable` from `SocialSource`; `DebugNotificationSource` is `@MainActor` so fetchers can be captured in task groups
- `StoryBarViewModel` owns story bar state/actions, Instagram story ordering/pagination/optimistic updates, Spotify activity fetch/seen marking and story viewer helpers; `FeedViewModel` owns only notification feed state, refresh flags, feed errors, health check and `FeedService` access
- Single home screen: `ContentView` owns the root `NavigationStack` (path `[HomeDestination]`) with the feed as root; Search and Settings are pushed via `.navigationDestination(for:)` and have no internal `NavigationStack` or Done buttons

### Accounts (multi-account)
- Any number of accounts per network. `Account` (`Models/Account.swift`): stable `id`, `network`, `remoteId` (the network's own user id: X rest id, Instagram pk, Bluesky DID, Farcaster fid, GitHub/Spotify username, Telegram user id, Apron user id), `handle`, `displayName`, `avatarURL`, Apron `serverURL`, `addedAt`, and `AccountPreferences` (category sets, where `nil` means every category so new ones start on; Instagram stories/DM shares; Apron requested name).
- Ids: new accounts get `<network>-<uuid>`; Apron's is `apron.<server URL>`, which was already its token's Keychain item. Accounts from before multi-account support keep the network's name (`x`, `instagram`…), which is the Keychain item their credentials were already in, so migration moves no credentials.
- `AccountStore` syncs the list through iCloud KVS (`com.stupid.accounts`), generalized from the old `ApronServerSync`: one entry per account id holding the account, a removed flag and when it last changed; removals stay as tombstones; the newer change wins per account. Loopback Apron servers stay on the device. Validation status (`accounts.statuses`) and what an Apron server reported at sign-in (`accounts.apronInfo`) are per device. The Debug server is a device setting (`account.debug`), not an account. Posts `didChangeNotification` (list/record changes, here or remote) and `statusDidChangeNotification`.
- Same identity twice: adding an account whose `(network, remoteId)` is already listed updates that account and moves the new credentials to it. Two devices that each added the same account before syncing both keep their entries; `accounts` lists the older and hides the newer (not for Apron, whose user ids are per server).
- `AccountMetadataStore` is now one account's view in the old per-network shapes (`xAccount`, `instagramAccount`…), so sources kept their code: reads come from the account and its status, writes update handle/avatar/preferences and the status; writing `nil` does nothing.
- `KeychainCredentialStore.scoped(to:)` keys an account's credentials by its id (X Chat keys under `xchat.<id>`, Telegram's device-local session under the id). `deleteAccountCredentials()` deletes both. The unscoped store reads the pre-multi-account items, for migration only.
- `AccountMigration` runs once per device (`accounts.migratedLegacy`): each legacy `account.<network>` metadata or Keychain credential becomes an account with the network's name as id, with fixed `addedAt` dates so every device migrates to identical entries; X Chat keys move from `xchat` to `xchat.x`; the X "follows" category is turned on once as before; a signed-in Telegram session becomes an account; each `account.apron.servers` entry becomes an Apron account. Legacy keys are then deleted. Migrated X accounts have no `remoteId` until `SettingsViewModel.repairAccountIdentities()` (foreground) resolves it, along with missing Instagram avatars and Spotify usernames.
- `AccountServices` builds one `AccountComponents` per account (clients, sources, X Chat client/unlock/live feed, Telegram client/connection) and keeps them for the account's life, so Telegram sessions and sockets survive list changes; it rebuilds on `didChangeNotification` and registers every Telegram client with `TelegramFileURLProtocol`. `FeedService`, `StoryBarViewModel`, `ChatListViewModel` and `TelegramPushService` read its components on each use. `AppContainer` reloads chats and stories and starts live feeds when the account set changes.
- Chats: `ChatListViewModel` loads one `ChatListSource` per account (`AccountServices.chatListSources`, from `AccountComponents.chatListProvider`) plus one for every Apron server, concurrently (see Chats list below). `ChatSummary.scoped(to:)` sets `accountId` and prefixes `id` with it; Apron's source scopes its own rows to `apron.<server URL>` and lists with no account id, so the list leaves them as they are. A failed account keeps its previous rows. `ChatListViewModel.connections` holds each connected account's last load (label, error), shown at the top right of Chats only when a connection failed (while `isLoading`, a small spinner holds that place, so a reload shows before its result): the badge of each failing network and a red exclamation mark, whose popover lists only the failed connections with their errors, each with a reload button that reloads the list; errors no longer appear under the search bar. A provider that lists several accounts without one (Apron) reports each in `ChatListPage.accounts`, so one Apron server failing (such as with HTTP 503) shows as that server and keeps only that server's rows. A cancelled reload keeps the last status; an account that isn't connected (`notConfigured`) has none. Chat factories in `AppContainer` take the row's `accountId`. Chat lists cached before scoping fail to decode and reload.
- Adding requires the network to say who signed in (X `verifiedUser`, Instagram homepage viewer, Spotify username, GitHub username, Bluesky DID, Farcaster fid); otherwise nothing is kept and the error shows. The X and GitHub login WebViews use the persistent store (X limits logins from a non-persistent one), so they delete only the session cookies (`auth_token`/`ct0`/`twid`/`att`; `user_session`/`__Host-user_session_same_site`/`logged_in`/`dotcom_user`) before loading; otherwise a second login captured the first account's session.

### Credentials
- `KeychainCredentialStore`: production writes synchronizable (iCloud) Keychain first, falling back to non-synchronizable Keychain (deleting any existing non-synchronizable item first to avoid duplicate-item failures); plaintext `UserDefaults` fallback (extracted values only) writes only on simulator, because Keychain can return `errSecMissingEntitlement` (`-34018`) for pseudo-signed builds. Save returns `CredentialSaveResult` (`.synced`/`.localOnly`)
- Local/legacy values are promoted opportunistically; when local and cloud items coexist the newer Keychain modification date wins and stale local copies are removed. Accounts arrive on other devices through `AccountStore`'s KVS sync; an account whose credentials haven't synced yet reads as not configured until they do
- Simulator builds use local non-synchronizable Keychain: a Keychain access-group entitlement made pseudo-signed simulator apps fail to launch with `NSPOSIXErrorDomain Code=163`
- Notification caches stay local; account preferences sync with the account

### Storage
- SwiftData `CachedNotification` properties need declaration defaults so existing caches migrate (e.g. `unreadCount` defaults to `nil`)
- The SwiftData store uses `ModelConfiguration(groupContainer: .none)`, so it lives in the app's own container. The default (`.automatic`) put it in the App Group once the extension added one, and iOS kills an app that is suspended while holding a SQLite lock in a shared container (`0xdead10cc`, seen in build 162 when a cache save ran as the app left the foreground). Nothing in the App Group may hold a database or file lock; the extension shares only JSON files written atomically. Moving the store dropped the cached feed once, which the next refresh refills
- `StorySeenSync` mirrors Spotify (`spotifyActivitySeenTimestamps`, keyed by `spotify:user:` URI) and GitHub (`githubActivitySeenTimestamps`, keyed by `actorId`) seen dictionaries into `NSUbiquitousKeyValueStore` under `com.stupid.storyseen.spotify` / `com.stupid.storyseen.github`. Timestamps are monotonic per actor, so both sides do an element-wise `max` merge (`StorySeenSync.merged(_:_:)`) and write back to `UserDefaults` + iCloud, converging despite KVS last-writer-wins. `UserDefaults` is the fast read path; works without iCloud. Needs `com.apple.developer.ubiquity-kvstore-identifier` (`$(TeamIdentifierPrefix)$(CFBundleIdentifier)`) in `App.entitlements`; covered by `StorySeenSyncTests`
- `SpotifyActivitySeenStore` isolates the Spotify seen `UserDefaults` persistence
- Chat history is the kit's `ChatStore` (SQLite) in the app's own container, like the SwiftData store. The notification extension never opens it: it leaves pushed messages in an App Group inbox (`SharedChatStore`). See § Chats & chat screen › Chat store
- Avatars are kept on disk, stale-while-revalidate (`CachedAsyncImage(persist: true)`, kit `docs/DESIGN.md` § Images): feed avatars by `NotificationActor.avatarCacheKey` (network and id, since some networks sign avatar URLs anew), Chats rows and chat avatars by URL. The feed loads the avatars of the next 20 rows ahead, read from disk and decoded off the main thread.
- Feed refreshes replace cached notifications with SwiftData predicates (network and account) and drop removed accounts once a refresh; they had loaded every cached item on the main thread once per source.
- `CachedAsyncImage` (shared `ImageCache`, both in `StupidChatKit`) replaces `AsyncImage` for avatars. Optional stable `cacheKey` (`instagram-avatar-<userId>`, `spotify-avatar-<userURI>`) because Instagram signs `profile_pic_url` differently between `reels_tray` calls; on URL miss it keeps the keyed image while fetching
- `ImageCache` is limited by decoded size (150 MB), not count, and shares one load per URL, decoded off the main thread (`byPreparingForDisplay`). `CachedAsyncImage` seeds its state from the cache, so a cached image draws in the first frame instead of after `.task` runs. When a feed row appears, the feed preloads the avatars (first five actors) and preview images of the next 10 rows, so they are ready before they scroll into view

### Models & helpers
- `AccountStatusSnapshot` has `.networkUnavailable` and `.serviceError` besides valid/invalid/not configured. Status is set when an account connects and when the GitHub and Instagram sources see an auth failure while fetching (they mark the account invalid); there is no separate health check of every account at startup
- `NotificationActor.timestamp` is optional per-actor action time (Farcaster only); `NotificationTarget` has `imageURL` and an unused `album` field
- `Date.compactRelativeTime` (`StupidSocialCore/Date+RelativeTime.swift`): shared compact relative time (`now`, `1m`, `12h`, `1d`, `2w`, `1mo`, `1y`)
- `Date.twitter(_:)` (X's REST `EEE MMM dd HH:mm:ss Z yyyy`) and `Date.iso8601(_:)` (fractional seconds, then without) (`StupidSocialCore/Date+Parsing.swift`) parse every network's dates with formatters made once; X, Bluesky, Farcaster and GitHub use them instead of building formatters per date
- `XClient.send(_:)` runs every X REST/GraphQL request: 401/403 throws `SourceError.notConfigured`, other non-2xx `invalidResponse`; callers decode. `headers` (web client headers) and `apiHeaders` (the API minimum plus User-Agent, shared with XChat) both start from `authHeaders`; they still differ on purpose, so don't merge them without checking `scripts/x-web-client.py`
- `RefreshTiming.measure` emits an `os_signpost` interval plus duration log (subsystem `tech.stupid.StupidSocial`, category `Refresh`) around per-source fetches and cache writes in `FeedService`, story branches in `StoryBarViewModel`, Instagram tray/per-reel, Spotify buddylist/per-track

### Usage metrics
- `AppMetrics` (`StupidSocialCore/Services/AppMetrics.swift`) names every event and holds the app's public ingest key; `StupidSocialApp.init` configures it. Events: `refresh` {network, trigger `manual`/`foreground`, outcome `ok`/`error`/`cancelled`, value ms} plus `source_error` {network, trigger, and for a `DecodingError` `path`: where it failed as model key names, e.g. `notifications.[].cast.timestamp`, array positions `[]` and dictionary keys `{}` so no ids get through, the last 64 characters since the ingest rejects longer values} (both from `FeedService.fetchSourcesConcurrently`; `notConfigured` and `endpointSpikeRequired` are skips, not errors, and a refresh iOS cancelled in the background is `cancelled`, not an error), `story_source_error` {network} (Instagram reels, Spotify activity), `accounts` {network, value count} once per `AppContainer` init, `chat_open` (`ChatView.task`, which re-runs when a chat reappears after a pushed screen), `chat_search` {network} (`ChatViewModel.beginSearch`), `message_send` {network, attachment, voice, reply, outcome} and `message_send_failed` (`ChatViewModel.sendOptimistically`; Apron commands and over-size attachments don't pass through it), `message_forward` {network, from (the source network), native `yes`/`no`, outcome} and `message_forward_failed` (`AdapterChatViewModel.send(_ forward:)`; a copy's messages also count as `message_send`), `message_edit` {network, outcome} and `message_edit_failed` (`ChatViewModel.editOptimistically`; unchanged text sends nothing and isn't counted), `message_delete` {network, outcome} and `message_delete_failed` (`AdapterChatViewModel.delete`), `message_button_press` {network, outcome} and `message_button_press_failed` (`AdapterChatViewModel.press`, `.press` buttons only; links and copies aren't counted), `voice_transcribe` {network, chosen `yes`/`no` (a language picked by hand), outcome `ok`/`not_understood`/`no_speech`/`unsupported_language`/`unavailable`/`audio_unavailable`/`error`} and `voice_transcribe_failed` for `audio_unavailable` and `error` only, the others being expected (`AdapterChatViewModel.transcribeVoice`), `notification_open` / `notification_reply` (Apron and Telegram, `AppDelegate`), `post_engage` {network, action `like`/`unlike`/`repost`/`unrepost`/`bookmark`/`unbookmark`/`reply`/`quote`, outcome} and `post_engage_failed` (`PostActionBar`; `repost` on X counts opening X's repost page), `account_connect` {network, reconnect} / `account_connect_failed` / `account_remove` (`SettingsViewModel`), `story_open` and `story_post` / `story_post_failed`, `chat_reminder_set` {network, delay `5m`/`30m`/`1h`/`3h`/`tomorrow`/`custom`, delivery `pushed`/`local_only`/`push_failed`} (`ChatReminderStore.schedule`), `chat_reminder_open` {source `local`/`push` from the notification, `feed` from the row}, `chat_reminder_done` {via `chat` (Mark as Done)/`feed` (Remove Reminder)}, `chat_reminder_push_failed` {step `register`/`schedule`/`cancel`} (`ChatReminderPush`, `ChatReminderStore`); `chat_inbox_import_failed` (`ChatInbox`, a pushed message the extension left that couldn't be stored); `instagram_login` {outcome `ok`/`checkpoint`/`checkpoint_unresolved`/`missing_cookies`} (`InstagramLoginWebView`); `chat_mute`/`chat_unmute`/`chat_archive`/`chat_unarchive` {network, outcome} (+ `<name>_failed` errors)
- `StupidMetrics` is a package dependency from `https://github.com/stephancill/stupid-metrics-swift` (public, so anyone can build the app; the service repository stays private), pinned in `Package.resolved`. It was vendored until `stupid-app` 0.0.27 built package dependencies
- Settings → Advanced → Share Usage Data mirrors `StupidMetrics.isEnabled`; turning it off drops the queue and the install id
- The notification service extension doesn't report: push decrypt failures stay in `PushExtensionLog`. MetricKit diagnostics cover the app process only
- Dashboard: `https://metrics.stupidtech.net/dashboard/apps/tech.stupid.StupidSocial`

### Preview / demo mode
- App Review preview content works in release builds (no `#if DEBUG` around `DemoData`, `FeedService`/`StoryBarViewModel` demo paths, `AppContainer`, `SettingsView`); requirement in `docs/PLAN.md` § App Review Preview Access
- Reveal: tap the Settings About heading four times, then Demo Data → **Preview content** toggle (the dev toggle doesn't turn it on; turning the dev toggle off turns it off). The toggle reloads the feed, stories and Chats list. Also launch arg `-demoData` / env `DEMO_DATA=1`. Activation persists across launches
- `DemoData` (`StupidSocialCore/Sources/DemoData.swift`) builds feed notifications across networks plus story-bar content with `i.pravatar.cc` / `picsum.photos` placeholders (initials offline). The feed's people are the demo chats' cast with the same portraits (`DemoChats.maya` etc.), on handles that fit each network, and the viewer is a fictional `sam`: the demo shows up in App Store screenshots, so it avoids real handles, real products and posts or messages about the app itself. Feed, stories and chats tell one everyday story (a coastal weekend away, a bouldering gym, a side project) in the lowercase, short-message way people actually write, and every photo is a `picsum.photos/id/<n>` picked by eye to match its caption (`DemoData.photo`), since seeded picsum URLs return arbitrary images. Demo items are in-memory and never replace the real cache or credentials; refreshes short-circuit while `DemoData.isDemoMode`; detail/profile hydration uses fixtures; story/notification mutations (likes, seen) are skipped and demo seen state isn't pushed to iCloud. Not simulated: search, publishing, Spotify audio, external links. App Review notes must describe this coverage accurately
- Demo chats (`DemoChats.swift`): in demo mode the Chats list loads only `DemoChatListProvider` (rows on Apron, Telegram, X, Instagram, Spotify and Bluesky, with unread, muted and presence states) and each row opens `.demo(chatId:)` through `DemoChatAdapter`, which supports reactions and the Apron demo server's custom emoji images (`media.apron.chat`). Sends and reactions change only memory. The rows are fake, but other networks' live connections still run, so a Telegram update can briefly add a real row until the next load
- Turn demo mode on in the simulator with the `-demoData` launch argument (`xcrun simctl launch <udid> tech.stupid.StupidSocial -demoData`). `simctl spawn … defaults write` writes outside the app's sandbox container, so the app never sees it
- Name redaction (`DebugRedaction`, and in chats `ChatRedaction`, which `DebugRedaction` leaves to) is driven by `@AppStorage("redactionEnabled")` (`ChatRedaction.defaultsKey`) (Settings "Redact names", default off), separate from `devModeEnabled`, which only gates debug UI

## Feed & notifications

### Refresh policy
- Automatic refresh is foreground activation only (`scenePhase == .active` → `FeedViewModel.refreshOnForegroundActivation()` → `FeedService.foregroundActivationRefresh()`), plus pull-to-refresh. No BGTaskScheduler (iOS delivery wasn't deterministic) and no periodic polling (a 15 s loop was tried and removed). Foreground activation passes `.background` to sources and the story bar; Instagram reuses what it fetched in the last 5 minutes for those (§ Instagram, Stories: reading)
- `ContentView` calls `refreshOnForegroundActivation()` right after creating the container, because the `.active` transition can be missed before dependencies exist. Returning from Settings also triggers it
- Foreground refresh full-fetches X notifications (not count-only), accepting X's possible server-side read side effect because fresh content is preferred
- Successful notification loads clear provider-side unread state for X and Bluesky. Instagram's notifications route already issues `POST /api/v1/news/inbox/`; Instagram DM read endpoint intentionally not used (product decision). GitHub excluded; Farcaster unsupported because Hypersnap seen writes return 501 (observed 2026-09-03)
- `manualRefresh` / `foregroundActivationRefresh` fetch all `NotificationFetching` sources concurrently (task group of `@MainActor` closures); errors are per source; refresh time tracks the slowest source
- Feed and story refreshes run inside `BackgroundActivity.run` (`ProcessInfo.performExpiringActivity`), so a refresh started as the app opens keeps running if the user leaves right away. Before, iOS froze it mid-request and the requests failed with `URLError.timedOut` when the app came back: foreground refreshes that "took" 6 to 20 minutes and timed out on every network at once (observed 2026-10-29). When the background time runs out the refresh is cancelled; a cancelled source is skipped like an unconfigured one, isn't reported as `source_error`/`story_source_error`, and doesn't fail a manual refresh. `refresh` durations use `ContinuousClock`, so they still include any time the app spent suspended
- `FeedService` reads `FeedAccountSources` (one per account, plus the Debug source) on every refresh. Items are rewritten with `NotificationItem.scoped(to:)`: `accountId` becomes the app's account id and `id` gets an `<accountId>|` prefix, since two accounts on one network can see the same event and `CachedNotification.id` is unique. Sources still build items with the network's own ids (Farcaster target details read the fid from the account, not `item.accountId`)
- Cache writes (`replaceAccount` per account + `deleteExpired`) replace only accounts that refreshed successfully and returned items; failed or transiently empty accounts keep cached rows. Each write also drops items of accounts that no longer exist (`deleteAccounts(notIn:)`), which also clears items cached before accounts were scoped
- Target details, profiles, follows and post notifications go to the account the item or profile came from (`NetworkProfile.accountId`, set by `FeedService`); a profile with no account (opened from a story) uses the network's first account. Search uses each network's first account, so people appear once
- `manualRefresh` skips `SourceError.notConfigured` and `.endpointSpikeRequired` without counting them as errors. With genuine failures and no incoming items it returns the cache if non-empty, else throws ("Refresh Issue" alert)
- Story loading runs concurrently with the feed refresh (launch, foreground, pull-to-refresh), not gated on feed success
- Feed parsing must not run on the main actor: sources/clients are `@MainActor`, so parsing after a network await blocked scrolling ~1 s (GitHub For You ~364 ms twice, Instagram tray ~359 ms). `GitHubActivityParser.parseConcurrently` and `instagramHomepageTrayConcurrently` are `@concurrent` wrappers
- `.refreshable` must stay attached permanently in `FeedView`: a conditional modifier removed it when `isRefreshing` flipped, cancelling the task (`NSURLErrorDomain -999`). Overlap is guarded in `FeedViewModel.refresh()`
- Global Cmd+R: hidden `Button` with `.keyboardShortcut("r", modifiers: .command)` in `ContentView` calls `refreshFeedAndStories(container:)` (hardware keyboards only). Toolbar `ProgressView` shows for `isRefreshing || isForegroundRefreshing`, since the native spinner appears only during the pull gesture
- Chats keyboard (hardware keyboards: a Mac, an iPad): Cmd+K and Cmd+F focus the Chats search field (hidden buttons setting `searchable(isPresented:)` in `ChatsView`). In a chat, Escape ends a message search, else goes back to Chats (a hidden `.escape` button in `ChatView` calling `dismiss()`). The composer would swallow Escape, so `ChatComposerTextView` has its own Escape key command (`wantsPriorityOverSystemBehavior`, left out while an input method is composing) that goes back through `ChatUIKitView.goBack`. Shortcuts on the Chats list don't reach a pushed chat, whose hosting controller isn't in the list's responder chain, so each screen has its own. Presented screens (image viewer, the long-press menu, pickers) don't receive the chat's Escape. Up in an empty composer edits the user's newest message when it can be edited (`ChatViewModel.lastEditableMessage`, `nil` while a later window isn't loaded); the key command exists only while the composer is empty, so Up still moves the caret in text

### Feed scope
- Apron rooms and Spotify Messages chats appear only on the Chats screen: `ApronNotificationSource` and `SpotifyMessagesSource` aren't `NotificationFetching`; they build `ChatSummary`s (`ApronNotificationSource` still validates the Apron account). `FeedService` deletes cached `.apron`/`.spotify` items on start, or they'd linger for the 24-hour retention. Instagram DMs stay in the feed until Instagram has a chat screen
- `NotificationItem.unreadCount` (`CachedNotification` default `nil`) drives a blue count capsule and blue timestamp on a row

### Notification detail
- Target hydration: `fetchTargetDetails(for:)` on the source / `FeedService`, cached by network/account/target id (`cachedTargetDetails`) so revisiting doesn't reload; unsupported sources fall back to cached metadata
- `NotificationTarget` carries optional display-safe metadata: multiple image URLs, author, posted timestamp, cached like count; decoding stays compatible with old rows that only stored `imageURL`
- Detail shows network, usernames and content in a `Post` section (avatar/name header, relative time, full text, full-width images or horizontal carousel, total likes, external link); hydration shows only a compact loading indicator. People rows push `ProfileDetailView` and show relative time only when the actor has a timestamp
- When both `parentTarget` and `target` exist, one `Thread` section shows parent, a connector line, then the reply
- X reply parents come from `globalObjects.tweets` when included. Farcaster payloads carry only the parent hash/FID; `PostThreadSections` shows that shell until the thread loads

### UI
- Feed rows: notification-type icon, actor avatar strip, inline network badge, bold usernames without `@` (avoids display-name spoofing), relative time; no unread dots. Follow rows have no preview line. Any notification with an `imageURL` shows a 48×48 thumbnail. Summary uses interpolated `Text` (concatenation deprecated on iOS 26). Grouping text: `@user and N others liked your tweet` / `reacted to your cast` / `followed you`. Reply and mention rows keep the bold actor summary and take the rest from the source's text after the first actor's handle or name (and any " and N others"): "quoted your cast", "replied in your thread", "replied to your tweet"; text that doesn't start with the actor falls back to "replied to you" / "mentioned you"
- Rows use state-driven navigation instead of `NavigationLink` (avoids its reserved disclosure column) and fill the width with a rectangular `contentShape` (plain button hit-tests only drawn content). Same insets as Chats via `compactListRowInsets()`
- Trailing swipe "Open Post" (`allowsFullSwipe`) via `@Environment(\.openURL)`; URLs from `NotificationTargetURL.resolve(network:target:)` (`AppModels.swift`), shared with detail: Farcaster `quilcast.com/c/<hash>`, X `x.com/i/status/<id>`, others use the target `url`; follows/URL-less targets get no action
- `networkBadgeImage(named:)` caches badge images (used to read the PNG from disk on every row body evaluation)
- Feed filter (`AccountFilterBar` in `AccountFilterBar.swift`, shared with Chats search; selection a `Set<AccountFilter>`): a horizontally scrolling row of pills above the notifications (`FilterPillBar`/`FilterPill`, shared with Chats and Search: led by a glass circle that stays put at the leading edge while the pills scroll under it (the scroll view masked from the circle's centre, so pills scrolled back don't show again beside it): the filter icon (an inert button of the clear button's style, so the two match), replaced by the clear button while anything is selected; the pills glass (tinted with the accent colour while selected), all on an opaque `systemBackground` backing on iOS 26, drawn with `StillGlassButtonStyle` (`.glassEffect` without `.interactive()`, dimming while pressed): the `.glass` button styles are interactive glass, which stretched and followed a drag across the pills, since over Chats' rows glass alone showed their text through the labels (observed 2026-11-22, iOS 27.0); bordered before), shown when there are at least two pills. A Reminders pill (shown while any chat reminder is in the feed, or while it's selected) comes first, then one pill per account that has feed items (network badge + `SettingsViewModel.name(for:)`, redacted like Settings), ordered in the feed by its newest item, most recent first (`AccountFilterBar.accountsByRecency`, by `timestamp`, so a chat reminder counts at its due date, as the feed sorts it), and in Chats search by how many chats it has; ties keep the account order. A selected account keeps its pill when its items run out, so it can be turned off. Unselected pills' labels use the secondary style, so a row of names doesn't compete with the list under it. Pills multi-select and the feed shows items matching any selected pill: an account pill by `NotificationItem.accountId` (so reminders also show under their chat's account), the Reminders pill by the reminder item id prefix. The clear button deselects all. The pills start after the circle's measured width (leading `contentMargins`). The selection is view state only, not persisted, and ignores accounts that were removed. Stories aren't filtered
- Empty state: `ContentUnavailableView` with a centered borderless **Open Settings** button calling an `onOpenSettings` closure that appends Settings to the root path

### Debug source
- `DebugAccountMetadata(serverURL:)` → `DebugNotificationSource`/`DebugNotificationsClient` fetching `GET <debug-server>/notifications`
- `scripts/debug-notifications-server.js` (Bun) binds `0.0.0.0`, appends 0–5 random notifications per request, logs to `logs/debug-notifications-server.log`, persists state to `logs/debug-notifications-state.json` so IDs/timestamps aren't reissued after restarts
- `Info.plist` has `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` and `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` so device builds reach a LAN debug server

## Profiles, search & settings

### Search
- Recent: profiles opened from search (`ProfileSearchViewModel.recordVisit`, on the profile screen appearing) are listed under Recent while the field is empty, newest first, without duplicates (network and id), the newest 20, with swipe to remove and Clear. Kept on the device only, in UserDefaults `recentProfileSearches` (`RecentProfile`: id, network, handle, name, avatar, verified and the account it was found through, which opens it through the same account).
- Profile search uses search endpoints only (no fabricated fallback profiles): Farcaster Hypersnap `GET /v2/farcaster/user/search?q=...&limit=10`, Instagram the web client's search box query `PolarisSearchBoxRefetchableQuery` (root `xdt_api__v1__fbsearch__topsearch_connection`, variables `{data: {context: "blended", include_reel: "true", query, rank_token: "", search_session_id, search_surface: "web_top_search"}, hasQuery: true}`, no providers, doc id from the homepage's bundles; `/web/search/topsearch/` answers `login_required` while the session is fine, observed 2026-11-21, probe `search-people`), Spotify Pathfinder `findTopResults` (`sha256Hash=755858df4daab8d212980b02a81dcf8c9a58447de318b59d07c4651a1d0450b9`) keeping only users (observed 2026-07-14)
- Native `searchable` (shared via `SearchBar.swift`; iOS 26 Liquid Glass field at the bottom), debounced `.onChange(of: viewModel.query)`; repeated identical queries aren't suppressed (allows retry); autocapitalization/autocorrection off. Network filter: while the field is focused, `NetworkFilterBar` (`AccountFilterBar.swift`, the feed's and Chats' pills) is the header of an empty first section, one pill per network in `FeedService.profileSearchNetworks` (X, Instagram, Farcaster, Spotify, Bluesky; the others' sources return no profiles) with a connected account (`searchableNetworks()`, read on appear), shown when there are at least two. The selection (`ProfileSearchViewModel.selectedNetworks`, empty = every network) limits the requests: `searchProfiles(query:networks:)` asks only the selected networks. Narrowing filters the current results (`filteredResults`) without a request; adding a network the current results weren't searched with searches again. It also filters recents, and ending the search clears it, since the pills are gone then. It follows the field's focus a run loop later, animated (`showsFilter`): a top `safeAreaBar`, even at zero height, made the rows jump instead of sliding with the field as it took focus, and a removal in the same run loop as the field's dismissal wasn't animated. A header rather than a row, since the inset-grouped card clipped the row at its edge; padded out by the header's 16 pt inset so the pills line up with the field and scroll to the screen's edges. A header shows at once, without the insertion's fade, over the rows as they move down, so it fades itself in 150 ms after appearing (`FadesInOnAppear`; started in `onAppear` itself the animation was dropped). `contentMargins(.top, 0)` takes out the space the list otherwise left above it (observed 2026-11-22, iOS 27.0). The story composer's mention search asks Instagram alone. Rejected: the filter in a top-right `Menu` (replaced for the pills), in `.bottomBar` with `DefaultToolbarItem(kind: .search, …)` (draws inside the search capsule), a bottom `safeAreaInset`, a home-screen search affordance, a home `searchable` that pushes `.search`
- Empty state is a fixed-height row (`.frame(minHeight: 320)`): `GeometryReader` centering animated jankily on iOS 26 and `.containerRelativeFrame(.vertical)` crashed in `UICollectionView` layout invalidation
- Result rows show the network badge beside the username

### Profile detail
- `ProfileDetailView` is the one profile screen for search, notification people rows, story mentions and one-to-one X, Instagram and Bluesky chats, laid out like the chat profiles (Telegram, Apron, Discord): a 96-point `ChatAvatarImage` that opens full screen, name with verified badge, @username, "You follow each other", then one `DetailActionRow` of `DetailActionButton`s (Follow/Following, X's Notify bell, and the chat's Mute and Archive when opened from one), then bio, counts, and a section with location, join date, website and "View on Network". It has no navigation title, like the other details screens.
- From a chat: an adapter that knows the other person conforms to `ChatProfileLinking` (`profileActor: NotificationActor?`, nil for groups and until known; X, Instagram, Bluesky), kept in its stored state. `InfoChatView`'s title opens `ProfileDetailView` with the chat when the chat names someone (`ChatProfileProviding`, which `AdapterChatViewModel` conforms to), else `ChatInfoView`. A chat opened from the store skips its newest page, so `XChatAdapter.startFollowing` also reads the person from the inbox the Chats list loaded. `FeedService` reaches chats through the `feedService` environment value, set on the Chats `NavigationStack` (set on `ChatsView`, pushed chats didn't get it).
- Posts on a profile: `ProfileTimelineLoading.fetchProfileTimeline(userId:kind:cursor:)` (`FeedAccountSources.timelines`, `FeedService.profileTimelines`), X only. `ProfileTimelineSection` shows a Posts | Media segmented picker, and a Sort By menu (Latest, Most Liked) that both tabs share: `ProfileTimelineKind` is `.posts`, `.mostLiked`, `.media` or `.mediaMostLiked`, each its own timeline; Posts is `PostCardView`s with a "Pinned" or "<name> reposted" line (`PostCardView.context`), Media a three-column grid of each post's first photo, video frame or link card image. `ProfileTimelineViewModel` owns the loads (each in a task it starts, so no row's lifecycle cancels one): a kind's first page when it is first shown, the next page when the profile's `List` scrolls within two screens of its end (`onScrollGeometryChange`, again each time a page grows the list while it is still that close), nothing while a page loads, nothing after an error until Retry, and no more pages once one adds no new posts. Measured with request logging (2026-11-17): opening a profile costs one `UserByScreenName`; showing Posts one `UserTweets`; Media one `UserMedia` plus pages until the grid fills two screens; more only while scrolling, and nothing while idle. Two designs that went wrong: a `.task` on the section (which runs per row) plus restarting cancelled loads made ~60 `UserMedia` requests a second, each cancelled by the next re-render and started again (1,012 in ~15 s, 368 of the 500-per-15-minutes allowance in one burst); before the restart, the cancelled load showed "cancelled" and stopped. And paging from the footer row's `.task` loaded a second page on every visit, because `List` prepares rows below the screen. Tapping a post pushes `ProfilePostView` (`PostCardView.open`), its thread through `PostThreadSections`. Tapping a Media tile opens `MediaViewerController` (`MediaViewer.swift`, see § Media viewer below), a UIKit full-screen viewer presented over the app, with the post's text and `PostActionBar` in a hosted SwiftUI caption (`MediaViewerCaptionView`; `MediaViewerCaptionModel` keeps each post's engagement as it changes, so it holds across the post's pages) like the kit's `ChatImageViewerController`: a `UIPageViewController` with one page per photo or video of every post in the grid (`ProfileTimelineItem.media`, or the tile's card image when a post has none), so swiping sideways goes through a post's media and on to the next post. Each page is a zooming `UIScrollView` sized to the media's aspect-fitted rect (pinch, double-tap; a zoomed page pans instead of paging or closing); tapping toggles the post's text, the "n of m" counter and the buttons (and the status bar); a vertical swipe closes it. Videos and GIFs loop on an `AVQueuePlayer` (GIFs muted) and play only while their page is shown, with a pause/play button. The viewer keeps controllers for the pages beside the current one (one behind, two ahead) with their views loaded, so their photos download and their videos load and `preroll(atRate:)` while paused; the pager gets those same controllers, and ones further away are let go so only a few players hold video. Photos four pages past those are preloaded into `ImageCache`. UIPageViewController itself only asks for the next page when a swipe starts, which is enough on a fast network (on the simulator a video was ready about 16 ms after its page appeared either way) but not on a phone. The viewer observes `ProfileTimelineViewModel.$timelines`, asks for the grid's next page within four pages of the end, and resets the pager's cached neighbors when a page arrives while it is on the last one. Photos load at `name=large` (`XUserTimelineParser.largeImageURL`, up to 2048 px) with the grid's medium thumbnail shown meanwhile; videos take the best MP4 up to 2.5 Mbps (`XUserTimelineParser.maxBitrate`), since they stream, where chats' 1 Mbps cap is for whole-file loads. Post cards show the author's display name, X's verified badge (`NotificationActor.isVerified`, from `is_blue_verified` or `verification.verified`; nil where unknown) and the handle, as X does; `DebugRedaction.displayName` picks the name (feed rows keep `actorName`, the handle). `PostCardView` reloads engagement on becoming active only after its repost opened X's page, so a long profile feed doesn't refetch every post. Instagram keeps its own post grid (`NetworkProfile.posts`), loaded by `PolarisProfilePostsQuery` pages as it scrolls, with a Sort By menu: Latest, or Most Liked, which orders the posts loaded so far by `like_count` (nil when `like_and_view_counts_disabled`, sorted last) since Instagram has no such order of its own, with a footer saying how many are included. Tapping a tile opens the media viewer over every loaded post in the grid's order, loading the next page near the end, with the post's likes and a share button. Like counts on profile posts were checked in the app, not a probe: the probe has no profile posts command, and the query's variables come from the live page's config. `NetworkProfile` has `bio`, `postsCount`, `joinedAt`, `websiteURL`, `isVerified`, `isMutualFollow`, optional `location`; GitHub shows "Repositories" instead of "Posts". `FeedService.fetchProfile` takes optional `username` (X lookup is by screen name)
- Search results are partial: detail renders immediately and lazily hydrates; count rows are hidden while hydrating
- Follow/Unfollow for X and Instagram, plus an X-only bell (post notifications) independent of following (user decision: bell doesn't imply follow). `NetworkProfile.isFollowing`/`isNotifiedForPosts`; `RelationshipMutating` backs `FeedService.setFollowing`/`setPostNotifications`, wired for `.x`/`.instagram` in `AppContainer`. Buttons render only once state is known; `ProfileDetailView.shouldHydrateProfile` forces hydration for X when either is nil and Instagram when `isFollowing` is nil. Optimistic, reverts on failure

### Settings & connections
- Top-right toolbar: Search (`NavigationLink(value: .search)`) left of the Settings gear. `SettingsToolbarIcon` observes `SettingsViewModel` directly (a plain `Bool` went stale); red dot when any credential-backed connection is invalid, attached to the gear image and offset inward because the iOS 26 glass toolbar clipped it. Connection rows show a red dot too
- `ContentView` reloads `SettingsViewModel` statuses after foreground, shortcut and pull-to-refresh refreshes (`FeedView` gets an `onRefresh` closure), so a refresh-invalidated account badges without relaunch
- Settings → Accounts lists every account (network name, an 18 pt avatar beside its `@handle` or the status alone, red dot when invalid) and pushes `AccountDetailView`; "Add Account" opens a sheet listing networks with their icons, alphabetically by display name (`SocialNetwork.addable`) and filtered by the shared `searchBar` field in the navigation bar drawer, each pushing the network's view in add mode, and the sheet closes once the account is added. X, Instagram, Spotify and GitHub build on `LoginConnectionForm`, supplying their settings sections, dev-mode manual fields and login sheet. Each `<Network>ConnectionView` takes `accountId`: `nil` adds, otherwise it shows that account's preferences, a "Reconnect <Network>" login at the top only while its credentials are invalid (re-adding the same identity updates it; a different identity is added as a new account), and Remove Account (centered alert; removes the account, its Keychain credentials and cached items on every device). Telegram's detail signs in or out on this device only; Apron's detail is `ApronServerDetailView`, add is `ApronAddServerView`
- Dev mode (4 taps on Settings → About header) reveals manual cookie-header paste in the add views (WebView login is primary), the Debug row and the preview/demo controls
- Settings → Advanced is a collapsed-by-default section (header button with a chevron, as in stupid wallet's network detail) holding Share Usage Data and, when the app has a `ChatControlServer`, Command-Line Access; each row's explanation is a footnote row inside the section, since one section has only one footer
- Per-account notification category toggles (X, Farcaster, Instagram) and Instagram stories/DM-share toggles are `AccountPreferences`, synced with the account
- Foreground `repairAccountIdentities()` fills a migrated X account's user id and missing Instagram username/avatar and Spotify username
- `Account.avatarURL` (synced) is filled after adding and by the foreground repair when missing: X profile by handle (`_normal` suffix dropped for the full image), Instagram homepage viewer, Bluesky profile, Farcaster `pfpUrl`, Spotify profile image, `https://github.com/<user>.png?size=96`, the Apron user's avatar, and for Telegram a `tgfile:` URL from `TelegramClient.ownAvatarURL()` (`users.getUsers([inputUserSelf])`, downloaded through `inputPeerPhotoFileLocation` with `inputPeerSelf`; validated with `scripts/telegram-client.py avatar --peer self`), which loads only where a client signed in to that account can download it. Without a picture the row shows the initial. Apron rows show the server host, without an avatar. Redaction hides avatars too
- No credential-storage UI: the per-connection storage row, the Settings local-only/iCloud footer and `SettingsViewModel.hasLocalOnlyCredentials` were removed (product decision; `docs/PLAN.md` dropped the sync-status requirement). Local-only fallback storage and retry are unchanged
- Telegram rows set `avatarColorKey` to the peer id, so every chat without a photo shows initials on its `ChatUserColor` (for users the same id as their bubbles). Private chats get the row's photo (passed through `telegramChatFactory`) and `avatarUserId` beside the title.
- Chats rows show a small thumbnail of the latest photo, video, GIF or large link preview before the preview text, as Telegram does (`ChatSummary.previewThumbnailURL`, hidden by "Redact names"). Telegram rows set it from the top message's media at 320 px (`TelegramMediaParser.previewThumbnailURL`), and a captioned photo's preview is the caption alone ("Luke: clean", not "Luke: Photo · clean"). Other networks get it once the chat has been open, from its latest message's embeds (the kit's `ChatEmbed.previewThumbnailURL`).
- "Redact names" redacts every Chats row title (including Telegram groups and the Archived Chats list) as "Redacted", and row avatars show the initial
- Settings → Debug lists delivered notifications (title, subtitle, body, thread, category, whether the extension rewrote it; for Telegram the payload shape — `userInfo`/`aps`/alert/`custom` keys and `loc-key`, never text), the extension's `push-extension-log.json`, the saved Telegram avatar count, and a button posting a local notification from a saved-avatar user through the extension's code. Exists because iPhone Mirroring can't open Notification Center

## Stories

### Data & loading
- `StoryBarItem` enum (`AppModels`) wraps `.instagram(InstagramStoryReel)` / `.spotify(SpotifyActivityItem)` (and GitHub, `StoryBarItem.isGitHub`) with unified `id`, `timestamp`, `isSeen`, `userAvatarURL`, `userName`, `network`; provider-specific rendering lives in bubble views and `StoryBarItem` extensions
- Story bar state (`storyBarItems`, `storyBarLoading`, `storyBarContentLoaded`, `ownInstagramStoryReel`, `loadNextStoryBarPage()`) lives in `StoryBarViewModel`; `fetchStoryBarContent` shares one in-flight task so duplicate triggers don't fan out
- Initial load fetches Instagram and Spotify together and publishes atomically; the bar (incl. own bubble) is hidden until `storyBarContentLoaded`, with a loading skeleton meanwhile. Refreshes keep the existing bar visible until both sources finish
- Transient Instagram story fetch failure preserves existing Instagram stories: `InstagramNotificationSource.fetchStoryReels()` throws on tray failure rather than returning empty. Stories cleared only when Instagram is not configured
- Ordering: hard split unseen then seen with a compact `Seen` divider; unread Close Friends reels sort before other unread. Instagram's `reels_tray` order is only weakly chronological, so the first 15 Instagram reels are merged with Spotify reverse-chronologically and the rest appended in Instagram's order (observed 2026-05-16). Spotify activity sorted by timestamp descending
- Pagination (Instagram only): `reels_tray` gives the full ordered tray; `/feed/reels_media/` fetched for 15 active, unmuted reels per page; reaching the row end loads the next 15 (observed 2026-05-13)
- Instagram tiles: duplicate users (active reel + highlight) deduped by user PK
- Several accounts: the bar merges every Instagram account's tray (each person once, from the first account whose tray lists them), every Spotify account's friend activity (deduped by user URI) and every GitHub account's activity (deduped by actor). Marking a reel seen and liking a slide go through the account whose tray listed it (`instagramSourceByOwner`). The own-story bubble and the composer use the first Instagram account; there is no account picker for posting yet
- Seen sync: `StoryBarViewModel` pushes seen state to iCloud after `markSpotifyActivityAsSeen`/`markGitHubActivityAsSeen`, observes `StorySeenSync.didChangeNotification`, re-derives via `refreshSeenState()` (`spotifySortedUnreadFirst`/`githubSortedUnreadFirst`)

### Viewer (`UnifiedStoryViewer`)
- Single viewer for all providers, presented via one identifiable `StoryViewerSelection` holding a snapshot captured at tap time; marks seen by stable id (reel id / `userURI`). Separate bool/index/snapshot state caused blank modals, and live bar re-sorts changed the shown item
- Not `TabView(.page)`: it swallowed the dismiss drag and could show untappable empty states when the selected index was nil during `fullScreenCover` lifecycle
- Seen-marking delayed until the full-screen presentation animation settles
- Tapping unread opens only unread items; tapping seen opens the full seen set starting at the tapped item. Row's newest-first user order preserved
- Start slide (`StoryBarViewModel.storyViewerStartSlideIndex`): Instagram and GitHub resume at the oldest un-viewed slide via shared `resumeIndex(in:after:)` (Instagram uses the tray's server `seen` timestamp, slides oldest-first; GitHub uses `GitHubActivitySeenStore.seenTimestamp(actorId:)`); fully seen → oldest slide / 0. Spotify stays index 0
- `lastSlideIndexByItemID` (in-memory, keyed by `StoryBarItem.id`) records the slide when leaving a user; tap-back/swipe-back resume it. Unvisited user: tap-back → last slide, swipe-back → slide 0. `onChange(of: currentItemIndex)` must not reset `currentSlideIndex` (it overrode `goBack()`'s jump to the previous user's last slide); every navigation path sets the slide index explicitly
- One unified drag gesture: horizontal swipe jumps users, short left/right tap moves slides, long-press pauses without skipping on release, swipe down dismisses; top bar excluded. Hold-to-pause applies to `isInstagram || isGitHub` (and Spotify audio); GitHub only pauses the timer (tick returns early when `isPaused`)
- Keyboard: Left/Right prev/next slide (`goBack()`/`advance()`), Shift+Left/Right prev/next user (`goToPreviousItem()`/`goToNextItem()`), Space `togglePause()` (also pauses Instagram player); hidden `.keyboardShortcut` buttons in a zero-size background
- Segmented progress: N segments for Instagram reels, 1 for Spotify. Instagram image-slide progress starts only after `CachedAsyncImage` loads or fails (loading uses the Spotify-preview-loading inactive capsule style) so slow images aren't skipped
- Audio: mute toggle (top bar, persisted in `AppStorage`) applies to the active `AVPlayer` (Instagram video, Spotify preview), icon swaps without animation. Muted → `.ambient` + `.mixWithOthers`; unmuted → `.playback` (required, else no audio on devices); closing deactivates with notify-others
- Spotify slides: auto-play the 30 s preview via `AVPlayer`; progress uses real audio duration (`AVAsset.load(.duration)`, 5 s fallback); `Open in Spotify` link instead of play/pause; circular art
- Preloading: adjacent Spotify preview audio (`AVURLAsset` with duration loaded, then `AVPlayerItem` from the warm asset) and adjacent Instagram images. `prepareForCurrentItem()` calls `preloadAdjacentGitHubStories()`: prev/next slide plus first/last slides of adjacent groups (`activity.targetAvatarURL`; forks also the owner avatar `https://github.com/<owner>.png?size=192` from `GitHubOriginalCache`); forks resolving mid-slide trigger the owner-avatar download via `store(_:forFork:)`
- Instagram like/unlike is optimistic: immediate heart + haptic, duplicate taps ignored in flight, revert + error alert on failure
- Wrapped in a `NavigationStack` with `FeedService` so Instagram @mention pills with a user id open `ProfileDetailView`; mentions without id are plain pills
- Top bar: username and timestamp one line, tail-truncated; timestamps use `Date.compactRelativeTime` (e.g. `3m`)

### Composer (`StoryComposerView`)
- Opened from the own bubble `+`; Instagram is the target. Top-right vertical toolbar: image picker, text, background (black/white/blue/purple and sunset/ocean/graphite gradients), download (iOS: renders 1080x1920 aspect-fit-on-black + overlays, saves with add-only Photos permission; temporary checkmark on success, alert only on failure)
- Two bottom destinations, "Your Story" and "Close Friends" (green star); the tapped one shows progress, both disabled while posting; hidden while the trash target is visible during drags
- Renders portrait 1080x1920; tries WebP then falls back to JPEG because ImageIO has no WebP encoder on current Apple platforms (`CGImageDestinationCopyTypeIdentifiers` lacks `org.webmproject.webp`); upload metadata must match the actual bytes' type/dimensions
- Posting is optimistic: rendered bytes written to a temp file and inserted as the user's own story; upload runs in background; success refetches the tray and replaces the optimistic slide when the server story appears; failure removes it and shows a feed error. `CachedAsyncImage` supports file URLs and fit/fill mode for this
- iOS text captions use UIKit overlays so pan/pinch move native text views without per-frame SwiftUI layout; SwiftUI notified only at drag end (with delete flag). Trash target lives inside the UIKit canvas; drag-time layout must not reset the text view to its committed offset
- Captions store per-element scale, background, font, color. Pinch scale clamped to 0.1–10× (`minimumScale`/`maximumScale`); hit area at least 140pt around tiny captions. Focused captions get a keyboard accessory toolbar (`BG`, `Font`, `Color`, `Done`), a full-screen dim overlay between non-focused and focused captions, and animate to an upward edit-center without changing saved offset. Composer ignores keyboard safe-area resizing
- Any element (text, background) counts as content (empty state hides, posting enabled); always a tappable background layer to clear focus

### Stories bar UI
- First bubble is the user's own Instagram story (profile pic + bottom-right `+`): opens the own reel if active (`ownInstagramStoryReel`, not duplicated in the list), `+` opens the composer; placeholder outline when none; hidden when Instagram isn't connected
- Modes `All Stories` / `Instagram` / `Spotify`: single row when only one source has content, pager only when both do, hidden when neither. Each row scrolls horizontally and launches the viewer scoped to it. In All Stories `+` opens a destination menu (Instagram only for now); in the Instagram row it opens the composer directly
- Pager: masked vertical pager in a fixed-height scroll. Repeats the modes 200 times in a `LazyVStack`, starting in the middle at a multiple of the mode count; page `i` shows mode `i % count`, so wrapping is plain scrolling. When the mode set changes it returns to the middle, keeping the current mode if it still exists. Not sentinel copies: they snapped visibly and fought paging deceleration. The position is set through `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` on appear and when the modes change (the current mode read from the offset), never a `scrollPosition(id:)` binding: the binding is written as each row passes the top mid-drag, and the re-render re-applied that id, so the pager jumped a full page ahead for a frame or two and then back under the finger, and some drags didn't move it at all (seen frame by frame in a simulator recording with demo data, 2026-11-20)
- Per-frame scroll offset for the dots lives in a small `@Observable` read only by `StoryPagerDots`; in `StoriesBar`'s `@State` it rebuilt every row each frame. Rows filtered and sorted once per body evaluation
- Instagram tiles: gradient ring (purple→pink→orange) when unread, gray when read. GitHub pills show only the actor avatar, no network badge
- Spotify tiles spin album art with gray pulse rings in front of the art: tempo → timing, tempo confidence → opacity, loudness → pulse scale; Reduce Motion disables rotation/pulse
- Loading skeleton: pulsing avatar/label shapes (timeline-driven 2.4s opacity wave, adaptive fills, static under Reduce Motion). Pager/skeleton heights scale with Dynamic Type

## X

### Auth & session
- Credentials: selected `auth_token` and `ct0` cookies only.
- `XLoginWebView` uses the default persistent website data store and the platform-default (Safari/WKWebView) user agent: a non-persistent store / Chrome UA triggered `We’ve temporarily limited your login` from `begin_login` (observed 2026-07-15). A cookie-store observer completes as soon as both cookies exist (post-2FA).
- GitHub and X log in through `CookieLoginWebView`: the persistent store, the previous session's cookies deleted first (so another account can log in, device cookies kept), and a check after each page load and cookie change until the network's extractor returns credentials. Instagram (non-persistent store, seeded cookies, checkpoint handling) and Spotify (blocks the player, failure and retry UI) keep their own web views; all four use `LoginSheet` for the title and Cancel
- WebView cookie extraction (X and Instagram) folds `WKHTTPCookieStore.getAllCookies()` into a dictionary with later duplicates winning; `Dictionary(uniqueKeysWithValues:)` trapped on same-name cookies from different domains/paths.
- Handle resolution: `XClient.verifiedUser()` → `GET /i/api/1.1/account/multi/list.json` (`{"users":[{"screen_name","user_id",…}]}`); `account/verify_credentials.json` returns 404 via `i/api` (observed 2026-05-01).

### Notifications
- Every fetch first asks for the unread counts, as the web client does on load: `GET /i/api/2/notifications/all/unread_count.json?include_tweet_replies=true` (`{"id", "unreadCount"}`, camelCase) and `GET /i/api/2/badge_count/badge_count.json?supports_ntab_urt=1` (`ntab_unread_count`, `dm_unread_count`). Without them `NotificationsTimeline` (every query id, and the legacy REST `notifications/all.json`) left out a 27-minute-old notification that both counts reported; right after the two calls every timeline returned it (observed 2026-11-15). Which of the two does it isn't known; the probe's `notifications --no-prime` skips them to narrow it down on the next new notification. A failed count fails the refresh rather than showing a stale feed.
- Feed: GraphQL `GET /i/api/graphql/8kmedJD_u653xqEjZIS7yA/NotificationsTimeline?…` with `notificationsTimelineFeatures()`, decoded as `XNotificationsTimelineResponse`. Not legacy `notifications/all.json`, because its `fromUsers` never reorders on new likes (observed 2026-09-02).
- `XNotificationsTimelineParser` takes aggregate actors from `template.from_users` in server order (newest first, no reversal) and mentions/replies/quotes from `TimelineTweet`. Reply/mention/quote summaries use `actor.username`, matching like/follow rows.
- Side effect: `NotificationsTimeline` doesn't mark read, so `XClient.notifications()` after a successful parse calls `POST /i/api/2/notifications/all/last_seen_cursor.json?cursor=<top-cursor>` with the opaque `TimelineTimelineCursor` of `cursorType` `Top`; a numeric `sortIndex` is rejected with code 358 (observed 2026-09-03). Probe: `x-web-client.py load-notifications`.
- `XNotificationCategory.follows` maps from `NotificationType.follow`; accounts from before it get it enabled once, by `AccountMigration` (it replaced the `account.x.followsMigrated` flag).
- Post notifications (`device_follow_tweet_notification_entry`, `user_tweeted`, `user_tweeted_entry`, `tweet_notification`, `user_posted`) normalize to `.post`; entries with empty `targetTweets` render from `fromUsers` and the `sortIndex` timestamp. Source filters by enabled categories.
- `.post` detail fetches `GET /i/api/2/notifications/device_follow.json` (legacy shape: `globalObjects` `users`/`tweets` + `timeline.instructions[].addEntries.entries`, parsed by `XNotificationParser`) and shows every returned tweet as a card, since grouped entries can omit `targetTweets`.
- Legacy decoding: snake-case keys, numeric `id` with `id_str` fallback, `created_at` `EEE MMM dd HH:mm:ss Z yyyy`.
- Targets carry author, creation time, all media URLs from `extended_entities.media` (`XExtendedEntities`/`XMediaEntity`, `media_url_https`) and `favorite_count`. Tweet detail is `TweetResultByRestId` (below); `GET /i/api/1.1/statuses/show.json` returns 404 for live posts (observed 2026-10-31).

### Posts & engagement
- Source of request shapes: X's web client, not third-party clients. Its `main.<hash>.js` (linked from the signed-in `https://x.com/home`; the logged-out page loads a different `x-web` bundle without these operations) declares each operation as `queryId:"…",operationName:"…",operationType:"…",metadata:{featureSwitches:[…],fieldToggles:[…]}`, and the home page's `featureSwitch` config gives each switch's value (a switch missing from it is sent as `false`). Variables are read from the call sites in the same bundle. Ids in `XClient.TweetQueryIds` and `x-web-client.py` `TWEET_QUERY_IDS` (observed 2026-10-31); when one starts returning an empty 404, re-read the bundle.
- Read: `GET /i/api/graphql/<id>/TweetResultByRestId` with `{tweetId, withCommunity: false, includePromotedContent: false, withVoice: false}` and `tweetFeatures`. `data.tweetResult.result` is a `Tweet` or a `TweetWithVisibilityResults` wrapping one in `tweet`; `legacy` has `favorite_count`, `retweet_count`, `reply_count`, `quote_count`, `favorited`, `retweeted`. `XClient.tweetEngagement` maps it to `PostEngagement`; the repost count shown is retweets plus quotes, as X shows it.
- Mutations are `POST /i/api/graphql/<id>/<operation>` with a JSON body `{variables, queryId}` (plus `features` for `CreateTweet`, the same switches minus `creator_subscriptions_tweet_preview_api_enabled`): `FavoriteTweet`/`UnfavoriteTweet` `{tweet_id}`, `DeleteRetweet` `{source_tweet_id}`, `CreateTweet` `{tweet_text, media: {media_entities: [], possibly_sensitive: false}, semantic_annotation_ids: []}` with `reply: {in_reply_to_tweet_id, exclude_reply_user_ids: []}` for a reply or `attachment_url: "https://x.com/i/status/<id>"` for a quote. Like/unlike verified live and restored with `x-web-client.py like|unlike|tweet`; `CreateTweet` reaches X's validation (an empty post returns error 170), but a real reply or quote hasn't been posted from the probe.
- `CreateRetweet` (`{tweet_id}`, the bundle's current id) returns an empty-bodied 404 to this client while the other mutations succeed with the same headers (observed 2026-10-31): X gates it beyond cookies. So `XNotificationSource.setReposted(true)` throws `.unsupported` and `repostPageURL` gives `https://x.com/intent/retweet?tweet_id=<id>`, which the card opens; undoing a repost works natively. The card refetches engagement when the app becomes active again.
- Threads: `GET /i/api/graphql/<id>/TweetDetail` with `{focalTweetId, referrer: "tweet", rankingMode: "Relevance", includePromotedContent: false, withCommunity: true, withQuickPromoteEligibilityTweetFields: false, withBirdwatchNotes: true, withVoice: true}` and `tweetFeatures` plus `rweb_video_screen_enabled` and `responsive_web_enhance_cards_enabled` (both false). `data.threaded_conversation_with_injections_v2.instructions[].entries`: `tweet-<id>` items before the focal post are its ancestors (root first) and `tweet-<focal>` is the post; after it come reply chains (`conversationthread-…` modules) and a `Bottom` cursor, which the app doesn't show. `XThreadParser` maps the ancestors and the post, using `note_tweet` text for long posts and otherwise `full_text` cut to `display_text_range` (Unicode scalars), which drops a reply's leading @mentions and the trailing media link, with `&amp;`/`&lt;`/`&gt;` unescaped; posts carry their engagement, so thread cards need no per-post request. Probe: `x-web-client.py thread <id> [--cursor …]` (prints replies too). Not handled: a top cursor for very long ancestor chains (observed 2026-10-31).
- `PostThreadLoading` is the network-neutral ability (`FeedAccountSources.threads`, `FeedService.postThreads`), implemented by X and Farcaster. `PostThreadSections` loads the thread on appear for reply/mention/reaction details (not `.post` unless it replies to something, since that detail is the device-follow list); without a loader the detail shows the target alone. Without `PostEngaging` (Farcaster) `PostCardView` shows the like count in place of the action bar. The ancestors are one row and the focal post its own row, with no inset and no separator between them so the line crosses the boundary; the focal row's `listRowBackground` is tinted when there are ancestors. `listRowInsets(_:_:)` for chosen edges is iOS 26+, so earlier versions get full `EdgeInsets` with a 20-point side inset. `PostCardView` draws the line as an overlay over the card's full height, since a flexible rectangle in the avatar column only takes its ideal height: `continuesThread` from the avatar's bottom edge to the card's bottom, `followsThread` from the card's top to the avatar, across the card's 6-point top padding, so the line touches every avatar with no gap.
- `PostEngaging` (in `NotificationSource.swift`) is the network-neutral ability; `FeedAccountSources.engagement` carries it and `FeedService.postEngagement(network:accountId:)` returns it (nil in demo mode). `NotificationDetailView` shows `PostCardView` for a target exactly when the account has one, and the old `TargetPostView` otherwise; the reply-thread section stays as it was. `PostActionBar` is the counts and actions under a post (in `PostCardView` and the profile media viewer), offering Bookmark exactly when the source conforms to `PostBookmarking` (X). `PostEngagement.bookmarkCount`/`isBookmarked` come from `legacy.bookmark_count`/`bookmarked`, present in `TweetResultByRestId`, `TweetDetail` and the profile timelines. X: `DeleteBookmark` (`Wlmlj2-xzyS1GN3a6cj-mQ`, `{tweet_id}`) works; `CreateBookmark` (`aoDbu3RHznuiSkQ9aNM67Q`, the same variables, no feature switches) answers the empty 404 that `CreateRetweet` does, with or without the web client's `x-twitter-*` headers and a browser user agent. The web client sends `x-client-transaction-id` on these requests (behind `rweb_client_transaction_id_enabled`), which this client doesn't generate; that is the likely gate for both. So bookmarking opens the post on X (`bookmarkPageURL`, `https://x.com/i/status/<id>`) and the bar reloads engagement on return, as reposting does. Probe `x-web-client.py bookmark|unbookmark <id>`, `tweet <id>` shows the state (observed 2026-11-19). Events: `post_engage` with `action` like/unlike/repost/unrepost/bookmark/unbookmark/reply/quote.

### Profiles, search & relationships
- Profile timelines: `GET /i/api/graphql/<id>/UserTweets` (`D6z-FJI2pKuwEeYYxVIGbg`) with `{userId, count: 20, includePromotedContent: true, withQuickPromoteEligibilityTweetFields: true, withVoice: true}`, and `UserMedia` (`iTSlWbsmD00dAE7Sk9xvIw`) with `{userId, count: 20, includePromotedContent: false, withClientEventToken: false, withBirdwatchNotes: false, withVoice: true}`; both with the `TweetDetail` features and `fieldToggles {withArticlePlainText: false}`, variables from the bundle's `fetchUserTweets`/`fetchUserMedia`. Response: `data.user.result.timeline.timeline.instructions`: `TimelinePinEntry` (one `entry`, the pinned post), `TimelineAddEntries` with `tweet-<id>` items, `profile-conversation-…` modules (self-threads), `promoted-tweet-…` and `who-to-follow-…` (skipped) and Top/Bottom cursors. Media's first page is one `profile-grid-0` module; later pages add to it with `TimelineAddToModule` `moduleItems`. A repost's `legacy.retweeted_status_result` is the post shown. Some media posts are link or player cards with no `extended_entities`; their image is in `card.legacy.binding_values` (`player_image_large` and similar; some cards have no `legacy`). X returns a Bottom cursor even past the end, so a page without posts ends the timeline. `XUserTimelineParser` maps both (`XUserTimelineParserTests`); probe `x-web-client.py user-tweets|user-media <user id> [--cursor …]` (observed 2026-11-17).
- Most liked: the web profile's Popular sort sends `sortByMostLiked: true`, but only with `UserTweetsAndReplies`, `UserOriginalsTimeline` (posts only; id `ZFKu1DFS63hgrkLzR3Hsdw`, the same switches as `UserTweets`) and `UserHighlightsTweets`, behind its `responsive_web_profile_redesign_enabled` switch. `UserTweets` ignores the variable and stays newest first. The app sends `UserOriginalsTimeline` with `{userId, count: 20, sortByMostLiked: true, includePromotedContent: true, withQuickPromoteEligibilityTweetFields: true, withVoice: true}`: the profile's own posts (no reposts or replies) by likes, highest first, and later pages continue the order (for @jack 349K, 304K, 274K … and page 2 from 66.8K down to 36.6K). Same response shape as `UserTweets`. Probe `x-web-client.py user-originals <user id> --most-liked` (observed 2026-11-17). Media by likes: `UserMedia` ignores `sortByMostLiked` (likes come back in date order), and the web client's sorted filter, `ProfileFilter` (`_-RMD5S0IgQHXFZl3dxhPg`, `fetchUserTweetsWithSortAndFilter`: `{mode: "Likes"|"Latest", bucket: "Posts"|"Replies"|"Quotes"|"OnlyVideo"|"Retweets", reverse}`; `OnlyImage` also passes validation, `Media`/`OnlyPhoto` are 422), answers an empty timeline for every bucket and profile on this account. So `.mediaMostLiked` is `UserOriginalsTimeline` sorted by likes, keeping posts with a photo, video or card image; `XNotificationSource` reads up to four pages per ask until it finds some (about one most-liked post in seven has media for @jack; most of kmacb.eth's do). Probe `x-web-client.py profile-filter <user id> [--mode --bucket]` (observed 2026-11-17).
- Rate limits: each query has its own allowance per account per 15 minutes, in `x-rate-limit-limit` / `-remaining` / `-reset`: `UserTweets` 50, `UserOriginalsTimeline` 250, `UserMedia` 500, `TweetResultByRestId` 500, `TweetDetail` 300, `UserByScreenName` 150 (observed 2026-11-17). Every simulator or device signed in to the same X account shares them. Over the limit X answers 429 "Rate limit exceeded"; `XClient.send` turns that into a "try again in a few minutes" error instead of an invalid response.
- Profile lookup: GraphQL `UserByScreenName` as the web client's `fetchOneUserByScreenName` sends it: query id `AMIBMjtxEEATh4z8V9GtRg`, `{screen_name, withGrokTranslatedBio: true}`, its twelve feature switches and `fieldToggles {withAuxiliaryUserLabels: true, withPayments: false}` (v1.1 `users/show.json` is deprecated). The result has no `legacy`: `screen_name`/`name`/`created_at` in `core`, `following`/`followed_by` in `relationship_perspectives`, the bell in `notifications_settings.notifications_enabled`, counts in `relationship_counts {followers, following}` and `tweet_counts {tweets}`, the bio in `profile_bio.description` with its t.co links in `profile_bio.entities.description.urls` (the app shows the expanded URLs) and the website in `profile_bio.entities.url.urls[0].expanded_url`, `location.location`, `avatar.image_url`, `banner.image_url`, `is_blue_verified`. `XUserProfileParser` maps it (`XUserProfileParserTests`). The older id `IGgvgiOx4QZndDHuD3x9TQ` the app sent until 2026-11-17 answers with `legacy` (the bell as `legacy.notifications`) and no `notifications_settings`, so the bell never showed. Probe: `x-web-client.py user <screen name> [--legacy]` (observed 2026-11-17). SearchTimeline results include neither following nor bell state.
- Search: GraphQL `POST /i/api/graphql/hz_94eVAtrtQo_vO3my7Rw/SearchTimeline` with `product: "People"`. `GET /i/api/1.1/users/search.json` returns code 34 / HTTP 404 (observed 2026-07-15). Needs only auth headers plus a non-empty `User-Agent` (omitting it → 404), so app and probe send `StupidSocial/1`.
- Relationship mutations use legacy REST (no GraphQL id needed), `auth_token`/`ct0` headers + form body of `include_*` fields: follow `POST /i/api/1.1/friendships/create.json`, unfollow `POST /i/api/1.1/friendships/destroy.json`, bell `POST /i/api/1.1/friendships/update.json` with `device=true|false&cursor=-1&id=<user_id>` (`XClient.setFollowing`/`setPostNotifications`) (observed 2026-08-18).

## X Chat

### Protocol & endpoints
- Legacy DMs still answer (`GET /i/api/1.1/dm/inbox_initial_state.json`, `dm/conversation/<id>.json` paged with `max_id`, `dm/user_updates.json?cursor=`, `GET /i/api/2/badge_count/badge_count.json`; probes `dm-inbox`, `dm-conversation`, `dm-updates`, `dm-unread-count` in `scripts/x-web-client.py`), but conversations moved to X Chat, so the newest legacy conversation was months old (observed 2026-10-28).
-
- Juicebox recovery with the PIN (official JS SDK `juicebox-sdk` 0.3.7 under bun; the bundler build needs the wasm instantiated by hand) returns a 64-byte secret whose halves derive the registered identity and signing public keys.
- Unencrypted messages: a create event without a key version (101), with 109 true and an unknown millisecond timestamp in 104, carries the plain `MessageEntryHolder` in 100, no franking (108) and no signature; such a conversation has no key-change events at all (observed 2026-10-29, a conversation with a user without X Chat keys). `XChatClient.decrypt` reads it directly; before, it showed as "Couldn't decrypt this message." and the Chats row as "Encrypted message". `scripts/xchat-client.py key-changes <conversation>` shows a conversation's key sources and message shapes without contents
- Message contents version 0 use the conversation key directly; the per-message HKDF applies only to version 1 (not seen in practice, observed 2026-10-28).
- Create-event signatures cover the unpadded base64 ciphertext, not the plaintext; create and read-marker signatures verify against canonical payloads. Franking tags match except for events carrying media hashes (franking's encrypted media hashes aren't checked).
- Incoming reactions are create events: unread counts match X's `xchat_unread_count` only when counting decrypted messages (observed 2026-10-28).
- Inbox conversation details: `participants_results` holds every member but the viewer with full profiles, in groups too; a group's `group_members_results` (and admins) are bare `rest_id`s, and an unnamed group has no `group_metadata.group_name` (observed 2026-10-29). Reading members from `group_members_results` left groups with no members, so their Chats rows had an empty title.
- Group names: `group_metadata.group_name` is base64 of a version-0 sealed box (nonce 24 ‖ secretbox) under one of the conversation's keys. It doesn't name the key version (`latest_group_title_change_message_event` carries the same ciphertext with its version at `7.4.1.5.6`), so `XChatClient` tries each key, newest first, when loading the inbox (observed 2026-10-29). Probe: `scripts/xchat-client.py group-names` (`--show-names`). Renames arrive as socket events that aren't applied yet; the next inbox load picks them up.
- Encrypted media (observed 2026-10-30, 54 of 59 media items in the user's chats; probe `scripts/xchat-client.py media [<conversation>] --download N`): a message's `MediaAttachment` (attachments field 1) has 1 media hash key, 2 dimensions {1 width, 2 height}, 3 type (1 image, 2 GIF, 3 video, 4 audio, 5 file, 6 SVG), 4 duration ms, 5 size (the encrypted size, not the plaintext's), 6 filename, 7 attachment id, 8/9 legacy URL and preview (migrated DM media only, unencrypted), 10 Grok tag, 11 plaintext SHA-256 (newer uploads). The bytes are at `GET https://ton.x.com/i/ton/data/xchat_media/<conversation id>/<media hash key>` with the X cookies and bearer (401 without them; no content type). They are a libsodium `crypto_secretstream_xchacha20poly1305` stream: 24-byte header, then chunks of 1024 plaintext bytes + 17, the last tagged final (dm-core's `LibsodiumStreamConfig`). For contents version 0 the key is the conversation key of the message's key version; the attachment id doesn't enter it. dm-core derives HKDF-SHA256(message key, salt "xchat-attachment-key", info attachment id) only for contents version 1, which no message uses yet. Decrypted jpg, mp4 and md files matched `file` and field 11's hash.
- `XChatCrypto.SecretStream` opens each chunk with CryptoKit's `ChaChaPoly`: a secretstream chunk is RFC 8439 ChaCha20-Poly1305 over (tag ‖ 63 zero bytes ‖ message) whose first 64 ciphertext bytes are the encrypted tag byte then keystream block 1, so the chunk is rebuilt from that keystream (sealing 64 zero bytes) and opened. That holds only when the message length is a multiple of 16: libsodium pads the ciphertext in the MAC by `length % 16` bytes, RFC 8439 to the next multiple of 16, so a stream's short last chunk is checked with the native Poly1305 (one-time key from a native ChaCha20 block 0) and decrypted with `ChaChaPoly` keystream. HChaCha20 and rekeying use a small native ChaCha20 block. Files decrypt a thousand chunks at a time and a stream without the final tag fails, so truncated downloads never show.
- `XChatMediaURL` (`xchatmedia://media/<conversation>/<hash key>?v=<key version>&ext=<ext>`) names no key; `XChatMediaURLProtocol` (registered in `AccountServices.rebuild` with every `XChatClient`) serves it from `Caches/xchat-media/` or downloads it through the X account that holds the conversation key (others try next), one download per attachment at a time, and hands the file over memory-mapped. Forget Keys clears the cache. `XChatAdapter` maps images to `.image` (viewer), videos to `.video` (no poster: X Chat has no encrypted thumbnail), GIFs to `.video` loops (or `.image` for `.gif` files), and audio, files and SVG to `.file`; media without a hash key or key version stays a card that opens X.
