- Shared posts and links (probe `xchat-client.py links`, shapes only, observed 2026-11-11): attachments field 2 is a post `{1 rest id, 2 URL (x.com/<name>/status/<id> or /i/status/<id>), 3 a 25–36 byte id}`, with no author, text or media; fields 3 and 4 are links `{1 URL, 2/3 image structs, 4 title, 5 UUID}` / `{1 URL}`. `XChatAdapter` maps both to cards that open the post (a post attachment without a URL gets `https://x.com/i/status/<id>`), and the framework draws them as the post (§ X posts in every chat below).
- X posts in every chat: An x.com/twitter.com post link (`XPost.id(in:)`), as a card, link preview or a link in a message's text, draws as the post in every chat once it has loaded, through the first signed-in X account (see § Chats & chat screen › Posts linked in chats). The post is loaded with `TweetResultByRestId` through that account's `XClient` (`XClient.post(id:)`, probe `x-web-client.py post-preview <id>`): author (name, handle, avatar with `_normal.` swapped for `_200x200.`, verified from `is_blue_verified` or `verification.verified`), `created_at`, `display_text_range`-trimmed or note text with t.co links shown as their `display_url` and the trailing `quoted_status_permalink` link removed, the quoted post (`quoted_status_result`, parsed the same way), and `extended_entities.media` (photo URL, `original_info` size, `video_info` MP4 variants; the app plays the highest bitrate up to 1 Mbps, else the lowest, since chat videos load whole files). pbs.twimg.com and video.twimg.com media load without auth. `XPost.chatEmbed` maps a post to `ChatPost` (network `.x` for the corner icon, the quoted post nested, and `counts` from `reply_count`, `retweet_count` and `favorite_count`: X's reposts leave out quotes, which `quote_count` holds and the card doesn't show; counts are stupid-chatkit 0.7.1).
- Edits (probe `xchat-client.py edit-message`, validated in a conversation the user named for testing, observed 2026-10-31): a create event whose entry is `MessageEdit {1 target sequence id, 2 updated text, 3 entities, 4 target's client message_id}` (field 4 of `MessageEntryContents`), sent through `SendMessageCreateMutation` like messages and reactions; X's web client builds it that way (`new LF(sequenceId, text, entities, messageId)` in `assets_dm-core`). Its `canEdit` (`_Nn`) allows the sender's encrypted messages that aren't call cards or forwards without text, with no time limit, which `XChatAdapter.editableText` follows (encrypted = create event has key version 101). The latest edit's text shows, with `isEdited`
- Replies (probe `send-message --reply-to`, validated in a conversation the user named for testing, observed 2026-11-03): `MessageContents` field 4 is a `ReplyingToPreview` {1 sender id (i64), 2 text, 3 entities, 4 attachments, 5 sender display name, 6 sequence id, 7 client message id}; received previews always carry 3 and 4, even empty. Fields 8–14 (raw source event, its key, attachment id) are only sent when the feature switch `xchat_additional_reply_preview_validation_send` is on; it is off (x.com/i/chat and /home `featureSwitch`, observed 2026-11-03), so neither the probe nor the app sends them. Re-check that switch if replies start failing validation. `XChatClient.replyPreview` re-decrypts the quoted event and copies its attachments as stored (Swift's Thrift decoder keeps field types) minus the plaintext hash, as dm-core does. The viewer's own messages are quoted without a name (field 5 is shown to everyone, so "You" can't go there, and the app doesn't know the viewer's display name); the app shows a quote whose sender id (field 1) is the viewer as "You"
- Sending media (probe `send-media`, read from dm-core's `DmMediaUploadHandler`/`DefaultMediaUploadDriver`, validated live 2026-11-03 from the probe and the app, both downloaded back matching their SHA-256): encrypt with the media secretstream under the newest conversation key (contents version 0; `xchat_send_contents_encryption_version` is 0); `InitializeXChatMediaUploadMutation` (`6M543IPabOfZm7-yyIczdw`) `{conversation_id, message_id, total_bytes: <encrypted size string>, is_public: false}` → `{media_hash_key, resume_id, resume_upload_url}`; POST each part raw (X cookies and bearer, `application/octet-stream`) to `https://ton.x.com/i/ton/data/xchat_media/<conversation>/<hash key>?concurrent=true&resumeId=<id>&partNumber=<0-based>`; `FinalizeXChatMediaUploadMutation` (`3O4jPQ2hwW9LbqCc-wFmqA`) `{conversation_id, message_id, media_hash_key, resume_id, num_parts, is_public: false}`. `message_id` is the id the create event then carries. The web client sizes parts by connection quality (1 MiB, 6 MiB or 7.5 MB); the app uses 1 MiB. The `MediaAttachment` has the hash key, dimensions (i64), type, duration for videos, the encrypted size, filename, a fresh lowercase UUID as attachment id (received ones are UUIDs, not the hash key) and the plaintext SHA-256. Franking's tag covers each attachment's plaintext SHA-256 in order, and franking carries no field 3 (received media messages' tags only match with those hashes). A message that is only a file leaves out the text field. The app writes the plaintext into the media cache after uploading, so the sent file shows without downloading it
- `XChatCrypto.encryptMedia`/`SecretStream.push` mirror the pull side: CryptoKit `ChaChaPoly` over (tag ‖ 63 zero bytes ‖ chunk) for keystream and, for 16-byte-aligned chunks, the MAC; the unaligned last chunk's MAC is the native Poly1305 over libsodium's padding. `XChatCryptoTests.testEncryptedMediaOpens` opens pushed streams of edge lengths with the libsodium-checked pull
- `XChatOutgoingMedia` maps the composer's attachment: `image/gif` → GIF, other images (not SVG) → image with pixel size (turned for EXIF rotation), `video/*` → video with size and duration from AVFoundation, everything else (audio included, so X doesn't treat it as a voice message) → file
- Forwards (probe `forward-message`, validated in a conversation the user named for testing, observed 2026-11-07; field numbers from dm-core): `MessageContents` without field 1, field 6 `ForwardedMessage {1 text, 2 entities}`, field 3 the original's attachments downloaded, decrypted and uploaded again (media is encrypted per conversation; the web client caps forwarded media at 100 MB and 5 conversations), field 7 `sent_from` 5 (`MESSAGE_FORWARD_SHEET`). No original sender or conversation is sent; X's reader marks a message forwarded when field 6 has text and field 1 is empty, and the app when field 6 is present
- Voice messages (probe `send-voice`, same conversation, observed 2026-11-07): AAC in MP4 (the web recorder: 32 kbps, 22.05 kHz, max 240 s) named `voice_note_<ms>.m4a`, `MediaAttachment` type 4 (AUDIO) with `duration_millis` and no dimensions; X stores no waveform, so voice messages show a flat one. Types: IMAGE 1, GIF 2, VIDEO 3, AUDIO 4, FILE 5, SVG 6; a picked audio file goes as FILE
- Deleting for everyone (probe `delete-messages`): `DeleteMessageMutation` (`4gsDQKEmYkOtvsSIpHXdQA`) {sequence_ids, conversation_id, delete_message_action `DeleteForAll`/`DeleteForSelf`, action_signatures [{message_id, encoded_message_event_detail: base64 `MessageEventDetail {7: MessageDeleteEvent {1 sequence ids, 2 action (2 all, 1 self)}}`, message_event_signature}]}, signing `"MessageDeleteEvent,<message id>,<sender>,<conversation>,<action>,<sequence ids…>"`; the same event through `SendMessageEventMutation` is `InvalidRequest` (observed 2026-11-07). The app doesn't delete X messages yet
- `GetConversationPageQuery`'s `min_local_sequence_id` is an exclusive upper bound: `0` fails with "range order reversed; begin must be <= end", so the newest page uses `Int64.max` (observed 2026-10-28).

### WebSocket
- `GenerateXChatTokenMutation` mints a token; `wss://chat-ws.x.com/ws?token=` accepts it with `Origin: https://x.com` and no subprotocol. Server sends nothing on open; a 30 s keep-alive `Message{2: MessageInstruction{2: KeepAliveInstruction{}}}` keeps it open with no reply. Events arrive as `Message{1: MessageEvent}` ~0.1 s after a GraphQL send, same sequence id (observed 2026-10-28). Probe: `scripts/xchat-client.py listen` (`--send-to`).
- `XChatLiveFeed` (one socket, foreground only, on `LiveConnection`) pushes events through `XChatClient.ingest` to Chats rows, then to open chats (ingesting first takes in key changes the chats decrypt with); reconnects and pull instructions catch up with the newest page plus a list reload. Unlocking/locking restarts the feed (`XChatUnlockViewModel.refresh` reports a change, so disconnecting X closes it). Replaces 15 s polling.

### Swift implementation
- `XChatThrift` (generic binary-protocol coder), `XChatCrypto` (CryptoKit P-256/AES-GCM/SHA-256; XSalsa20-Poly1305 written natively, checked against PyNaCl vectors at Poly1305 block boundaries; CryptoKit accepts the 16-byte GCM IV), `XChatClient` (actor with the X account's scoped `KeychainCredentialStore`; keys under `xchat.<account id>`), one per X account with its own `XChatLiveFeed` socket, `XChatViewModel`, `XChatUnlockViewModel`, the X account's settings section. `XClient.apiHeaders` is a shared nonisolated helper. X rows join the Chats list; archived X chats go to its Archived section. Covered by `XChatCryptoTests`.
- Juicebox in Swift: `Vendor/JuiceboxSdk` (Swift wrapper + XCFramework of prebuilt FFI static libraries) made by `scripts/vendor-juicebox.sh`. Requires stupid-app ≥ 0.0.22, which links local static-library XCFrameworks.
- SwiftFormat's defaults drop `Sendable` from internal types, which main-actor default isolation then treats as non-Sendable across actors; X Chat value types crossing the actor boundary are therefore public, like Telegram's.
- Decisions: unnamed group chats are named for their members; replies are sent without the optional validation fields, as X's web client does while their switch is off; encrypted media is decrypted and cached on the device; undecryptable messages are shown, not hidden; keys are synchronizable like the X cookies, so one PIN entry covers the user's devices.

### Open issues
- Not done: legacy (pre-X Chat) conversations, message requests, video posters, live group renames, push, more than one attachment per message (X allows 10).

## Farcaster

### Endpoints (Hypersnap)
- Source cloned at ignored `third-party/hypersnap` (`src/api/http.rs::handle_notifications` → `src/network/server.rs::get_notifications`).
- `GET /v2/farcaster/user/by-username` returns `{ "user": {…} }`; leading `@` stripped. `GET /v2/farcaster/user?fid=N` for profiles (`profile.bio.text`, `registered_at`); `FarcasterUserResponse` uses `.convertFromSnakeCase` with synthesized `Decodable` (custom `CodingKeys` broke avatar decoding).
- `/v2/farcaster/user/search` is case-sensitive; the client lowercases queries (observed 2026-07-14).
- Manual refresh does exactly one `GET /v2/farcaster/notifications` and normalizes only that response; no per-notification reply enrichment (`user/replies_and_recasts`/`cast`), so refresh cannot fan out.
- Notification shape: `type`, `user`, ISO-8601 `timestamp`, nullable `cast`; `most_recent_timestamp` aggregate shape also decoded.
- Probe: `scripts/farcaster-client.py` (no credentials; `notifications --fid N [--quotes]`, `cast --hash H`, `thread --hash H`).
- The app sends `include_quotes_and_deep_replies=true`. Without it Hypersnap returns only `likes` (hydrated target casts), `follows` (`cast=null`) and `reply`; with it, also `quote` (a cast embedding one of the user's casts as `embeds[].cast_id`), `recasts` (hydrated target cast, recaster in `user`) and `reply` casts deeper in threads under the user's casts (observed 2026-11-09; `src/api/http.rs::handle_notifications`). Without the flag quotes never reached the app. Opaque base64-JSON cursor pagination.
- `FarcasterNotificationSource.kind(of:)` and `notificationType`: `likes`→`.reaction`, `recasts`→`.reaction` ("recast your cast"), `quote`→`.reply` ("quoted your cast"), as X maps quotes and retweets; `follows`→`.follow`. `reply` covers every cast: `parentAuthor.fid == account FID` → `.reply`; no parent → `.mention`; a deeper reply → `.mention` if it mentions the user, else `.reply` ("replied in your thread"). Feed rows show the source's text for replies and mentions (see Feed rows).
- Threads: `/v2/farcaster/cast/conversation` returns no ancestors (observed 2026-11-09), so `fetchThread` walks `parent_hash` with `GET /v2/farcaster/cast?identifier=<hash>&type=hash`, up to 20 casts (about 0.8 s each). Thread posts carry `reactions.likes_count`, no viewer engagement.
- Cast `text` is mention-stripped with zero-length ranges; `FarcasterCastResponse.displayText` reinserts `@username` at `mentionedProfilesRanges` using `mentionedProfiles`.
- Targets carry cast author, timestamp, embed URLs (`FarcasterEmbed`, `embeds[].url`; first embed is `imageURL`), and `reactions.likes_count` when present.
- Detail likes: `GET /v2/farcaster/reaction/cast?hash=...&types=likes&fid=<cast_author_fid>`, counting returned likes; `fid` is required or it can return zero (observed 2026-05-11). `GET /v2/farcaster/cast?identifier=...&type=hash&fid=...` refreshes author/text/timestamp.

### Grouping
- Category filter applies before grouping and unread counts.
- `mergeGroup`: likes sharing a target cast hash merge into one item, recasts of it into another (their `sourceId` is `recasts:<hash>`) (actors deduped by FID, newest timestamp); all follows in a batch collapse into one item; replies ungrouped. Each actor keeps its own timestamp.

### External client
- Cast/profile links open the configurable `FarcasterExternalClient` (`AppModels.swift`), default Quilcast (web client on the same Hypersnap network): casts `https://quilcast.com/c/0x<hash>` (40-hex hash, `0x` optional), profiles `https://quilcast.com/u/<username>` (username or fid). Used by `NotificationTargetURL` and `ProfileDetailView`.
- Settings shows an "External Client" row once connected (Farcaster, Quilcast, Custom). Custom takes cast/profile URL patterns with `{hash}` (`0x`-prefixed) and `{username}`; any scheme allowed. A pattern missing its placeholder or scheme yields no link, hiding the open action. `UserDefaults` keys `farcasterExternalClient`, `farcasterCustomCastURLPattern`, `farcasterCustomProfileURLPattern`.

### Open issues
- Parent-only replies (no @mention) now arrive: the deployed Hypersnap queries `get_casts_by_parent` per user cast, capped at `REPLIES_PER_CAST_CAP` per shard (observed 2026-11-09).

## Bluesky

### OAuth
- AT Protocol OAuth: PAR with PKCE and DPoP, DPoP-bound access/refresh tokens; tokens and the DPoP private key in the Keychain credential store, DID/handle on the account. A login started from an account's settings finishes into that account; a new one into a new id kept between start and finish.
- Client id `https://stupidtech.net/stupid-social/oauth/client-metadata.json`, redirect `net.stupidtech:/oauth/bluesky/callback`: atproto requires the private-use scheme to be the reversed FQDN of the `client_id` host.
- Metadata served by the Worker at `workers/oauth-metadata` (route `stupidtech.net/stupid-social/oauth/*`): 200 JSON without redirect for GET/HEAD, 405 other methods, 404 other paths.
- The custom PDS (`pds.stupidtech.net`, separate `cirrus-pds-config` repo) failed `invalid_client` / metadata 404 because Cloudflare Worker routes can't be targets of same-zone Worker `fetch()` (Custom Domains can). Fix: the PDS Worker has an `OAUTH_METADATA` service binding to `stupid-social-oauth-metadata` and a fetch shim routing only the client-metadata URL through it.
- Login hint discovery: handle → DID → DID document → PDS endpoint → protected-resource metadata → authorization server (not hardcoded `bsky.social`; the wrong server gave a bogus reactivation prompt).
- PAR returns the initial `use_dpop_nonce` challenge as HTTP 400, not 401 (observed 2026-07-14); `BlueskyClient` retries nonce challenges on both.
- Token requests (code exchange and refresh) retry once on `use_dpop_nonce` with the server's `DPoP-Nonce`, and keep the newest nonce as `authNonce`. bsky.social rejects a nonce within about 3 minutes (observed 2026-10-29), so before this every refresh after sign-in failed with `serviceError("OAuth token request failed")` until the user signed in again. Probe: `uv run --with cryptography python3 scripts/bluesky-web-client.py token-nonce [--auth-server URL]` (throwaway key and invalid refresh token, so no real session is touched).
- The Cirrus PDS (`pds.stupidtech.net`) sends no DPoP nonces. It rotates the refresh token on every refresh and revokes the previous access token straight away; access tokens last 1 hour, refresh tokens 90 days (`@getcirrus/oauth-provider`, observed 2026-10-29). Two refreshes with the same refresh token therefore always fail one with `invalid_grant`, e.g. two devices sharing the session through iCloud Keychain before the rotated tokens sync.
- So `BlueskyClient` refreshes once per refresh token: concurrent callers, in any client instance for the account, share one request (`RefreshCoalescer`). When a refresh is refused with `invalid_grant`, it uses the tokens in the keychain if another device or client saved newer ones since (refreshing those if they're expiring); otherwise it throws `BlueskyClientError.refreshTokenRejected`, and the feed source marks the account invalid so Settings offers to sign in again.
- A PDS 401 that isn't a nonce challenge (`use_dpop_nonce` in `WWW-Authenticate` or the JSON error) means the access token was revoked: the request retries once with newer saved tokens or a refresh. A 401 merely carrying a `DPoP-Nonce` header isn't a challenge; retrying on that alone looped forever on a revoked token.
- Errors are `BlueskyClientError` cases (`refreshTokenRejected`, `tokenRequestFailed`, `unauthorized`, `requestFailed`), so usage metrics, which send only the type and case, say which step failed.

### Endpoints
- `app.bsky.notification.listNotifications` (feed), `app.bsky.actor.getProfile` / `searchActors`, `app.bsky.feed.getPostThread` (detail).
- Side effect: `BlueskyClient.notifications()` marks seen after a successful list via `app.bsky.notification.updateSeen` on the user's PDS, DPoP-authenticated JSON `{"seenAt":"<ISO-8601>"}` (same as bsky.app). Probe: `bluesky-web-client.py load-notifications`.
- Timestamps may have fractional seconds (`…12:00:50.268751Z`) that `.iso8601` rejects, breaking the whole decode (observed 2026-07-14); `BlueskyClient` uses a custom decoder accepting both forms.
- Like/repost/follow notifications often have empty `record.text` and identify the target only via `reasonSubject`; links derive from it; no actor-handle preview fallback; reasons map to `liked`/`reposted`/`quoted`.

### UI
- Badge PNGs from `web-cdn.bsky.app/static/favicon-16x16.png`, `favicon-32x32.png`, 48px resize of `favicon.png`; `bsky.app/favicon.ico` 404s.

### Chat (DMs)
- `chat.bsky.convo.*` (`BlueskyChat.swift`) go to the user's PDS with `atproto-proxy: did:web:api.bsky.chat#bsky_chat`, which forwards them to Bluesky's chat service. They need the `transition:chat.bsky` OAuth scope: the app requests `atproto transition:generic transition:chat.bsky`, and the hosted client metadata (`workers/oauth-metadata`, `docs/BLUESKY_OAUTH_CLIENT_METADATA.json`) must list it, since a request can only ask for scopes the metadata declares. Sessions signed in before chat support keep their old scope through refreshes, so chat calls check the stored scope first and fail with "Sign in to Bluesky again to use chats." rather than calling.
- Chats list: `listConvos?status=accepted&limit=50` (message requests left out); `muted` → gray badge; no archive. Group titles are the other members' names.
- Chat: `BlueskyChatAdapter` polls `getMessages` (newest first, `cursor` for older pages) every 10 s; the first load of a visit also reads `getConvo` for members and `unreadCount`. Bluesky reports no read cursor, only `unreadCount`, so the read position is just before the `unreadCount`-th newest message from others in the first page (or the newest message when nothing is unread). `markRead` sends `updateRead {convoId, messageId}`.
- Sending is text only; Bluesky chat has no replies or attachments. Shared posts (`app.bsky.embed.record#view`) show as cards opening bsky.app; deleted messages (`#deletedMessageView`) as deleted. Facets aren't used.
- Reactions: `addReaction` / `removeReaction {convoId, messageId, value}` return the message; several emoji per person, so toggling adds or removes only that emoji.
- Unknown union members in `convos`/`messages` are skipped (`BlueskyLossyList`) rather than failing the page.
- Non-401 XRPC errors with a `message` throw it as `SourceError.serviceError` (such as why someone can't be messaged); others stay `BlueskyClientError.requestFailed`.
- Probe: `uv run --with cryptography scripts/bluesky-web-client.py convos|convo|messages --summary` (shape only; `--show-text` prints only the viewer's own text); `send`, `mark-read`, `react` and `unreact` require `--expect-member <handle>` matching the chat's other member. The probe reads the per-account fallback item (`credentials.bluesky-<uuid>`, `--account-id` with several), which exists only when the simulator's Keychain write failed; a session in the simulator Keychain can't be read by the probe (observed 2026-10-29).
- Members of deleted accounts come back with the handle `missing.invalid` and no display name (observed 2026-10-29); they show as "Deleted Account", as in Bluesky's app.

## Instagram

### Web client and requests

- `InstagramClient` is a stateful mobile-web client (not Android private API emulation): bootstraps `https://www.instagram.com/` with the iPhone Safari UA, parses web runtime tokens/config, tracks `x-ig-www-claim`, discovers Relay doc IDs at runtime from loaded web bundles (never hardcoded; they change per `instagram_web_pkg` build) and sends explicit selected-cookie headers.
- Mobile web app id `1217981644879628` with `X-IG-Max-Touch-Points: 5`. iPhone UA loads a broader chunk set than desktop (103 vs 67 operations; overlapping IDs identical); like/unlike media, comment like, repost, save, follow mutations are mobile-only. Desktop web path not pursued (observed 2026-07-12)
- Explicit `Cookie:` header required; URLSession strips manual Cookie headers under default handling. Use `URLSessionConfiguration.ephemeral` with `httpShouldSetCookies = false`, `httpCookieAcceptPolicy = .never`, `httpCookieStorage = nil`, plus `request.httpShouldHandleCookies = false` per request.
- `ensureBootstrapped` shares one in-flight `refreshState` task so cold-start requests don't each fetch the homepage.
- Relay mutations need `av=<actorID>` (17-digit Instagram actor FBID from the homepage, also `NON_FACEBOOK_USER_ID`, distinct from `ds_user_id`) and `__user=0` (the page's `USER_ID`, not `ds_user_id`). Without `av`: `for (;;);` error `1357001` "Not Logged In" (with friendly-name fields) or silent `null` root (without). `InstagramWebState.actorID` is parsed from the homepage and sent as `av` on every GraphQL POST (observed 2026-10-25)
- IGT bearer: `Authorization: Bearer IGT:2:<base64({ds_user_id,sessionid})>` (seen in `ig-set-authorization` from `accounts/current_user`) is deterministic and must keep base64 `=` padding, else `login_required` 403. Used for mobile `i.instagram.com` calls (story delete).
- Endpoint status: web `/api/v1/feed/reels_tray/` and `/api/v1/direct_v2/inbox/` return HTML (404 / not-logged-in); mobile `i.instagram.com/api/v1/feed/reels_tray/` and `feed/user/<id>/story/` with IGT bearer auth work (observed 2026-10-25)
- Rate limit: ~a dozen mutation attempts in a short window led a read-only GraphQL query to return HTTP 400 `{"spam":true}` (session survived). Space out experiments (observed 2026-10-25)
- Every request goes through `InstagramClient.send`, which keeps a per-account `InstagramCooldown`: a response `instagramResponseIsRateLimited` recognizes (HTTP 429, `{"spam":true}`, `message: "feedback_required"`, or a message containing "wait a few minutes", which Instagram sends as HTTP 401 with `require_login`) throws `SourceError.rateLimited(until:)` and nothing goes out for 5 minutes, doubling while Instagram keeps saying so, up to an hour; the first success after it ends starts over. The rate-limit check runs before the 401/403 → signed-out mapping, so a rate limit no longer asks the user to sign in again; sources mark the account `.rateLimited` ("Rate limited" in Settings) and `.valid` again on the next success. The web client itself has no retry or backoff for these: 401 `require_login` sends it to login or a rate-limit page, `feedback_required` opens the Account Status restriction dialog, 429 appears only in signup and video retry code (bundles of `/direct/inbox/`, observed 2026-11-20)
- Realtime Direct (probe `direct-live`, validated 2026-11-20; in the app as `InstagramLiveFeed`): `wss://gateway.instagram.com/ws/lightspeed?x-dgw-appid=<web app id>&x-dgw-appversion=0&x-dgw-authtype=6:0&x-dgw-version=5&x-dgw-uuid=<actorID>&x-dgw-tier=prod&x-dgw-deviceid=<IGDMqttWebDeviceID>`, authenticated by the cookies alone. DGW framing (types in the web client's `DGWConstants`): ping `09` and pong `0a` are one byte; other frames are type, stream id (2 bytes LE), length (3 bytes LE), payload. The client sends establish-stream (15) with `{}` and a data frame (13) whose payload is a 16-bit LE id (bit 15 asks for an ack) and `{"app_id","device_id","payload":"{database:223,epoch_id:0,failure_count:0,last_applied_cursor:{seq_id},sync_params:{user_agent:\"WMI Web\",snapshot_at_ms,prevalidated_graphql_doc_id},version:-3}","request_id","type":2}`; acks (12) carry the id back; it pings every 10 s. `seq_id` starts at the inbox mailbox's `iris_inactive_subscription_uq_seq_id`; the doc id is `IGDSlideDeltaProcessorQuery`'s. Pushes are data frames `{"request_id":null,"payload":<base64 protobuf>}`: field 2 { field 3 { 1: database, 2: JSON array of `IGDSlideDeltaProcessorQuery` results (`data.slide_delta_processor[]`, e.g. `SlideUQPPNewMessage` with `uq_seq_id`, `thread_fbid` and a `message` shaped like the thread query's), 3: seq id } }. A message sent from another client arrived within a second. The iPhone pages' scripts don't carry `IGDSlideDeltaProcessorQuery`: the iPhone inbox loads `requireDeferred("IGDMqttSetupWithSlideMailbox.react")`, and the bootloader (POST `/ajax/bootloader-endpoint/?modules=IGDMqttSetupWithSlideMailbox.react` with the page's ajax fields) returns scripts that do (doc `29410918118524512` then); asking it for the query's own modules answers 404. The iPhone web client sends `app_id` `1217981644879628` in the sync request; the app sends the validated `936619743392459` for both.
- `InstagramLiveFeed` (one per account, a `LiveConnection`): `InstagramClient.directRealtimeSubscription()` reads the inbox (for the seq id), the device id and the bootloaded doc id, and builds the request and opening frames (`InstagramRealtime.swift` has the framing and payload decoding, covered by `InstagramRealtimeTests`). It acks data frames that ask, pings every 10 s, and counts the connection open when the sync request is answered. Pushed `SlideUQPPNewMessage`s are written into open chats' history as they arrive; other changes to an open chat's thread load its newest page once per burst; each changed thread's Chats row is fetched again (`directThread`, debounced 1 s). A reconnect catches up: open chats load their newest page and the Chats list reloads. Signed out stops it; a rate limit retries after the cooldown, and nothing connects during one. With the feed, open chats poll every 5 minutes as a safety net (60 s without). Testing this got the account challenged once (all sessions signed out): one client per session at a time, no user-agent overrides on a signed-in browser
- Instagram's web client sends no data requests while a page sits open: in 90 s idle on `/direct/inbox/` and 150 s on `/`, only `/logging_client_events`, `/ajax/bz` and the bootloader. It keeps four websockets: `edge-chat.instagram.com/chat` (MQTT, subscribes `/pubsub`), and `gateway.instagram.com/ws/realtime` (mostly Falco analytics), `/ws/lightspeed` and `/ws/rpsignaling`. Nothing story-related arrives on any of them; stories are fetched only on page load, from the homepage HTML (observed 2026-11-20)
- WARNING: never send signed mobile `POST i.instagram.com/api/v2/media/seen/?reel=1&live_vod=0` (HMAC `signed_body`, IGT bearer, Android UA): it returned 403 `login_required` (`logout_reason: 3`) and invalidated the session server-side (observed 2026-10-25). Wrong signed-body variants of that endpoint also return `{"status":"ok"}` without effect, so `status: ok` proves nothing.

### Identity, homepage and session validity

- Identity and the stories tray come from the authenticated homepage (`InstagramHomepage.swift`), not the Direct inbox or a standalone tray request: parse only the `PolarisViewer` module for own identity, verify its numeric ID against the selected credentials, read the named preloaded tray payload. Empty tray is accepted; missing/malformed payloads and unrelated users are rejected. Homepage reads bypass local HTTP caching and share an in-flight fetch. `currentUserProfile()` (setup, validation, revalidation, composer's own actor) is built from it; numeric profile-query setup gets the viewer username from homepage state.
- `USER_ID: "0"` on the homepage is NOT a logged-out signal: it is also `0` for logged-in Instagram-only accounts (observed 2026-10-26)
- Error classification (validation, settings revalidation, story refresh): generic service/parse failures keep saved credentials, known identity for the same account, and notification/story preferences; only genuine auth failures, explicit logged-out payloads and mismatched identities require reconnect. Missing usernames can be repaired even when an avatar is cached.
- Auth failures throw `SourceError.notConfigured`; sources mark metadata `invalidCredentials` but keep stale credentials for checkpoint recovery. Triggers:
  - HTTP 200 login/challenge HTML from an authenticated request.
  - Expired session: `GET /` answers `302 → https://www.instagram.com/` with `Set-Cookie: sessionid=…; expires=Thu, 01-Jan-1970 …; Max-Age=-…`. With manual cookies URLSession would follow with the dead cookie until "too many redirects"; `webDataRequest`/`webTextRequest` attach a per-task `InstagramSessionExpiryRedirectGuard` that refuses such redirects, and any response expiring `sessionid` throws (observed 2026-10-25). Covered by `testInstagramDetectsExpiredSessionCookie`.
  - Checkpointed sessions still load the homepage (with `actorID`) and expire no cookie; API calls fail instead: `POST /api/v1/news/inbox/` → HTTP 400 `{"message":"checkpoint_required",…}`, `GET /api/v1/direct_v2/inbox/` → HTTP 404 logged-out page (observed 2026-10-26). `instagramBodyRequiresLogin` matches JSON `message` `checkpoint_required`/`challenge_required`/`login_required` or an `<html>` class list containing `not-logged-in`. Covered by `testInstagramDetectsLoginRequiredBodies`.
  - A suspended or checkpointed session can instead get the UFAC page for `GET /`: still signed in, with `PolarisViewer`, but routed to `/accounts/suspended/` (`initialRouteInfo.route.routePath`, `PolarisUFACRoute`) with no preloaded tray. A challenged session gets the auth platform's redirect instead, routed to `/auth_platform/logged_in_redirect/` (`PolarisAuthPlatformLoggedInRedirectRoute`, `next=…?__coig_challenge_redirected=1`), also with no tray (observed 2026-11-10). The homepage parser treats any route under either prefix like `is_logged_out_user`; before, the missing tray surfaced as `SourceError.invalidResponse` on every story refresh (observed 2026-10-30). `scripts/instagram-web-client.py homepage-tray` runs the app's homepage checks and reports which one fails

### Login WebView

- `InstagramLoginWebView` uses WKWebView's native Safari UA (not Chrome/Android UAs) and saves once `sessionid`, `csrftoken` and `ds_user_id` exist (on a page outside login and the challenge), keeping `mid`, `rur` and `ig_did` when present. It used to wait for all six, and a tester's login sat signed in with nothing shown and no `account_connect_failed` (observed 2026-11-20). A fresh non-persistent WebKit session gets `csrftoken`, `mid`, `ig_did`, `datr` before login (observed 2026-11-20); `rur` comes only from the login itself, and without it story upload/delete on `i.instagram.com` may fail while everything else works.
- When the login can't finish, the sheet says why below the page with Try Again (reloads `/` in the same web view and lets the challenge open once more): Instagram still asks for verification after the challenge ran, or a `sessionid` arrived without `csrftoken`/`ds_user_id`. Each outcome reports `instagram_login`.
- Checks cookies after every load but skips capture while the URL is a login/challenge/email-signup page, so an already-authenticated home page is captured immediately.
- Reconnect: Settings shows "Reconnect Instagram", hides content toggles and seeds the WebView with existing cookies; captured cookies are deleted only if validation still reports expired login.
- A checkpointed session still renders signed in, so before accepting cookies the WebView calls `GET /api/v1/accounts/edit/web_form_data/` from the page; `checkpoint_required` (carries the challenge URL) or a logged-out page opens the challenge. `/challenge/` hands off to Meta `/auth_platform/logged_in_redirect/` pages, treated as part of the challenge. The challenge is opened at most once (an earlier version looped), so a locked session is never saved.

### Notifications

- `InstagramClient.notifications` runs `POST /api/v1/news/inbox/` and Direct (when enabled) concurrently (`async let`); Direct failures degrade to empty so news still loads.
- News inbox returns `new_stories`, `old_stories` and `priority_stories` (latest story likes land in `priority_stories`; merged first) (observed 2026-05-07)
- `rich_text` uses `{username|color|weight|user?id=N|suffix}` placeholders; actors parsed from those and matched to `profile_id`/`second_profile_id` (arrive as strings; decoded with the flexible string helper) for avatars (max 2 structured actors). Text after `:` is comment content (`target.text`); `timestamp` is Unix epoch (observed 2026-07-14)
- `story_like` aggregate counts exist only in `rich_text` (`@a, @b and N others liked your story`); normalization stores `2 + N` as `likeCount`.
- `InstagramNotificationCategory`: `.follows`, `.comments`, `.likes`, `.storyHighlights`, `.directMessages`; unmapped types (churn, Threads, `suspicious_login`) dropped. No metadata migration: accounts saved before Direct Messages existed must reconnect to get it.
- Story URLs from `destination` (`story_fullscreen?reel_id=archiveDay%3A{HASH}&feeditem_id={MEDIA}_{USER}`): archive `https://www.instagram.com/stories/archive/{hash}/?initial_media_id={id}`, or active `https://www.instagram.com/stories/{username}/{media_id}/` when `reel_id` matches the account id (`parseStoryURL(accountId:accountUsername:)`).
- Story-like rows have `args.media: null`; the real media id is `feeditem_id=<media_id>_<owner_id>` in `args.destination` (row `pk` is just a notification id). Detail also recovers `initial_media_id` and archive reel ids from cached target URLs.
- Story-like detail hydration: active stories via `/stories/{username}/`; archived via `archivedStoryReel` → `POST /api/v1/feed/reels_media/` with form `reel_ids=["archiveDay:<archive_reel_id>"]` (JSON array string; plain ids or `reel_ids[]=` give `400 Invalid reel id list`); picks the widest `image_versions2` candidate.
- Story/post targets set `NotificationTarget.author` to the connected account (actors stay the likers); a missing account avatar triggers an opportunistic current-user refresh.
- `mediaInfo` is unsupported (throws `SourceError.unsupported`) since the Android `media/info` endpoint left the production path.

### Direct (web GraphQL)

- No REST: `GET /api/v1/direct_v2/inbox/` answers HTTP 404 with a logged-out page even on a valid session; the web inbox uses "IGD Off-Msys" Relay operations (observed 2026-10-26)
- All operations are `POST /api/graphql` with `fb_api_req_friendly_name` and `X-Root-Field-Name` (`/graphql/query` returns a null root):
  - `PolarisDirectInboxMobileQuery` (root `get_slide_mailbox_for_iris_subscription`, var `device_id_for_iris_subscription`)
  - `IGDThreadListOffMsysPaginationQuery` (`fetch__SlideMailbox`; `id` = mailbox id, `count`, `cursor`, `folder: "INBOX"`)
  - `IGDThreadDetailQuery` (`get_slide_thread_nullable`; `thread_fbid`, `min_uq_seq_id: null`; fails with `missing_required_variable_value` without the provided variables `IGDSafetyNoticesWebEnabled: false` and `IGDHideSeenWhenReadReceiptsOffGK: false`, observed 2026-11-20; desktop and iPhone bundles both use doc `40139187875726817` then)
  - `IGDMessageListOffMsysQuery` (`fetch__SlideThread`; `id` = thread fbid, `after` = previous `slide_messages.page_info.end_cursor`)
  - `useIGDMarkThreadAsReadMutation` (`xig_direct_item_seen_mutation_with_slide_messaging_response`; `{data: {item_id: "", message_id}, metadata: {ig_thread_igid: <legacy thread_id>}}`)
  - `IGDirectTextSendMutation` (`xig_direct_text_send_with_slide_messaging_response`; `ig_thread_igid` = thread fbid, `offline_threading_id` = ms << 22 | 22 random bits, `text.sensitive_string_value`, optional `reply_to_message_id`, `mentions`/`mentioned_user_ids` `[]`, `send_attribution: "igd_web_chat_tab:in_thread"`, `replied_to_client_context`/`sampled`/`commands`/`recipient_igids`/`replied_to_item_id` null; the older shape without `replied_to_client_context`, `sampled` and `send_attribution` fails with `field_exception` 1675030, observed 2026-11-20). `ig_thread_igid` is the thread's `thread_fbid` (16 digits here), not the id in a `/direct/t/<id>/` URL the web app opens, which can differ and fails the same way
  - `IGDirectReactionSendMutation` (`xig_direct_reaction_send_with_slide_messaging_response`; `{input: {emoji, item_id: "", message_id, reaction_status: "created" | "deleted", thread_id: <thread_fbid>}}`, returns `slide_message.reactions`)
- Relay provided variables (`__relay_internal__pv__…`) are sent with the web's values: professional GK false, pinned threads true, max unread 5, thread list actions true, chat themes false, initial page 20 (observed 2026-10-26), safety notices false (observed 2026-11-20). A missing one fails the whole query with `missing_required_variable_value`, so compare against the web client's request (`x-fb-friendly-name`) when a Direct query starts failing. A query's module in the scripts lists only its own arguments and provided variables, not those its fragments use (`IGDHideSeenWhenReadReceiptsOffGK` isn't in `IGDThreadDetailQuery`'s), so the request is the reference, not the module. `directGraphQL` logs a failed query's operation, doc id and variable names (category `InstagramDirect`, no values)
- Inbox returns a null mailbox unless `device_id_for_iris_subscription` is the page config `IGDMqttWebDeviceID.clientId` from a Direct page (not `ig_did`) (observed 2026-10-26)
- Doc IDs: `/direct/inbox/` scripts carry only inbox, inbox pagination and thread detail; message list, send, read and reaction operations are only in a real thread page `/direct/t/<thread_fbid>/` (`/direct/t/0/` serves the inbox bundle). `InstagramClient` loads the needed page once per missing operation (coalesced), keeps doc IDs and device id in memory (observed 2026-10-26)
- Shape (observed 2026-10-26): threads have `thread_fbid` (URL/send id), legacy `thread_id` (read mutation), `thread_title`, `users` (excludes viewer; `id` plus `interop_messaging_user_fbid`), `viewer`, `slide_read_receipts` (`participant_fbid`, `watermark_timestamp_ms`), `slide_messages` (newest first). Inbox message nodes have `id` but not `message_id`, and only `text_body` plus an `igd_snippet` ("X sent a photo."). Content types: `SlideMessageText`, `SlideMessageImageContent` (`attachments[]` with `attachment_cdn_url`, `preview_cdn_url`, `preview_width/height`), `SlideMessageXMAContent` (`xma` with `header_title_text`, `title_text`, `eyebrow_text`, `target_url`, `preview_image`; `xma_text_body` = story-reply text), `SlideMessageAdminText` (`text_fragments`), videos, others. Reactions `reactions[] {reaction, sender_fbid}`.
- Reactions: one per person per message (new emoji replaces). Instagram stores emoji without U+FE0F (❤️ → ❤), and delete only works with the stored form. Probe `direct-react <thread_fbid> <message_id> <emoji> [--remove]` (observed 2026-10-26)
- Native: `InstagramDirect.swift` holds DTOs (decoded with `convertFromSnakeCase` after extracting the root field) and client calls; DTOs decode the GraphQL shape only.
- Forwards (probe `direct-forward`, validated in a thread the user named for testing, observed 2026-11-07; the web client's `useIGDForwardMessageViaIGDMAPIOffMsys`): text through `IGDirectTextSendMutation` with `forwarded_from_thread_id` (source thread fbid) and `is_forwarded_from_own_message` (sender is the viewer's `interop_messaging_user_fbid`); one photo, video or voice clip through `IGDMediaForwardPublisherMutation` (root `xig_direct_media_send_with_slide_messaging_response`, doc id in the thread page's scripts) {attachment_fbid, forwarded_from_thread_id, forwarded_from_thread_item_id (`mid.$…`), is_forwarded_from_own_message, offline_threading_id, recipient_users: null, thread_id}; both answer `{id, message_id, timestamp_ms}`. Shares (XMA) use `POST /api/v1/direct_v2/threads/broadcast/forward/`, which answers this web session with a logged-out page, as `direct_v2/inbox` does, so `forwardReference` returns nil for them and they go as copies. Received forwards have `igd_is_forwarded: true` and nothing about their origin. Voice forward was validated; photo and video use the same mutation but weren't sent
- Voice clips (probe `direct-send-voice`, same thread): Ogg Opus `POST https://rupload.instagram.com/messenger_audio/<uuid4>` with the raw file and headers `X-Entity-Name: audioclip-<ms>-<duration ms>.ogg`, `X-Entity-Type`/`Content-Type: audio/ogg`, `X-Entity-Length`, `Offset: 0`, `ig-u-ds-user-id: <interop_messaging_user_fbid>` (not `ds_user_id`), `audio_type: fb_voice_message`, `waveform_data: {"amplitudes": [64 floats 0–1], "sampling_freq": levels per second}` (integers 0–100 came back clamped to 0 and 1) → `{media_id, media_id_str}`; then `IGDirectMediaSendMutation` {attachment_fbid: media_id_str, forwarded_from_thread_id: null, is_forwarded_from_own_message: null, offline_threading_id, reply_to_message_id, thread_id}. Received: `content_type: AUDIOS`, `content.audio_attachments[0]` {attachment_cdn_url (signed, expiring fbcdn .ogg), attachment_fbid, playable_duration_ms (Instagram measures it: 2007 for 2 s), waveform_data}. The Swift upload goes through `webDataRequest`, so it carries the web headers the probe left out
- Unsending (probe `direct-unsend`): `IGDMessageUnsendDialogOffMsysMutation` (doc `26948700068153789`, root `direct_unsend_message`, only from bootloading `IGDMessageUnsendDialogOffMsys.react` via POST `/ajax/bootloader-endpoint/?modules=…`; a GET is error 1357004) {message_id, send_data: {thread_id: <legacy thread_id>}} → `true`. The app doesn't unsend yet

### Direct in the feed and Chats

- Feed DMs (`InstagramDirectMessageParser`) use the inbox first page (15 threads): one item per thread whose newest message is from someone else and newer than the viewer's read receipt (or thread marked unread). Item ids `instagram:direct:<thread_fbid>:<mid>`. The feed fetch doesn't mark read.
- Titles: `<name> sent you a message` / `sent you a reel` / `sent you a post` / `replied to your story`, using the latest sender; media shares show the XMA preview image.
- `directMediaSharesEnabled` (Messaging section, `Posts and Reels` toggle) suppresses media shares (`MESSAGE_INLINE_SHARE`); `storiesEnabled` (default `true`, "Show Stories") skips story fetching.
- Chats list shows the newest 35 threads (two inbox requests per reload), unread counts over each thread's 5 newest messages. Muted state isn't read for Instagram chats.
- `InstagramChatAdapter` (driven by `AdapterChatViewModel`): newest 20 messages, older pages on scroll, new messages pushed by `InstagramLiveFeed` (`ChatLiveUpdating`) with a 5-minute safety poll, text send, swipe-to-reply, photos in the image viewer, shared posts/reels/stories as cards opening Instagram, admin messages as captions. Opening a chat marks it read; the read receipt moves when an unread incoming message shows. Covered by `InstagramDirectTests`.
- Reactions UI: `InstagramClient.setDirectReaction`; the view model overrides `yourEmojis`/`toggleReaction`, `canReact` once the thread loads. Toggling your current emoji removes it (sends the stored form). Optimistic, replaced by returned reactions, reverted on failure. `displayEmoji` maps stored emoji to the quick reaction differing only by U+FE0F.

### Stories: reading

- The bar is built from the tray alone: `fetchStoryReels(reason:)` returns every tray entry as a reel without slides (`latestMediaAt` from `latest_reel_media`, seen when `seen >= latest_reel_media`), and `loadStoryReel(id:)` fetches the person's `/stories/<username>/` page when the viewer reaches them (and the person after them), coalesced and cached while the tray lists the same `latest_reel_media`. The user's own reel loads with the tray, so the composer's optimistic slide keeps working. Before, every refresh loaded 15 story pages (full HTML) at once, plus more as the bar scrolled; the web client loads stories only when they open. `UnifiedStoryViewer` shows a spinner on a reel with no slides, doesn't advance or mark it seen until they load, resumes at the first unseen slide, and moves on if they can't load. Marking seen also moves the cached tray entry's `seen`, so a refresh that reuses the tray doesn't show the stories as unseen again
- Automatic refreshes (foreground, account changes) reuse a tray and a notifications fetch younger than `InstagramNotificationSource.automaticRefreshInterval` (5 minutes); pull-to-refresh, ⌘R, posting and deleting a story always fetch
- Tray comes from the homepage's preloaded payload (`storiesTrayResponse`). Rejected: `POST /api/v1/feed/reels_tray/` (web: SPA-shell HTML ~600KB for valid credentials, observed 2026-08-19) and GraphQL `PolarisStoriesV3TrayContainerQuery` (HTTP 400 `{"spam":true,"status":"fail"}`, observed 2026-07-12).
- Tray decoding: `InstagramTrayItem.id` tries `UInt64` then `String` (highlight ids like `highlightRewind:3022481654`); `media_ids` is not decoded because a type mismatch failed the whole tray and falsely invalidated the account. A successful tray fetch re-validates the account to `.valid`; stories refetch on pull-to-refresh, and on foreground activation at most every 5 minutes.
- Tray `seen` is a timestamp: a reel is seen only if `seen >= newest slide takenAt`. Slides sorted by `takenAt`.
- Muted reels filtered before dedupe/media fetch using tray `muted`, `user.friendship_status.is_muting_reel`, `.muting`.
- Close Friends is only visible on the tray (`has_besties_media`, `latest_besties_reel_media`), not per slide; `InstagramStoryReel.hasCloseFriendsMedia` drives a green star badge.
- Slide media comes from server-rendered `/stories/<username>/` Relay payloads (`xdt_api__v1__feed__reels_media`); the standalone stories-media GraphQL resolver returns execution errors. Not batched via `POST /api/v1/feed/reels_media/`: with normal reel ids it strips `video_versions`, `story_music_stickers`, `story_feed_media`, `story_link_stickers` (observed 2026-08-16); it also rejects >30 ids with HTTP 400 `Too many reels requested`.
- `storyReel` doesn't download page `<script src>` bundles on read; it records inline doc ids and script URLs (`recordStoryPageDocIds`); story mutation ids are discovered on demand via `discoverStoryPageDocIds`.
- `InstagramNotificationSource.ownStoryActor` uses stored username+avatar, calling `currentUserProfile` only when missing.
- Slide fields: `video_versions` (playable MP4) + `video_duration`; `story_feed_media` (`media_code`, `media_id`, `media_type`, `product_type`, placement) gives post/reel embeds without caption/thumbnail, linked as `https://www.instagram.com/p/{code}/`; `story_music_stickers.music_asset_info` (`title`, `display_artist`, artwork `cover_artwork_thumbnail_url`/`cover_artwork_url` or `_uri` variants, may be missing; timing `start_time_ms`/`end_time_ms` or `duration_in_ms`/`duration_ms`/`audio_asset_duration_ms`) → `InstagramStoryMusic` pill; `story_link_stickers` → pills; `has_liked`.
- Mentions come back as `story_bloks_stickers[].bloks_sticker.sticker_data.ig_mention` (not `reel_mentions`), decoded into `InstagramStorySlide.mentions`; `InstagramStoryMention.actor` falls back to username as actor id (detail hydrates by username).
- Video plays via a small `AVPlayerLayer` wrapper (SwiftUI `VideoPlayer` transport controls overlap the story UI); duration drives progress; images stay 5 s even with music stickers. Music sticker audio is never played separately.

### Stories: seen, like

- `InstagramStorySlide.id` is `media.pk` (or the numeric prefix of `id`) for seen/like/delete.
- Seen: `PolarisStoriesV3SeenMutation` (doc `26234228992942885` at the time) on `/api/graphql` with `fb_api_caller_class=RelayModern`, `fb_api_req_friendly_name` + `X-FB-Friendly-Name`, no `X-Root-Field-Name`. Variables: `reelId`, `reelMediaId` (media `pk`), `reelMediaOwnerId`, `reelMediaTakenAt` (int), `viewSeenAt` (int seconds). Root `xdt_mark_story_reel_seen` (`XDTMarkSeenResponse`): non-null object = success, `null` = failure; no web REST fallback (observed 2026-10-25). `markStorySeen` throws unless it is an object; `markReelAsSeen` doesn't swallow errors; `StoryBarViewModel` reports via `errorMessage`. Covered by `testInstagramStorySeenSendsActorAndFailsWhenIgnored`.
- Like: homepage media mutations `usePolarisLikeMediaXIGLikeMutation`/`usePolarisLikeMediaXIGUnlikeMutation` (roots `xig_media_like`/`xig_media_unlike`) via `setMediaLiked`, because story-specific like mutations need story-page discovery and the like call only has a media ID. Generic REST `POST /api/v1/media/{media_id}/like/` returns 200 `status=fail` with no effect. Heart shown for non-own slides.

### Stories: posting and deletion

- `InstagramClient.publishPhotoStory` uses the web flow (mobile signed `configure_to_story` returned HTTP 500 "something went wrong during media publish" for every variant tried, likely session trust gating, observed 2026-05-16): upload raw bytes to `https://i.instagram.com/rupload_igphoto/fb_uploader_{upload_id}` with web app id `1217981644879628`, `Content-Type: image/jpeg`, `X-Instagram-Rupload-Params` of only `media_type`, `upload_id`, `upload_media_height`, `upload_media_width` (width/height required); then unsigned form `POST https://www.instagram.com/api/v1/media/configure_to_story/` with `caption`, `configure_mode=1`, `upload_id`, `jazoest` (`"2" + sum(CSRF unicode scalars)`) and headers `X-Requested-With`, `X-CSRFToken`, `X-ASBD-ID`, `X-Web-Session-ID`, `X-Instagram-AJAX`. Failures surface the step/status in the composer.
- Close Friends: form gets `audience=besties`; response echoes `media.audience=besties`. `publishPhotoStory(closeFriends:)` is threaded through `StoryPosting.postPhotoStory`, `StoryBarViewModel.postInstagramStory` and the composer's `onPost`.
- Mentions: composer `@` opens an Instagram-only profile search overlay (`GET /web/search/topsearch/?context=blended&query=...`); posting serializes `reel_mentions` (normalized caption-center geometry, e.g. `{"user_id","username","x","y","z","width","height","rotation"}`) plus `include_e2ee_mentioned_user_list=1`.
- Delete: `InstagramClient.deleteStory` uses mobile REST `POST https://i.instagram.com/api/v1/media/<pk>_<ds_user_id>/delete/?media_type=PHOTO|VIDEO`, form `media_id=<pk>_<ds_user_id>`, IGT bearer, `X-IG-App-ID: 567067343352427`, Instagram Android or iPhone Safari UA. Returns `{"did_delete":true,"status":"ok"}`; fails loudly unless `did_delete` is true (observed 2026-10-15). Rejected: web Relay `usePolarisStoriesV3DeleteMediaMutation` (`1357001` "Not Logged In", possibly the missing `av`), web `POST www.instagram.com/api/v1/media/<id>/delete/` (HTTP 400 `useragent mismatch`), mobile with cookie auth (generic `status=fail`).
- Reference (unimplemented): video stories need `/rupload_igvideo/`, cover via `/rupload_igphoto/`, upload-finish, then `configure_to_story/?video=1`; direct story sends `configure_mode=2`; link stickers serialize to `story_cta=[{links:[{webUri}]}]`. Sources: `third-party/instagram-private-api` and decompiled APK under ignored `third-party/instagram-429-decompiled`.

### Profiles and posts

- Profiles use Relay `PolarisProfilePageContentQuery`; posts paginate with `PolarisProfilePostsQuery` (`InstagramProfile.swift`), both POSTed to `/graphql/query` with the Relay form fields and friendly name, as the web client sends them: a GET of `/graphql/query/` is answered `{"spam":true}` (observed 2026-11-21). Doc ids, the providers each query passes (its `.graphql` module's `__relay_internal__pv__<Name>relayprovider` variables) and each provider's definition (`__d("<Name>.relayprovider",["gkx"|"qex"|"justknobx"],…("<id>")`, `===!0` for a strict `true`) come from the profile page's bundles, so a changed set doesn't break the query; values are read from the homepage's `gkxData` (`result`), `qexData` (`r`) and `justknobxData` (`r`), by table, since an id can be in several (684 is in gkx and justknobx). On 2026-11-21 Short Drama moved from config 1697 and gate 12774 to JustKnobs 14 and both queries gained providers, which had failed every profile with "configuration is missing". Errors on single fields come with the data (`field_exception` on a post's `location.profile_pic_url`), so only a missing root fails a query, with Instagram's message. Probe: `profile-config` (providers and values; `--save-html`, `--from-html`), `profile-page <username>`, `profile-posts <username>`; usernames resolved from route metadata; mismatched responses or missing cursors rejected. `web_profile_info` is retired (HTTP 429 HTML "Page Not Found", observed 2026-09-29). Private profiles may have an empty post connection despite a nonzero media count.
- `FeedService.fetchProfile` passes the username for Instagram since cached/story actor ids can be non-numeric/stale.
- Topsearch returns `user.pk` as a string; `InstagramUserInfoResponse.InfoUser` accepts string or number. Media/carousel ids and nested `user.pk` also mix types and decode flexibly; optional media fields decode best-effort per item so one bad item can't fail the page.
- Profiles with empty posts and unknown/nonzero post count still hydrate; a positive media count with zero decoded posts is an error shown in the Posts section; Instagram HTTP error bodies are included in `SourceError.serviceError`; the pagination footer shows the exact error with retry and doesn't auto-retry a failed cursor.
- Follow/unfollow (`InstagramClient.setUserFollowed`, runtime doc ids): `usePolarisFollowUserFollowMutation`, root `xdt_create_friendship`, vars `{"target_user_id": <id>, "data": {"include_follow_friction_check": true}}`; `usePolarisFollowUserUnfollowMutation`, root `xdt_destroy_friendship`, vars `{"target_user_id": <id>}`. Guessed `xig_user_follow`/`{"user_id":...}` returned `execution error`.

### Android private API (reference)

- `i.instagram.com/api/v1/` with cookies needs `rur` besides `sessionid`/`csrftoken`/`ds_user_id`, a UA matching the session's issuing UA (else HTTP 400 `useragent mismatch`), and an explicit `Cookie:` header; `current_user` is more permissive than `reels_tray`/`news/inbox`.

### Open issues

- Missing `av` may also have broken like/unlike/follow Relay mutations before 2026-10-25; not re-tested.

## Spotify

### Auth & session
- WebPlayer credentials captured by `SpotifyLoginWebView` (Bearer, `client-token`, `sp_dc`, `sp_t`). Bearer refreshed via `GET https://open.spotify.com/api/token?reason=transport&productType=web-player&totp=...&totpServer=...&totpVer=61` using `sp_dc`, expiry saved.
- Login: `https://accounts.spotify.com/login` (no `/en/`, no custom UA), `continue` = `https://open.spotify.com/?nd=1`; `Accept-Language` from `Locale.preferredLanguages`.
- Login completes from a `WKHTTPCookieStore` observer on a valid `sp_dc` (optional `sp_t`/`sp_key` kept); only root-path, unexpired cookies for the token host accepted. Main-frame navigation to the WebPlayer is cancelled, as are non-web schemes (`spotify:`, App Store), otherwise login hands off to the Spotify app. Cookie-only credentials carry an expired token date so native renewal runs before validation.
- Account validity is decided only by spclient friend activity (`listening-activity/v1/feed`). Username is display-only and best-effort: Pathfinder `profileAttributes`, falling back to stored `credentials.username`; `resolveUsername()` retries once after minting a fresh client-token from `clienttoken.spotify.com/v1/clienttoken` with the `clientId` returned by `/api/token` (reason=init). `SpotifyCredentials.clientId` is optional/decode-safe. Root cause: an expired/anonymous session (`/api/token` → `isAnonymous: true`) makes Pathfinder 401 while spclient friend activity works; `api.spotify.com/v1/me` is rate-limited (429) (observed 2026-08-30).
- spclient requests (friend activity, user profile, following, followers, audio analysis, speechless) omit the `client-token` header: a stale captured one caused 401 even with a refreshed bearer. Pathfinder still needs a current client token (401 stale / 403 without) (observed 2026-08-16).
- Pathfinder returns 403 without browser `Origin`, `Referer`, `User-Agent` (observed 2026-09-20); all calls go through `SpotifyClient.makePathfinderRequest(...)`/`pathfinderBody(...)`. Client-token response requires the `granted_token` coding key.
- Library ops prefer `initialBearerToken`/`initialBearerTokenExpiresAt` (from `https://open.spotify.com/api/token?reason=init&productType=web-player`) while fresh, refresh it from persisted cookies when expired (~12 h; expired returns 401), else fall back to `credentialsForRequest()`.

### Friend activity (stories)
- `SpotifyClient.friendActivity()`: `POST spclient.wg.spotify.com/listening-activity/v1/feed`, JSON body `{"unused":true,"resultLimit":100}`, the call the desktop app's Listening activity panel makes. Works with the WebPlayer bearer and no client-token. Returns `entities[]`, each `followEntity` or `userEntity` (meaning of the difference unknown; both are friends, other kinds are skipped) with `uri` (`spotify:user:…`) and `activity {entityUri (track), contextUri? (playlist, album, list…), isPlaying, timestamp (ISO 8601 with milliseconds)}`, with no names or artwork. Parsed by `SpotifyListeningActivityParser` (`SpotifyListeningActivityParserTests`). `listening-activity/v1/subscribe` (usernames plus a dealer `connectionId`) is the desktop's realtime path, not needed for polled stories. Probe `listening-activity`.
- `presence-view/v1/buddylist`, used before, returns 200 `{"friends":[]}` for every token, web player and desktop alike, and the web player's own friend feed is a stub (`isSupported: false`) (observed 2026-11-07). Found by relaunching the desktop app with `--remote-debugging-port` and logging its xpui requests over CDP; its friend feed comes through that page, not the native core.
- `SpotifyActivitySource` resolves every entry: tracks through `trackMetadata` (`metadata/4/track/<gid>`: name, first artist, album, cover), users through `userProfile` (display name, avatar; a failed lookup shows the username), playlist/album/artist/show titles through oEmbed, and audio analysis. The four lookups run together, at most 4 requests each at a time, cached in memory per source (about 6 s for 15 friends on a cold launch versus 39 s run one after another, observed 2026-11-07). Entries that aren't tracks (episodes) or whose track didn't load are left out; when no track loads at all, the fetch throws. Story errors are reported as `story_source_error` {network `spotify`}.
- All entries shown, not filtered to the 15-minute now-playing threshold, since valid responses can have none inside it.
- Activity is `SpotifyActivityItem` from `SpotifyActivitySource.fetchActivity(reason:)`, shown only in the stories bar, never in the notification cache.
- Audio analysis: `GET https://spclient.wg.spotify.com/audio-attributes/v1/audio-analysis/{track_id}` (bare id only; URI forms 400/404; `/audio-features/` 404; `extended-metadata` GET 405). No danceability/energy/valence (observed 2026-05-09); only `tempo`, `tempoConfidence`, `loudness`, `mode` kept (`musicAnimation`). Fetched concurrently (limit 4), persisted in `SpotifyAudioAnalysisStore` (UserDefaults JSON).
- Preview: `SpotifyClient.trackPreviewURL(trackId:)` scrapes `audioPreview.url` from unauthenticated `https://open.spotify.com/embed/track/{id}`; nil when no preview (common).
- Liked songs (icon toggle beside Open in Spotify): Pathfinder `areEntitiesInLibrary` (hash `134337999233cc6fdd6b1e6dbf94841409f04a946c5c7b744b09ba0dfe5a85ed`) and `addToLibrary`/`removeFromLibrary` (shared hash `7c5a69420e2bfae3da5cc4e14cbc8bb3f6090f80afc00ffc179177f19be3f33d`, variables `{ "libraryItemUris": ["spotify:track:..."] }`) via `SpotifyClient.isTrackSaved`/`saveTrack`.
- Pathfinder `findTopResults` may put users in `searchV2.topResultsV2.itemsV2` (`UserResponseWrapper`) instead of `usersV2`; both parsed, deduped by username.
- Gander (`gander/v2/GetUserHasUnreadNotification`, `GetNotifications`, need `accept-language: en`) returns only marketing/concert notifications; social notifications likely need the Dealer WebSocket — out of scope.

### Messages (speechless) protocol
- Mobile-only (web player force-closes its `messaging` panel). Endpoints from a static jadx decompile of the Android app (never run; `com.spotify.campfire.datasource.impl.proto`): codename "campfire", backend **speechless**, `POST` on `spclient.wg.spotify.com` with protobuf bodies.
- Auth: WebPlayer bearer (no `client-token`). Must send `Content-Type: application/x-protobuf`; `application/protobuf` → HTTP 400 (observed 2026-10-26).
- `speechless/v1/retrieve/chats-share-set/latest`: `RetrieveLatestNodeRequest{1 sort_order, 2 limit}` → every chat with its latest message. `speechless/v1/retrieve/hierarchy`: `RetrieveNodeRequest{1 hierarchy_id, 5 sort_order, 8 page_token, 9 page_size}` → one chat's messages newest-first plus `next_page_token`.
- Shape: chat = `Hierarchy`; id = base62 part of `spotify:chat:<id>`; `hierarchy_metadata.hierarchy_type` DM/group; member usernames in `group.group_member[]` and an unnamed field 2; `hierarchy_statistic.total_user_unseen_events`. Message = `ClientNode` with `node_identifier.uri` (`spotify:chat-message:<id>`), `event_capture.init_username`, `event_capture.event_capture.occurred_at`, `message_payload`. Contributions: `tracked_share{uri}`, `op_tracked_share{uri, op_share_id, destination}`, `free_text{message_text}`, others. Reactions/receipts ("played") are `child_node`s of their message. URIs can carry `?si=`.
- Schema caveat: rebuilt from protobuf-lite `*_FIELD_NUMBER` constants; names/numbers reliable, some nested types not. `NodeIdentifier.node_class` arrives length-delimited, not a varint enum, so decoders fall back to generic decoding (`#<n>`) when the wire type contradicts the schema.
- Send: `POST speechless/v1/submit/request` with `ClientNodeRequest{1: NodeRequest{2: MessageRequest{1: AddContribution{1: chat id, 2: ContributionPayload{1: FreeText{1: text}}}}}}`. No node id, timestamp or digest (like the Android app); `ClientNodeResponse` echoes the created `ClientNode`, inserted directly. No reply reference for text. Probe: `spotify-web-client.py chat-send`.
- Seen: `ClientNodeRequest{1: NodeRequest{3: BulkMessageRequest{2: AddHierarchyActivity{1: chat id, 2: ActivityRequest{3: ack_type USER_SEEN=1}}}}}` marks the whole chat seen (probe `chat-seen`).
- Track metadata: spclient `GET metadata/4/track/<gid>?market=from_token` with the WebPlayer bearer; gid = base62 id as 32 hex digits. Returns name, artists, album, release date, duration and cover file ids (`https://i.scdn.co/image/<file_id>`), no preview. Probe `track-metadata`.
- Share metadata for albums, episodes, playlists: public oEmbed `GET https://open.spotify.com/oembed?url=<open.spotify.com URL>` (no auth; no artist). Senders via `user-profile-view/v3/profile/<username>`. Both best-effort, cached in memory, falling back to raw username and "shared a track"-style text.
- No realtime (Spotify's push channel unavailable), so everything polls.
- Not yet called: `retrieve/hierarchy/shares`, reactions/receipts via `submit/request`, group create/rename/add/remove/leave, mute/unmute, read-receipt settings, invites, `can-message-users`, `hierarchy/delete`. Mutations need probe validation first.

### Messages in the app
- `SpotifyMessagesSource` (registered in `AppContainer`): one `chats-share-set/latest` request per refresh (20 chats); a chat's latest message becomes a `.message` item when someone else sent it within 7 days and it's a share or text, regardless of seen state (product choice, same as Apron rooms). `SpotifyMessagesParser` is a minimal protobuf reader/writer for needed fields only. `SpotifyClient.makeRequest` POSTs `application/x-protobuf` bodies, keeping the 401/403 token-refresh retry. Needs the viewer's username (`metadataStore.spotifyAccount.username`); throws a service error if missing rather than guessing.
- Feed items: share `<name> shared “<title>”`, target URL `open.spotify.com/<type>/<id>` without `?si=`, artwork in `target.imageURL`; text `<name> sent a message` with the message as target text. Usernames are often random IDs, so the actor keeps username as `id`, `username` nil, profile name as `displayName`. Item ids `spotify:chat:<chatId>:<messageId>`.
- `SpotifyChatAdapter` (driven by `AdapterChatViewModel`): `retrieve/hierarchy` pages of 30, 10 s poll of the newest page while open, text sending, reactions read-only from child nodes. Seen sent once per newer incoming message that becomes fully visible. DM titles use the other member's profile name and hide sender names; groups use the group name. Keeps the opening title until the partner's profile name arrives; refreshes cached profiles once per visit.
- Chats list: `SpotifyMessagesSource.chatSummaries()` uses `chats-share-set/latest` with limit 50; DMs titled/pictured with the other member's profile; unread from `HierarchyStatistic.total_user_unseen_events` (`SpotifyChat.unseenCount`). No read cursor, so an opened chat reports 0 unread and an unread chat always waits for history before showing.
- Inline previews: `.audio` embed card with artwork and play/pause; clip URL via `ChatViewModel.audioPreviewURL(for:)` (`trackPreviewURL`, probe `track-preview`), cached per track. One `ChatAudioPreviewPlayer` per chat plays one preview at a time under `.playback`, stopping when the chat disappears; tapping elsewhere opens Spotify. Covered by `SpotifyMessagesParserTests`.
- Notification detail of a shared track plays the preview (`trackPreviewURL`); `NotificationDetailView` takes the `SpotifyClient` from `FeedView`.

### UI (stories)
- Story activity deduplicated by `userURI` (one bubble per user); seen state is `UserDefaults` timestamps per `userURI`, seen only if stored ≥ activity timestamp; green ring (`Color.spotifyActivityBorder`) unseen, gray seen.
- Viewer: progress starts once preview audio plays, advances at `AVPlayerItem` end.
- Album art rotation and the shared `SpotifyPulseRing` are timer-driven phase interpolation, not `.repeatForever`, so state changes don't restart/snap.
- Liked-song toggle is icon-only next to Open in Spotify, light haptic on success.

## GitHub

### Session & client
- Feed: authenticated HTML `GET /conduit/for_you_feed`, ephemeral cookie-disabled session, matching fetch nonces, ETag conditional requests. `If-None-Match` often returns 200 with a new ETag for unchanged content, so don't rely on 304 (observed 2026-08-29).
- Login WebView captures the full GitHub-domain cookie set (`GitHubCredentials.additionalCookies`), resolving duplicate names (`WKHTTPCookieStore` returns same-named cookies from multiple domains; a dictionary without a resolver crashed on `tz`). `GitHubClient` replays all except per-request `_gh_sess`.
- Session model (observed 2026-09-13): `user_session` has a two-week Max-Age, but server-side it stays active while accessed within 2 weeks, so replaying it works past cookie expiry. No refresh or re-mint: `saved_user_sessions` (`<user_id>:<session_key>`, ~3 months) alone doesn't authenticate, the cookie set minus `user_session` returns 401, and GitHub never re-sends `Set-Cookie: user_session=` on reload. Keep-alive = an authenticated request at least every ~2 weeks.
- `GitHubClient.refreshedSessionCookies(from:)` parses re-issued `user_session`/`__Host-user_session_same_site` into `GitHubForYouFeedResponse.refreshedUserSession`/`refreshedSameSiteUserSession`; both sources persist them through `GitHubCredentials.updated(from:accountUsername:)`. Foreground-only keep-alive (no background task, by choice), so 2+ weeks unopened means reconnect.
- Expired session: `/conduit/for_you_feed` 302 → `/login?return_to=…`, which `URLSession` follows to 200 login HTML (zero cards, no error). `GitHubClient.isLoginRedirect(_:)` (final URL path `/login`) makes `forYouFeed` and `authenticatedUser` throw `SourceError.notConfigured`; sources `markInvalid()` and rethrow so `GitHubConnectionView` shows "Log in to GitHub". A later successful feed load sets the account valid again (as Instagram does), so one 401/403 doesn't leave a working session flagged until reconnect. Covered by `GitHubClientTests`.
- `dotcom_user` capture is unreliable, so `GitHubClient.authenticatedUser(credentials:)` reads the login from the `https://github.com/` homepage's `<meta name="user-login" content="...">` (empty when logged out; GitHub dropped the earlier `"userMenu":{"owner":{"login":…}}` JSON, observed 2026-10-29, probe `whoami`); nil when logged out, `notConfigured` on 401/403. `SettingsViewModel.addGitHub` persists it when missing. Credentials stored without it are healed after each feed load: `GitHubCredentials.updated(from:accountUsername:)` (used by both sources with the re-issued cookies) fills it from the account's handle.
- `GitHubForYouFeedLoader` (built in `AppContainer`, shared by `GitHubNotificationSource` and `GitHubActivitySource`) fetches and parses once: concurrent callers with the same credentials share the in-flight request, and a result is reused for 10 s. Each source still persists refreshed cookies. Settings connection checks call `GitHubClient` and the parser directly.
- `GitHubClient.userPage(for:)` parses `https://github.com/<username>` (`og:description`, `tab=followers`/`tab=following` spans, `homeLocation` vcard, `nofollow me` link) → `GitHubNotificationSource.fetchProfile`. `GitHubClient.repoPage(for:)` returns `GitHubRepoDescription` from OpenGraph (`ogMeta`).

### Parser
- Uses machine-readable `data-hydro-view`/`data-hydro-click` JSON, not presentation text. Elements inside hidden `Details-content--hidden` subtrees (balanced div depth) are excluded so rollup cards emit only visible entries; the visible `sub_position == 0` entry defines the rollup performer for its card position.
- Kinds: `STARRED_REPOSITORY`, `FOLLOW`, `FORKED_REPOSITORY`, `CREATED_REPOSITORY` (`createdRepository`: actor is the creator, target the new repo; stories only). Unmapped kinds → `.unknown`, dropped by `normalize(_:)`.
- Repo card metadata is scoped per `<article>`: language `itemprop="programmingLanguage"`, stars `aria-label="N stargazers"`, description from the text node after the repo-name container (`[^<]*`, none → nil). Owner avatar `https://github.com/<owner>.png?size=192`.
- `formattedStarCount`: ≤3 significant figures with `k`/`m`/`b` (`1,234` → `1.23k`).
- Followed-user bio only from the card's `wb-break-all` container (`class="m-0 mt-1 wb-break-all"` → inner `<div>`), excluding the `color-fg-muted` handle/counts.
- Fork events carry only the fork. `GitHubClient.originalRepo(forFork:)` fetches the fork page, extracts `forked from <a href="/owner/repo">`, fetches the parent and parses `og:url`/`og:description` (strip trailing ` - owner/repo`)/`og:image`; falls back to the fork card.
- Hovercard `created_at` is bracket-encoded (`…created_at%5D=2026-08-29+02%3A14%3A51+-0700`); `createdTimestamp(in:)` decodes `%5B`/`%5D` and the regex accepts `created_at\]?=` (otherwise rows fell back to `Date()`).

### Stories & notifications
- Stories: activities grouped per actor, chronological; seen state keyed by actor ID + newest timestamp.
- `GitHubActivityParser.parse(_:viewerUsername:)` returns `GitHubFeedParseResult` (`storyGroups`, `notificationItems`): `STARRED_REPOSITORY` cards whose repo owner is the viewer become notifications. Stored credentials can lack `username` (seen on a signed-in simulator account, 2026-11-11), so when it is missing the viewer is the owner of the first star card whose `feed_card.gatherer` is `starred_viewer_repo` (GitHub's marker for a star of the viewer's repo); with neither, all stay stories. The probe's `notifications` infers it the same way (`viewer_from_feed`). `GitHubNotificationSource` maps them to `.reaction` (target = repo, actor id = starrer's username).
- Aggregated star cards: per-actor `NAME</a> starred` rows (no per-row repo link) pair with the owned repo the card names, deduped against record-level items.
- Rollups where one actor stars many of your repos: head card (`card_sub_position` 0) names the actor; sibling cards (sub_positions 1..N, broken "Uh oh!" body) each carry one repo without actor. `GitHubActivitySource.aggregatedYourRepositoryItems` propagates the head actor to siblings at the same `card_position`.
- Star notifications grouped per owned repo (`@x and N others starred your repository`), deduped by `(actor, repo)`; row preview is `targetName` (owner/repo).
- Star detail: target author is the viewer; "Repository" section rendered by `GitHubRepoDetailView` via `fetchTargetDetails`; `ProfileDetailView` doesn't hydrate GitHub actor profiles.

### UI
- Badge: `Resources/GitHubBadge.png` (16/32/48) from `github.githubassets.com/favicons/favicon.png`, registered in `stupid-app.yml` resources.

## Apron

### Protocol and server behavior
- Spec: PROTOCOL.md v9, cloned in ignored `third-party/apron/`.
- Demo server (`apron-cloudflare-demo/9`): capabilities `history, edit, rooms, reactions, command, activity, embed:upload, status, ext`; `auth: [token, guest]`, `signup: [token]`, `ping: 45`. Guest auth issues no token; each connection is a new `guest_NNNN` user auto-joined to `general`, read-only (`guest_posting: false`). Upload limit `ext.media.upload[mime].max_bytes` (5 MiB per image type) (observed 2026-10-25)
- Demo server limits: connections per account ("connection limit reached for this account"; free-tier Worker); typing 10/min for accounts (guests' typing isn't broadcast or limited); `ext.settings.read_cursors: true` with `activity`: a registered user's cursors are kept and sent back after their listings, a guest's dropped; other members' only in private rooms of at most 10 members (`read_receipts: "private"`) (observed 2026-11-01)
- `apron-server-cloudflare` (`handleRoomList`) free plan: `roomListRequestsPerUserMinute: 6` for `room_list` (first `filter: "joined"` listing after auth exempt; excess returns `retry_after`); 60 frames per connection per minute (observed 2026-10-26)
- The server's client-facing limits are in apron-server-cloudflare `docs/limits.md` (refusals, close codes, a well-behaved-client checklist). Validated with probe `limits`: `?apron_connection_status=1` answers `{"available": true}` with `Apron-Connection-Status: 1`; an exact `{"method":"ping"}` gets `{"method":"pong"}`; a guest's `you.ext.permissions` is `{"write": false}`; the 7th `room_list` in a minute fails `-32002 "Room listing limited"` with `data: {retry_after: 59, ext: {limits: "read"}}`. Pipelined `history` requests (3 and 12) were all answered, not refused as "already in progress" (observed 2026-10-30)
- Server metadata (PROTOCOL.md v9 §3.1): the `server` frame may carry `title` (plain), `description` (CommonMark) and `avatar` (same rules as user avatars, §4.8.6), and may change per connection, such as after sign-in. The demo sends `title: "Apron Demo"`, a one-line description and `avatar: https://web.apron.chat/icon-512.png` (probe `apron-web-client.py hello`) (observed 2026-10-29)
- Custom emoji (EXTENSIONS.md `ext:emoji`): the `server` frame lists `ext:emoji` and carries `ext.emoji.catalog_url`, a JSON object keyed `:name:` → `{url, static_url?, alt?, width?, height?, category?, tags?, hidden?}`. The demo's catalog is on `media.apron.chat` (`Cache-Control: immutable`; a new URL whenever it changes) with `:apron:` and `:SideTable:`, 512 and 1024 px PNGs, and is public before sign-in (probe `apron-web-client.py emoji --images --scan TEXT`, which also runs the spec's key scan) (observed 2026-10-29)
- server.apron.chat answers WebSocket upgrades at its demo cap with `503 {"error":"Demo capacity reached"}` + `Retry-After: 60` (probe `apron-web-client.py hello`) (observed 2026-10-29)
- Read cursor is `activity.read_message_id` (§4.6); servers MAY send a user's cursor after `room_list`; the Go reference server does and persists them. Who receives a read is server policy (§4.6): `apron-server-go` broadcasts it to the room and sends every member's cursor after a joined `room_list` (`cursorFramesLocked`); `apron-server-cloudflare` advertises `ext.settings.read_receipts: "private"` and does so only in private rooms of at most 10 members, keeping others to the reader's own connections. Probe `read-cursors` (observed 2026-10-31 on the local Go server: other members' cursors after `room_list`, and Bob's read reached Alice's open connection at once; observed 2026-11-01 on server.apron.chat: other members' cursors in DMs and small private rooms only). Server-side unread counts exist only in push payloads (§4.9.3). Guests can't use server cursors (new `user_id` per connection).
- `room_list` with `members: true` gives `members` as `{user_id}` stubs (no `member_count` on demo) plus full objects in `users` (§4.2.1). A message's `from` usually omits `avatar`; avatars and starting statuses come from those `users` (the sign-in `user` burst precedes the chat's observer).
- `seen` (protocol v9, shazow/apron `dc137a1`): time the shown status last became `offline`, in `log_id` format (ms), sent only with a shown `offline` Demo server doesn't send it yet (observed 2026-10-26); app support untested against a live server.
- Demo lists a DM as `{"room_id": "private_…", "private": true, "members": [...]}` with no title (observed 2026-10-26)
- Edits (§4.4, probe `apron-web-client.py edit`, observed 2026-10-31 on the demo): a `message` request with the `message_id` saves it; the server broadcasts a snapshot with a later `log_id` and `prev_log_id` before answering. A save replaces every client field, so `ApronChatAdapter.edit` sends the kept snapshot's body (format, embeds as decoded) with the new text and mentions from it, its room and bare `reply_to`, as apron-web's `editMessage` does; `ext` merges and isn't sent. Offered for own messages on servers with `edit`. Nothing marks a message as edited: servers also republish a message when an upload finishes or is removed (apron-server-go SERVER.md), so a later `log_id` isn't an edit, and apron-web shows no label either. A message's time is its `message_id` (the creation's `log_id`, §2.2), not the latest snapshot's
