- Push support: `apron-server-go` offers `relay` and `webpush` with every wake scope and any public https endpoint; registrations expire only when not renewed (30 days). `apron-server-cloudflare` (`server.apron.chat`) offers only `webpush`, wakes for `mentions`, `replies`, `private`, allows only hosts in `PUSH_HOSTS` (operator allowlisted `push.stupidtech.net`); registrations expire after 7 days. Neither has per-room wake scopes; room `mute` is the only per-room control, hence the proposed `room_wake` extension (observed 2026-10-26)
- The server pushes only after the app's connection closes; messages while the app is foreground get no push (observed 2026-10-27)
- Local Go server: refuses `--public-url` with `--email.sender log`; without a public URL upload `write_url` is `http://localhost/…` (no port). Run `logs/apron-local/aprond --public-url http://localhost:8090` without `--email.enable`, signing in with tokens (observed 2026-10-28)

### Connection and service
- `ApronConnection` (`URLSessionWebSocketTask`) waits for the `server` frame, matches replies by request `id` (registered before send, 15 s timeout), pings `{"method":"ping"}` at `server.ping`, fails pending requests on close. Liveness: a ping with no frame back by the next ping, or a ping that fails to send, closes the connection as `timedOut`, since a socket whose peer vanished (sleep, network change) otherwise looks open and still holds one of the account's 3 connection slots server-side. A server close maps to `ApronError.closedByServer(code:reason:)` from `task.closeCode`/`closeReason`.
- Rate limits: a `-32002` reply becomes `ApronError.limited(message:retryAfter:category:)` (`data.retry_after`, `data.ext.limits`). `request` waits out a `retry_after` of up to 5 s and resends with the same `id` (the server dedupes repeated ids for 24 h, so a mutation isn't applied twice), at most twice; longer ones fail to the caller. Multiple `observe(notifications:onClose:)` observers, removed by token.
- Refused upgrades: URLSession reports them as `NSURLErrorBadServerResponse`, so `receiveLoop` maps a failed receive whose `task.response` status isn't 101 to `ApronError.rejected(status:retryAfter:)` (with the `Retry-After` header): 503/429 read "too busy … (HTTP 503). Try again later."; others "refused the connection (HTTP n)". The body isn't exposed, so the server's error text isn't shown.
- `ApronConnection.joinedRooms()` caches the last joined-rooms listing (always `members: true`, shared by chat and Chats list), coalesces concurrent requests, drops it on `room_update` (generation bump discards in-flight older listings) — needed for the `room_list` rate limit.
- `ApronService` stores servers in UserDefaults `account.apron.servers` and per-server tokens in Keychain account `apron.<url>` (iCloud-synced). Sign-in uses `token` if saved and accepted, else `guest`; any `token` returned by `auth` replaces the saved one (§3.2.1). `denied` marks the server invalid; no fallback from rejected token to guest.
- Passkey and external sign-in (PROTOCOL.md §4.10.1, §4.12, `ext:auth_external`, recipes/native_passkeys.md): Add Account → Apron reads the `server` frame first (`ApronService.serverPreview`), and `ApronSignInOptions` turns `auth`, then signup-only schemes (`signup`, absent: `auth`), into `ApronSignInMethod`s: `webauthn` is Sign In with Passkey where it is in `auth` and Create Account with Passkey where it creates accounts; `external` is one button per `ext.auth_external.options` entry (only with the capability and two or more options, `prefer` sent with the begin) or one with `labels.external`; email is left out. The options are saved in `ApronSignInInfo.signIn`, so a server's settings offer passkey and external sign-in again (not account creation).
- `ApronService.signInOnPage`: begin on a fresh connection, open `external.url` in `ASWebAuthenticationSession` (`WebAuthenticationSession.openApronPage`) with the page's own query kept byte for byte (the demo's registration page carries `name=Sim+Passkey`, `+` for a space; re-encoding it as `%2B` made the account's name "Sim+Passkey") plus `response_type=code`, `client_id=tech.stupid.StupidSocial`, `redirect_uri=tech.stupid.stupidsocial:/apron/signed-in` (a scheme with a `.`, which servers accept, §4.12.3), PKCE S256, `state` and `resource` (the server's origin from the address connected to: `https://host[:port]`, `http://` for `ws://` on loopback), check `state`, then finish (on the begin's connection only after a guest sign-in, below; otherwise on a new one) with `code`, `code_verifier`, `redirect_uri`, `client_id` and keep the token. `error=access_denied` or closing the sheet is `ApronError.signInDeclined`, which shows nothing. Covered by `ApronPageSignInTests`.
- The app never runs WebAuthn itself: it has no `webcredentials:` associated domain for any server, and a URLSession WebSocket sends no `Origin`, so servers answer a passkey begin with `external` alone (the demo: `{"external": {"url": "https://server.apron.chat/passkey?action=login"}}`; aprond the same for a connection whose `Origin` isn't a `--webauthn.origin`). A begin without `external` is `ApronError.noSignInPage` (observed 2026-11-17, probe `auth-begin`).
- Create Account with Passkey signs in as a guest first when the server offers `guest`: aprond takes a registration only from a guest ("Sign in as a guest, or present an invitation, before adding a passkey"), whose account the passkey becomes, and its code then works only on that guest's connection; the demo takes a registration either way. So the begin connection is kept open while the sheet is up, and the finish goes on it: it keeps pinging at `server.ping` (aprond closes a connection after a missed ping, which broke a probe finish after a minutes-long wait). A login or external sign-in has no such connection, and the demo closes a connection that hasn't signed in 30 s after it opens, pings or not (the app's first login failed with "The connection to the Apron server closed."), so those finish on a new connection, as a code may (§4.10.1, §4.12.2). Validated end to end with probe `external-sign-in` against aprond (register with `--guest`, then login finished on a new connection; the token then resumes on a plain connection), in the simulator against aprond, and in the simulator against the demo: a new account by passkey, then Sign In with Passkey from its settings after 40 s on the page, and the demo's rooms in Chats (observed 2026-11-17). The demo's passkeys use RP ID `apron.chat`. The simulator needs enrolled Face ID for passkeys: the stupid-simulator skill's `scripts/biometrics.py --enroll`, and `--match` for each prompt. `ASWebAuthenticationSession` first asks whether the app may use the server's host to sign in.
- aprond offers its passkey page only while the request's origin is a `--webauthn.origin` (default `http://localhost:8080`), so a client must send the port in `Host`; the probe's WebSocket now does. External sign-in with `ext:auth_external` is implemented from the spec only: no server offers it yet.
- `ApronService.session(serverURL:)` returns one shared signed-in connection per server, sharing one pending sign-in, reopened on demand; users only observe it and never close it. Each sign-in saves the connection's latest `server` frame (`title`, `description`, `avatar`, `agent`, `welcome`) to `accounts.apronInfo`, and a later `server` frame on the shared connection saves again, since each one replaces the last (§3.1); `setApronInfo` posts `statusDidChangeNotification` when it changes.
- Server names: `ApronServerInfo.displayTitle(serverURL:)` / `ApronServerMetadata.displayTitle` are the `title`, or the host without one; servers are still identified only by URL (§1). The title labels the Settings row (with the server's icon when it has one), the server detail screen (whose Server row always shows the address, and an About section renders `description`), Chats row and chat subtitles, failed-connection labels, notification subtitles (`ApronPushRegistration.serverTitle`, set on registration and room-title updates) and a saved server in the Apron link sheet, which always shows the address too so a copied title can't pass for another server (§6). `closeSessions` closes on background (via `ApronLiveFeed.stop()`), server removal, or token change.
- `ApronLiveFeed` keeps the shared connection per server while the app is active. Initial connect and a `room_update` with `joined` or `left` (the user's rooms changed) rebuild every room from history (`ChatListViewModel.replaceChats(where: isApronRoom(on:))`) and update push room titles; any other `room_update` rebuilds only the rooms its `updated` and `memberships` name (`ApronRoomUpdate.changedRoomIds`), so someone joining or leaving costs one page, not one per room; reactions, edits, deletions and moves (a `log_id` later than the `message_id`) rebuild only their room (`chatSummaries(in:roomIds:)`), debounced 800 ms. A new message (`log_id == message_id`, §4.3.1) updates its room's row from the frame alone (`ApronNotificationSource.summary(_:adding:session:)`: preview, time, unread +1 when from someone else after the read cursor), and an own `activity` read rebuilds its room only while the row still shows unread. Reconnect backoff 1 s doubling to 60 s with ±50% jitter, reset only after a connection stayed up 2 minutes (a connect-then-close loop would otherwise retry every second and spend the 12 admissions a minute), and never sooner than `ApronError.reconnectDelay`: `Retry-After`, `retry_after`, 5 minutes after a 1013 close, 60 s after a rate-limit 1008. A `denied` sign-in stops the loop; so does a 1008 close about the account ("Removed by an admin", "Device token replaced", "Bot token replaced", "Signed out…", `endsSignIn`), which marks the server's account invalid (`ApronService.signInEnded`).
- Servers without `history` (archives served read-only, scripted scenes such as `wss://scene.apron.chat/piper`, `apron-scene/1`): `history` answers `-32601 Unsupported method`, and the server pushes its whole log as `message`/`reactions` frames within about a second of `auth`, then nothing more (observed 2026-11-07, probe `apron-web-client.py listen`). That burst lands before any chat observes the shared connection, so `ApronConnection` keeps those two kinds of frames from the start on such servers (newest 5,000, `keptRecordLimit`), as apron-web's per-server store does (its `loadRoom` sends nothing without `history`). `keptPage(roomId:)` shapes them like a history page: each message's latest snapshot still in the room (a later one elsewhere is a move) and the room's reaction records. `ApronChatAdapter.load` applies it in the same main-actor turn it starts observing, so no frame is missed; the Chats list builds rows from it. Covered by `ApronKeptRecordsTests`.
- History pages are scarce: the public demo allows 1,000 per user per UTC day (2,000 per IP, 10 a minute), shared by every device signed in as that user, simulators included; past it every `history` fails with "Daily history limit reached" until UTC midnight (observed 2026-10-30). One page per room per Chats reload and one per live message used to exhaust it. So once the live connection has built a server's rows, `ApronNotificationSource.chatList()` reports that server's account `isCurrent` (`keepsRowsCurrent`, set by `ApronLiveFeed`) and the list keeps its rows instead of reading every room again; a reload reads history only for a server without a built live connection. `scripts/apron-web-client.py listen` labels live `message` frames new, edit, delete or transient.

### Chats list and unread
- `ApronNotificationSource.chatSummaries` lists every `joined` room on every server (with `members: true` for avatars), each with its latest message (preview "You: …" or "<name>: …") however old. The row title reads "<server> / <room>": `ChatSummary.titlePrefix` = server title (or host), drawn in the secondary style and matched by search; an empty room shows "No messages yet". Servers without `rooms` get their default room from history without `room_id` (§3.4); servers without `history` build each row from `ApronConnection.keptPage(roomId:)` (see Connection and service), and without `rooms` either produce none. Short-lived reads also store server cursors sent after `room_list`.
- A room with one other member shows that person's avatar; every other room shows the server's `avatar` (§3.1; rooms have none of their own), from the latest `server` frame, or for a row resolved before the list loads, from the saved server metadata. The chat title shows the row's picture (`showRowAvatar`), so rooms show the server's there too.
- `ApronRoom.displayTitle(users:you:)` follows apron-web: untitled top-level private room = other members' names sorted, joined ", "; "You" when nobody else; "Private room" without a member list; other rooms use title or `room_id`. Covered by `ApronModelsTests`.
- `ApronReadStore` keeps a cursor per server/room. If `ApronService.serverTracksCursors(session)` (server has `activity`, doesn't set `ext.settings.read_cursors: false`, signed in with token), server cursors are truth, captured from our own `activity` frames, cached even when moving backward. Otherwise cursors live on device and iCloud KVS (`com.stupid.apron.readcursors`), merged per room with `max`. All `user_id`s ever had on a server are remembered (`ownUserIds`) so own guest messages never count. A never-seen room starts with its cursor at its newest message (no flood). Server-as-truth path untested. Both cursor dictionaries are decoded once and kept in memory (dropped on `UserDefaults.didChangeNotification`): chats ask for the cursor per message on every scroll frame, and decoding the defaults each time took over half the main thread while scrolling an unread room and most of the time spent taking in live messages (observed 2026-11-20).
- Unread reads a 50-message page per room; `unreadCount` = messages after cursor that aren't deleted, system or own, capped at `unreadCap` 50 ("50+").
- Muted rooms (connection's `mutedRooms`, level None) show unread counts on a gray badge and a muted icon after the title (`ChatSummary.isMuted`).

### Per-room notification levels
- Room details at the top of the member list (`ApronMembersView` with `ChatDetailsHeader`): the chat's title, the other person's avatar in a DM (initials otherwise; Apron rooms have no picture), the member count and the room's `description`. Edit (`ChatDetailsEditView`) shows while connected for any room but a direct one: a title would stop `ApronRoom.isDirect` finding it. It sends `room_set {room_id, title?, description?}` (`ApronRoomEditParams`): omitted client fields are cleared, `private`/`parent_room_id` are kept and `ext` merges, so only the two fields go, empty ones left out. No permission is advertised (the spec's convention: members may edit; the Go server lets any signed-in user edit a room they can see), so a refusal shows under the form. The edit comes back as `room_update` `updated`, which the adapter applies to the title and description. Validated on the local Go server with probe `room-set` (observed 2026-11-01)
- In the member list: private rooms All Messages / None (default All), public rooms Mentions Only / None (default Mentions). None stores `room_mutes` server-side (comes back in sign-in `status` frames).

### Chat behavior (view model)
- UIKit chat (`UICollectionView` + diffable source) chosen over SwiftUI `LazyVStack` after a 1,000-message benchmark: ~half the first-frame time and CPU, ~4× less scroll hitching.
- `ApronChatAdapter` stores snapshots by greatest `log_id` (tombstone wins ties, §2.3, as the record version), orders by `message_id`, pages earlier with `before = first_log_id - 1`, joins the room if not in `joined`. `/`-prefixed text is sent as `command` when supported; `//` escapes (§4.1). Messages go out as `format: markdown`; Markdown is inline-only via `AttributedString`; raw HTML never rendered.
- Moves (§4.3.1, §4.4): a snapshot whose `room_id` is another room, in this room's history or live, means the message moved out, and its stored copy is deleted. A delete sent with the wrong `room_id` is such a move: Muse Bot's deletes on the demo carried `room_id: "general"`, so the tombstones landed in #general with `prev_room_id` the source room (observed 2026-11-03), and history-only filtering by `room_id` had left those messages showing
- Replies: `send()` adds bare `reply_to: {message_id}` (§3.5.4). Unloaded reply targets are fetched alone with `history` where `after` == `before` == id (§4.3.1), in parallel (~0.2 s each), and the first load waits for them. Missing targets show "Message unavailable" (retention, e.g. Bun server keeps 1,000 records).
- `jump(to:)`: a stored target shows in a window around it; otherwise the adapter fetches `history(before: id, limit: 30)` + `history(after: id + 1, limit: 30)` as one page around it (`ChatWindowing`), stored as its own chunk. `more` per direction becomes the chunk's cursors. Live messages go to the newest chunk, not the window. `jumpToLatest()` returns to the newest chunk.
- Unread: cursor at open places a "New Messages" divider above the first unread from someone else for the whole visit; room opens with divider at top, or at newest when nothing unread. When every stored message is unread the chat opens on up to four more stored pages back; beyond that it opens on the newest page with the divider at the top, and scrolling up fills the gap. Messages are marked read only when fully on screen while visible and app active, plus on send; `activity.read_message_id` sent as soon as the cursor moves, only when it advances. `reportVisibleRead` waits for `.connected` and runs once on connecting, so cached messages aren't marked read early.
- Reconnects: the shared connection closes silently on background; state returns to connecting and the chat rejoins on `didBecomeActiveNotification`. A reconnect raises the account's sync epoch and fetches the newest page, which joins stored history or starts a newer chunk above a gap; the divider applies only to a visit's first load.
- Reactions: per-user state messageId → userId → (log_id, emojis), greatest `log_id` wins; Toggling sends your complete new set with `reactions` (§4.7). Controls only when the server advertises `reactions`. `reactors(of:on:)` names reactors from kept user objects, falling back to the recorded `from` of their reaction set (§3.3.1). Write path not verified live.
- Unseen reactions (`ChatUnseenReactions`): Apron has no seen state for reactions (§4.7), so `ApronReactionStore` keeps it. Only reactions from the last `unseenWindow` (3 days, by their `log_id`) to the user's newest `messageLimit` (1000) messages across every room and server can be unseen (message ids are millisecond log ids, so they order across servers). The message limit is what bounds the synced state; the window keeps old reactions from badging after a long absence. Per device: each room's baseline (its newest log position when first shown, so joining doesn't flood) and the eligible message keys (`<server>|<room>|<message id>`), which the chat and the Chats row register as they see the user's messages. Synced: per message the newest reaction seen, kept for the newest 1000 marked messages (marks are only made on the user's messages, so these cover every eligible one), as one JSON value under iCloud KVS key `com.stupid.apron.reactionmarks`, about 70 bytes a mark, so at most ~70 KB of KVS's shared 1 MB; merged per mark with `max`. The state is read from UserDefaults (`apronReactionState`) once and cached, since every chat refresh and row reads it. A change from another device posts `remoteDidChangeNotification` per room: an open chat republishes its unseen reactions, and `ApronLiveFeed` rebuilds that room's row (one history page) only when it still shows unseen reactions, since another device can only clear them. The earlier per-room store (`apronReactionsSeen`) isn't migrated: rooms take a new baseline when next shown, so reactions from before then count as seen
- Ownership ("includes you" for reactions, `isOwn` for messages) uses every identity the user has had on the server (`ApronReadStore.ownUserIds` + `you`), so it's right from the first cached frame; toggling still sends the current identity's set.
- Read-only guests: `ApronUser.canWrite` is `you.ext.permissions.write`; when it is `false` the adapter's `ChatReadOnlyAccess.isReadOnly` is true, which turns off attachments and reactions, and the composer reads "Read only" and can't be edited or sent. Text over 4,096 UTF-8 bytes (`ApronConnection.maxTextBytes`, SPEC §6; the server frame doesn't advertise it) is refused before sending, since three `too_large` refusals in a minute close the connection. Quoted messages outside the loaded ones are fetched four at a time, under the server's 8-unanswered-requests close.
- Typing (§4.6), following apron-web (`sendTyping`/`composerInput`): announce `typing: 15`, refresh at most every 12 s, `typing: 0` after a 5 s input pause; sending a message only clears local state; commands send an explicit stop; skip a stop if the indication lapses within 2 s. Budget of 7 typing frames per rolling minute per server shared across rooms; excess dropped. Incoming typing ends on expiry, `0`, or a message from that user.
- Drafts use the shared `ChatDraftStore` (see Shared chat architecture); removing a server removes drafts whose `chatKey` starts with `apron:<server>|`; replies/attachments not kept.
- Members: `ApronRoom` decodes `members` and `member_count`. Set starts from `room_list members: true` (or `room_update.joined`), then live `room_update.memberships` (§4.2.2). `member_count` not adjusted live; `members` is `nil` without `rooms`.
- Presence: `ApronUser` decodes `status` (§4.5); Live `user` (`new`) notifications merge into kept objects (§3.3.1). Own entry follows `you.status` (`invisible`/`""` → offline), since the server only sends your own setting. `ApronUser.lastSeen` returns `seen` only while `offline`; decoded, merged and cached.
- `ApronUser` has hand-written `Codable`: free-form `ext`, only `ext.bio` read; an odd value never drops the user.
- Mentions: regex `ApronMention.pattern` (core) follows Appendix A.3 (no letter/digit before `@`, so emails aren't mentions), never inside code spans; a link wins over a mention inside it. Linked by text, not only when in `body.mentions`. `mentionNames` maps `user_id` → name, omitting names shared by another known user (§3.3.1) and unknown users (keep `@user_id`); tap target is always the `user_id`.
- Sending fills `body.mentions` with known users written as `@user_id` (§3.5.5). `@` autocomplete (up to 5; prefix matches on name or `user_id` first, then name words; empty query lists online first) inserts `@user_id `.

### Chat UI (UIKit, shared `ChatUIKitView`)
- Message buttons (`ChatMessage.buttons`): rows under the bubble, as wide as it, each button an equal share of its row, and the widest row widens a narrow bubble. `.link` asks before opening, `.copy` copies with a "Copied" toast, `.press` shows a spinner until the network answers (`ChatButtonPressing`), `.unsupported` is dimmed. Whether a button works is asked on tap, since snapshot cells are configured before the chat connects.
- Title pill (room + host) opens `ApronMembersView`.
- DMs (named for members, one other member) set `ChatInfo.avatarUserId`/`avatarURL` and `ChatSummary.avatarColorKey`, so the title pill and Chats row show the person's picture, else their initials on `ChatUserColor` like `ApronUserAvatar` (shared `ChatAvatarImage`; rows without a color key keep the gray initial). A live `user` update without `avatar` keeps the old picture (`ApronUser.merging`) until the next connection.
- Profile Message button (`ApronRoomViewModel.canMessage`/`directRoom(with:)`): a centered circular icon button in the profile header (a spinner while the room opens, errors in a line beneath), not a list row; hidden for yourself, without `rooms`, and inside the DM with that user. It looks the room up with `existingDirectRoom(with:you:)` (a `room_list` read) and opens it as `.apron`; without one it opens `ChatSummary.Destination.apronDirect(serverURL:user:)`, an `ApronChatAdapter` made with `directWith:` that has no room yet (`roomId` empty, `directUser` set). It shows just the two of you, loads no history and sends no typing or notification changes; the first `send` or `sendCommand` starts the room through `directRoom(with:you:)` (once, however many sends wait on it), then takes its id, reads its members from `room_list` (the `joined` record arrived before the id was known) and carries on as that room, and `ApronChatView` moves its open-room mark to it. Its chat key is `apron:direct:<user id>`, so an unsent draft is kept per person, and it saves no state, which would stay keyed by the person rather than the room. `ApronConnection.directRoom(with:you:)` follows apron-web's `directRoomWith`/`startPrivateRoom`: reuses a joined room where `ApronRoom.isDirect` (named for members, complete member list without `member_count`, exactly you and them); otherwise `room_set {private: true}`, waits up to 10 s for its `room_update` `joined` record, refuses to use it without `private: true` (§4.2.4), then `room_join {room_id, user_id}` (§4.2.2). `unsupported` reads "This server doesn't have private rooms." The room opens through `NotificationRouter.pendingChat`, which closes the stack back to Chats like a notification tap. Validated on the local Go server with probe `dm` (observed 2026-10-29); the `room_update` `joined` record arrives before the `room_set` result there.
- One-to-one rooms (named for members, one other member) show that member's presence in the title subtitle instead of the server, and "last seen 5 minutes ago" for an offline user with `seen` (§4.5.2); recomputed every 30 s while connected (no poll).
- Attachments: the kit's attachment sheet (recent photos from PhotoKit, a grid at full height, Camera where there is one, Files, and All Photos for the system picker; JPEG ≤2048 px, GIFs kept). It needs `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` and `NSCameraUsageDescription` in `Info.plist`, without which iOS ends the app when the sheet opens. Each staged attachment is its own message, sent as an `upload` embed (§4.8.4); the `message`/`command` result's `embeds[0].write_url` gets an HTTP `PUT` with the file MIME type. Disabled unless `embed:upload`; enforces `ext.media.upload[mime].max_bytes`. Upload write path not verified end to end.
- Embeds: `og.image` from the chat server host or an `ext.media.origins` host (demo `media.apron.chat`) renders as image sized from `og.image` width/height: an `upload` alone, a link preview as a `.linkPreview` (`og.title`, `og.description`, the image opening the viewer, the text opening `url`); else a card opening http(s) `url` (§3.5.2 fallback).
- Bubbles (`ChatMessageCell`): sender group = consecutive messages from one `user_id` < 10 min apart; Per-user color `ChatUserColor` = sum of `user_id` Unicode scalars indexed into seven system colors (`hashValue` is seeded per launch). Bubble width is computed in `configure` capped at 80% of the row, applied as a priority-999 constraint — Auto Layout fitting kept newly inserted cells at max width. `ChatUIKitView` remembers the width it configured messages at and reconfigures them all when the collection view's width changes (`reconfigureForWidthIfNeeded`): a message configured before the chat had its width got a negative width and collapsed to its time, wrapping its text a few characters per line. The body label's `preferredMaxLayoutWidth` is set from that width (minus the 24 pt of padding): left automatic, a multi-line label first wraps at its previous width, so cells scrolling into view measured many lines too tall and then shrank, shifting the messages around them. The buttons stack under the bubble has a zero-height constraint while empty: an empty `UIStackView` has no height of its own and stretched every cell to its estimated height. Layout jumps: log `preferredLayoutAttributesFitting` estimated vs fitted height per index path; a cell measured twice with different heights is the bug.
- Swipe to reply begins only for leftward mostly-horizontal drags (|vx| > 1.2·|vy|) so scrolling and nav back-swipe work.
- Custom emoji (`ext:emoji`, framework ability `ChatCustomEmojis`): `ApronChatAdapter` follows the latest `server` frame's `catalog_url` while it lists `ext:emoji` (a new `server` frame mid-connection reloads it), and keeps the URL in its saved state so stored messages render emoji before connecting. `ApronService.emojiCatalog(url:)` fetches each catalog once per URL (spec: keep it while the URL is unchanged); a failed fetch is retried on the next connection or `server` frame. `ApronEmojiCatalog.parse` drops entries with a key outside `:[A-Za-z0-9_+-]+:` or no usable `url` (avatar rules, §4.8.6) and defaults mistyped fields. Covered by `ApronEmojiTests`.
- Custom emoji in the UI: `ChatCustomEmoji.ranges(in:catalog:)` is the spec's left-to-right scan (`:a::b:` is two keys, `12:30:a:` one). Message text gets `NSTextAttachment`s the font's line height (`descender` baseline), after links and mentions and skipping their ranges and code spans; until an image loads the attachment is a blank of its size, so text doesn't reflow, and loads re-render messages with a `:` or key reactions once per run loop. Reaction chips put the image in the button configuration (22 pt). The More Reactions sheet lists non-hidden emoji by `category` (uncategorized as "Custom") before the common emoji, as centered attachments. Typing `:` at the start of a word shows a sideways-scrolling strip of matching emoji above the composer, in every chat (`ChatViewModel.emojiSuggestions`): custom emoji first (all for a bare `:`, else name prefix, then names and tags containing the query), then from two characters Unicode emoji by name (`UnicodeEmojiSearch`: whole name, whole word, name prefix, word prefix; `_` counts as a space). Tapping inserts `:key: ` or the emoji and a space. The Unicode names come from the standard library's scalar properties (`isEmoji`, `name`), so no emoji data ships: single code point emoji only (no flags, skin tones or ZWJ sequences), text-style ones with U+FE0F, and Unicode's names rather than Slack shortcodes (`:joy` finds 😂, `:+1` finds nothing). Covered by `ChatEmojiSearchTests`. Images load through `ChatCustomEmojiImages`: the still `static_url` when there is one (chats don't animate emoji), downsampled to 132 px off the main thread, since the demo's are up to 1024 px. Keys the catalog doesn't have show as text, including in reactions.
- Long-pressing a reaction chip lists who reacted (oldest first by set `log_id`, "You" for any own identity); menu avatars come only from `StoryImageCache`, so the chat prefetches reactors' avatars when configuring cells.
- Scroll anchoring: bottom scrolls repeat scroll → layout → measure up to 4 passes. `isPinnedToBottom` (changed only by user drag or jumps) re-anchors on growth, inset and bounds changes; `isAnchoredToDivider` keeps the divider at top while the room opens. A chat shorter than the screen sits just above the composer, as in Messages: `updateTopInsetForShortContent` pads `contentInset.top` by the empty space, on layout and on `contentSize` changes (KVO). `applySnapshot` anchors to an unread divider appearing in that snapshot (unless the user scrolled); a separate `$firstUnreadMessageId` subscription was removed because its animated scroll-to-bottom completion snapped past the divider.
- Jump-to-latest shows for an older window or when not near the bottom. `isNearBottom` is computed from the current layout, not stored from `scrollViewDidScroll`: a list that fits on screen never scrolls, so the stored value stayed the one from before the view had its size (false) and short chats showed the button. `scrollToDivider` and the non-animated `scrollToBottom` set `isRepositioning`, so their passes don't update the button from `scrollViewDidScroll`; they update it once they settle. A pass made while cells are still being measured can land short of the bottom: on a cold open with one unread message the button flashed while the list settled at the divider, which was also the bottom.
- Links: stored under custom `.chatLink` (not `.link`, which `UILabel` restyles), covering Markdown links, `NSDataDetector` bare URLs (code spans skipped) and bare `apron:?…` links (regex; `NSDataDetector` misses them). `ChatLinkLabel` lays text out with TextKit to find the tapped character; only `http`, `https`, `mailto` open (untrusted text). `.chatMention` runs report taps via `Actions.showMentionedUser`.
- Gotcha: overriding `UIView.gestureRecognizerShouldBegin` on a label is also consulted for ancestors' recognizers (incl. the collection view pan); returning `false` off-link blocked scrolling. Gate only the label's own tap and defer to `super` otherwise.
- Image viewer (`ChatImageViewerController`): Save downloads original bytes (keeps GIFs/full res), `PHAssetCreationRequest` after `.addOnly` auth. The `performChanges` block must live in a `nonisolated static` helper: inside the controller it inherited main-actor isolation and crashed with `_dispatch_assert_queue_fail`.
- `ApronMembersView` and `ApronProfileView` (shared `ApronUserAvatar`, `ApronUserTags`) show "Last seen …" for offline users (never your own). Profile opens from member rows, sender avatars (`Actions.showProfile`, `navigationDestination(item:)`) and mentions (bare `ApronUser(userId:)` filled via `currentUser(_:)`). The chat's `onDisappear` keeps its observer while members/profile are on top so they get live statuses.

### Message storage
- Records are `ApronMessage` snapshots keyed by `message_id`, version `log_id × 2` (+1 for a tombstone, so it wins a tie, §2.3), and each user's reaction set on a message as a record related to it (`reaction:<message>:<user>`). Pages are complete only by tombstones (`isComplete: false`) and cover their `first_log_id…last_log_id`, since a page can carry older messages edited within it. A snapshot in another room deletes the stored copy (a move, §4.3.1). Cursors: `before` and `after` are inclusive, so earlier history is `before: first − 1`.
- The adapter's saved state is the authors' and reactors' user objects, media origins (so cached image embeds render as images before the session is up), the emoji catalog URL and others' read cursor. Removing a server removes its stored chats and drafts.
- The live feed (`ApronLiveFeed`) writes every room's frames into the store and marks the server's account followed, so rooms stay current without history pages, which servers rate-limit.

### iCloud server sync
- Each server is an account in `AccountStore` (id `apron.<server URL>`, see Accounts), which replaced `ApronServerSync` and its `com.stupid.apron.servers` key. Tokens sync via iCloud Keychain (`saveApronToken`, synchronizable, item `apron.<server URL>` = the account id), read cursors via iCloud KVS.
- `ApronService.servers` is built from the Apron accounts plus this device's sign-in info and status. On `AccountStore.didChangeNotification` it compares server sets: a server removed elsewhere drops local cursors, cache and drafts but leaves the token to Keychain sync; servers added elsewhere sign in on first use with the Keychain token if present. Changes post `serversDidChangeNotification`.
- Loopback servers (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`) aren't synced (they'd point at the other device).
- `apronOwnUserIds` syncs via KVS (`com.stupid.apron.ownusers`) as a per-server union, so guest messages sent from another device render as your own. Two-device sync untested.

### Threads and moved messages
- A thread is a room with `parent_room_id` (PROTOCOL.md v10 §4.2); messages move into it as saves with its `room_id` (§4.4). The move snapshot keeps the message's `message_id` (its send time), gets a new `log_id` in the destination and carries `prev_room_id`; the source room's history gets the same snapshot under the destination's `room_id`. On the demo, apron-web's retroactive thread created the thread (`room_id` = `log_id` = `history_log_id`, a log id), then moved 46 messages one save each, not in `message_id` order, each with its reactions re-logged in the thread; later edits of a moved message don't carry `prev_room_id` (observed 2026-11-18, probes `rooms --parent-room-id` and `history`).
- Stored records sort by `message_id` while history pages by `log_id`, so moved messages fall outside a thread page's covered range, and the kit stored records outside it only as updates: the thread opened with "no history". The adapter now names them in `ChatPage.movedIn` (kit 0.9.9): `prev_room_id` from another room, or a `message_id` below the page's `history_log_id` (only a move can put one there, covering later edits). Live move frames go through `ChatHistory.write(_:movedIn:)`. A message moved out (a snapshot in another room) is deleted from the room's store, as before.
- Catching up: a Chats row names its room's latest message, which moves and edits don't change, so a room's store counted as current and kept moved-out messages. The adapter keeps a checkpoint (the newest `log_id` its pages and live records brought, saved in its state); when the joined listing's `latest_log_id` is past it, it reads the log forward from it (5 pages at most, as recovery in §4.3.3), applying each page as live records, a second after connecting unless a newest fetch moved the checkpoint first. Without a checkpoint (stores from before it) it reads the newest pages back instead.
- Thread cards: `ApronChatAdapter.threads` holds this room's threads from the joined listing, `room_update` `joined`/`updated` (thread record changes reach the parent's members, §4.2.3) and one `room_list {parent_room_id, filter: "all"}` per connection once the chat is first rendered (not for chats loaded ahead: `room_list` is limited to 6 a minute). `render` merges a card per thread at its `room_id` (when a log id, as apron-web anchors them) among the shown messages from the oldest on: a system notice with a `.card` (title, else "Untitled"; the description's first line; `bubble.left.and.text.bubble.right`; the thread's link), which the kit draws as a tappable centered caption. The link opens a joined thread directly and offers Join in the link sheet otherwise. A thread left keeps its card. No reply count or latest message: the card would need the thread's history.
- Chats rows of threads are prefixed "<server> / <parent title>" when the parent is joined.
- Not yet: starting a thread, moving messages, and a thread's parent shown inside the thread.

### Apron links
- `ApronLink` parses `https://link.apron.chat/#…` and `apron:?…` (Appendix A.4, link.apron.chat `SPEC.md`): form-decoded keys (bare `+` = space), first value of a repeated key wins, unknown keys ignored; for the web form, query keys count after the fragment's. A `token` in the query is refused (link.apron.chat already received it). `server` must be `wss`, or `ws` on loopback, no user info/fragment; empty path becomes `/` to match saved servers; other transports (e.g. `webrtc`) fail naming the transport. `message` positive decimal < 2^53, requires `room`. A link without `server` is valid only in content from an Apron server (resolves against that chat's server). `webURL` writes the web form (SPEC.md §4 encoding, never a token). Covered by `ApronLinkTests`.
- Places in message text follow apron-web's docs/behavior.md ("Links as chips", "Link cards", and `#room_id` under rendered bodies), simplified. `ApronLink.place` reads a link strictly: only `server`, `room`, `message`, `user`, each once as `key=value` with a value that decodes, shapes `room` / `room`+`message` / `room`+`user`, no token, no query on the web form; anything else stays a plain URL so nothing malformed looks official. On this server, a room link written as its URL reads `#<room name>` and a member link `@name`, drawn like mentions (link color, underlined on your own bubbles); links to other servers stay URLs. A message link stays a link, with the message as a card under the bubble (`ChatEmbed.message`, drawn with `ChatReplyQuoteView` like a reply quote: `"<sender> in #<room>"`, then its text, three lines until tapped open in place and tapped again to close; the name opens the message; at most three, never one to the message itself) when it can be read: on this server, or another server the user has an account on (its session; nothing signs in anywhere new). The message is fetched once with `history` `after`=`before`=its id (validated with `apron-web-client.py history … --after ID --before ID --limit 1`); `from` carries no display name there, so the sender comes from that server's user list. A deleted or unreadable message gets no card. `#room_id` (ID grammar of Appendix A.3: letters, digits, `_.+@:-`, ending in a letter, digit or `_`, not after a letter or digit) shows `#<title>` for a joined room and opens it; unknown rooms stay as written. Rooms come from the joined-rooms listing the chat already loads. Typing `#` in the composer offers joined rooms and threads (`ChatLinking.referencePrefix`/`referenceSuggestions`), matched by id, title or a word of the title, a bare `#` listing them by name; a pick inserts `#room_id`. Rooms named for their members (direct and group messages) aren't offered
- These reach the shared renderer through `ChatLinking` (`placeLabel(forLink:)`, `referencePattern`, `placeLabel(forReference:)`, returning `ChatPlaceLabel`), applied after link detection and before `@` mentions; a label keeps the link's URL, so a tap opens it as before (`NotificationRouter.open`, which opens a joined room directly)
- Entry points: `apron` URL scheme, universal links, and taps in chat text, link cards and image embeds; `NotificationRouter.open(_:contentServer:)` is the single entry point. A link to a room already in the Chats list on a saved server opens directly; otherwise `ApronLinkView`/`ApronLinkViewModel` (punycode host, escaped hidden characters) acts only on tap (join room, sign in with a link token via `addServer`/`updateToken`). A link to a new server reads its `server` frame as the sheet opens (`ApronLinkViewModel.loadServer`) and offers its sign-in methods (`ApronSignInChoices`, shared with Add Account → Apron) in place of one guest button, then opens the link's room once signed in. That contacts a server the user hasn't used before they confirm, which PROTOCOL.md A.4 advises against; it was chosen so the sheet can offer passkeys, and the sheet keeps its "Only add servers you trust" note. A link to a new server doesn't wait for the Chats list to load (`routeWaitingLink`): its room can't be joined yet, and with no accounts the list first loads only once Chats is shown, so links used to show nothing until then. The app never makes email/external proposals, so every sign-in link needs confirmation.
- `ApronLinkView` is laid out like a group invite: a sheet fitted to its content (measured `.height` detent), close button, large avatar (a room's initial with the server's icon, or Apron's, as a badge), title, subtitle, the server address under them (always, so a copied title can't pass for another server), optional description and member faces, at most one note, and one prominent button (for a new server, its sign-in methods: the ways to sign in and create an account, passkeys and external sign-in, the first prominent and the others bordered; a token and guest access behind an Other button, or shown at once when a server offers nothing else; a token typed only after choosing it; and the server's `welcome` above them in `ApronWelcomeText`, a 150 pt box that scrolls, with paragraphs and `-`/`*`/`+`/numbered list items laid out and each line's inline markdown rendered, since SwiftUI's `Text` shows block markdown such as lists as plain text). Add Account → Apron takes only the address (Continue, or return in the field), then replaces the form with the server shown the same way (`ApronServerCard`, welcome, choices); coming back to the screen starts again from the address. It requests no display name, so the server assigns one. Spinners in these buttons and rows are overlays on the hidden label, so loading never changes a control's size (AGENTS.md). Two text sizes only: `.title2` for the title and `.subheadline` for the rest. Notes are limited to a private/missing room, a failed preview, or the trust warnings for sign-in and new servers.
- Room preview: on a saved server only, `ApronLinkViewModel.loadPreview()` lists the one room on the shared session (`ApronConnection.room(roomId:)`: `room_list` with `filter: "all"`, `members: true`, `room_id`) for title, description, member count and faces. A server the user hasn't used isn't contacted before confirming (PROTOCOL.md A.4), so its links show the link's IDs. The demo returns an unlisted room in `not_joined` with full `members` and `users`, and answers an unknown (or invisible private) room with `-32602 Unknown room`, shown as private or missing (probe `apron-web-client.py rooms --members --room-id <id>`) (observed 2026-10-29)
- `NotificationRouter` holds an opened link until the Chats list has loaded (`hasLoaded`), since that list says which rooms are joined; otherwise a cold launch offered "Join Room" for a joined room.
- Linked message: `AdapterChatViewModel.focusMessageId` runs `jump(to:)` after first load; linked member opens `ApronChatView(profileUserId:)`; a link to the open room acts in place. Focus travels as `NotificationRouter.pendingFocus` beside `pendingChat`, leaving `ChatSummary.Destination`'s cached encoding unchanged. Messages with a `message_id` have "Copy Link" (web form).
- Universal links: `App.entitlements` has `com.apple.developer.associated-domains` = `applinks:link.apron.chat` (declared in `stupid-app.yml` as `ASSOCIATED_DOMAINS`); `ApronLink.isLink` accepts `https://link.apron.chat/`.

### Open issues
- Universal links hand off to the app only once link.apron.chat's `apple-app-site-association` lists `6JKMV57Y77.tech.stupid.StupidSocial` (apron-chat/link.apron.chat#9) and a build with the entitlement ships; until then they open in Safari unless tapped inside the app.
- Chats list shows raw Markdown in previews, and same-named rooms on different servers look identical.

## Telegram

### Approach & constraints
- No HTTP API for user accounts, and stupid-app rejects binary targets (`Planner.swift`: "binaryTarget … unsupported"), so TDLib is out; the app implements MTProto 2.0 itself. Session is device-local (Keychain, not synchronizable, under the account id); the account itself syncs, so another device lists it and offers to sign in there. A new sign-in whose user id is already listed moves its session to that account (`TelegramClient.moveSession(to:)` then `reloadSession()`). Each account registers the APNs token with the other signed-in accounts' user ids as `other_uids`. Telegram pushes name the chat but not the receiving account, so a tap opens the first listed chat matching the peer's kind and id. `tgfile:` URLs don't name the account either: `TelegramFileURLProtocol` tries each signed-in client in turn. API id/hash are build-time from gitignored `Resources/TelegramAPI.json` (public repo); without it Settings shows "Telegram, Unavailable". Broadcast channels are listed with the other chats (probe `dialogs` marks them `broadcast`): posts have `post` and no `from_id`, so they show the channel's picture; subscribers have neither `creator` nor `admin_rights`, so the chat is read-only and the details have no Edit (`banned_rights` are absent for subscribers, so the group rule would wrongly allow editing); `channels.getParticipants` answers non-admins `CHAT_ADMIN_REQUIRED`, shown as "Only the channel's admins can see its subscribers." (observed 2026-11-14)
- Add Account holds its `TelegramConnectionViewModel` (from `SettingsViewModel.makeTelegramSignIn()`) as a `@StateObject` in `TelegramAddAccountView`. Making it in the view body made a new one on every update (such as returning from the background to read the code), which cleared the phone number and lost the client waiting for that code. Other networks keep their add-account input on the long-lived `SettingsViewModel`/`ApronServersViewModel`, so they weren't affected.
- TL codec is generic over the official schema (`scripts/telegram-schema.py` → `Resources/TelegramSchema.tl`, layer 225, deduplicated by constructor id). MTProto and API schemas both define `message`: encode-by-name prefers the API one, decoding goes by id
- Swift matches the probe byte for byte (`TelegramTests`: AES-IGE, 2048-bit modpow, SRP, `messages.sendMessage` encoding vectors); `testLiveHandshake` runs with `TEST_RUNNER_TELEGRAM_LIVE_TESTS=1`. A modpow takes ~1 s in debug builds; key exchange is once per device, SRP once per sign-in
- `TLSchema` is `@unchecked Sendable` (immutable; parameter tuples not inferred Sendable); transport's one-shot start flag is a class because `NWConnection` state handlers are `@Sendable`

### Connection
- DC migration: `auth.sendCode` may answer `PHONE_MIGRATE_<n>` and the client moves DC; 2FA via SRP. Cross-DC file access uses `auth.exportAuthorization`/`importAuthorization`
- Calls time out after 25 s by closing the session (a socket iOS dropped during suspension can stay silent); the client reconnects and retries. So a call doesn't wait that out after the app resumes (a chat opened from a notification could wait the full 25 s for its messages), a session that received nothing for 60 s is closed before use (`MTProtoSession.isUsable`; the 45 s ping's pong keeps a live one recent) and a new one opens at once. The app opens the session as it becomes active (`TelegramClient.connect()` from `AppContainer.startLiveFeeds`), and a session replacing a closed one calls the reconnect observers, so an open chat catches up (`onCatchUp`) on updates it missed. A session that outlived the background calls them too as the app becomes active: updates Telegram pushed while the app was suspended never arrive on it (observed 2026-11-20: a chat open across 20 s in the background never showed the two messages sent meanwhile, and the Telegram client has no `getDifference` gap recovery). `TelegramLiveRows` observes reconnects as well and reloads the dialogs, since those messages change rows. A group's members load after the newest page returns, since only mentions need them
- A failed write or ping in `MTProtoSession.send` closes the session so calls fail as `connectionLost` and reconnect-and-retry runs (raw transport errors such as `NWError` 57 "Socket is not connected" after iOS suspends sockets didn't trigger reconnect)

### Dialogs & messages
- `messages.getDialogs` without `folder_id` mixes all folders; the main list passes `folder_id: 0`, the Archive (`folder_id: 1`) is a separate section behind an Archived Chats row (observed 2026-10-27)
- Saved Messages messages carry no `out` flag (only `peer_id`/`saved_peer_id`), so a message is own when it has `out` or its sender is the signed-in user (observed 2026-10-27)
- Active accounts get constant `updateUserStatus`; `TelegramClient.update(_:concerns:)` keeps only message, delete and read updates for the open chat
- Typing (`ChatTyping`): incoming `updateUserTyping` (private, `user_id` is the peer), `updateChatUserTyping` and `updateChannelUserTyping` (`from_id` `peerUser`) via `TelegramClient.typingUpdates(_:concerns:)`; senders repeat them about every 5 s while typing (observed 2026-10-30, probe `listen --typing`). An indication lasts 6 s unless repeated; `sendMessageCancelAction` or a new message from that user ends it. Typing, recording, uploading and choosing stickers count; emoji interactions, group-call speaking, history import and game play don't. Outgoing `messages.setTyping` with `sendMessageTypingAction` at most every 5 s while the draft changes, so it lapses 6 s after the last edit; clearing the draft sends `sendMessageCancelAction`; sending only clears local state. Not sent in Saved Messages. Group names come from the page's users or the member list, else "Someone". Validated with probe `typing --peer self [--cancel]` (returns `true`)
- Muted: dialog `notify_settings.mute_until` in the future (`2147483647` = forever) → `ChatSummary.isMuted`, gray badge and a muted icon after the title. A dialog without `mute_until` follows its kind's default (`account.getNotifySettings` with `inputNotifyUsers` / `inputNotifyChats`, the latter covering supergroups too), loaded once per chat list; a present `mute_until: 0` is an explicit unmute (observed 2026-10-29). `scripts/telegram-client.py notify-defaults` prints the defaults and `dialogs` marks muted chats
- Reply quotes outside the loaded page load in one `getMessages` call before the page shows
- Service messages: `TelegramClient.message(_:selfId:names:)` takes the page's `users` names and words actions like Telegram (actor = sender; `users`/`user_id` for added/removed people; "You" for self; "Someone" only when a name is missing). `messageActionChatJoinedByLink` is sent by the joining user. Covered by `TelegramTests`
- Last-seen in one-to-one titles (not Saved Messages or bots): `messages.getPeerDialogs` returns the user's `status` (`userStatusOffline {was_online}`, `userStatusRecently`, `null` for bots) → `TelegramDialogState.userStatus`; text matches the member list (`ChatPresence.text`). Pushed `updateUserStatus` updates it while open (`TelegramClient.addStatusObserver`); the 30-second poll re-reads the dialog since pushes don't come for every user and relative times age. The Chats list's `getDialogs` already carries status; `ChatSummary.presence` hands it to the chat (and the adapter's saved state keeps it) so the title doesn't open as "Telegram" and resize
- Members: `channels.getParticipants` (`channelParticipantsRecent`) for supergroups (`channelParticipantCreator`), `messages.getFullChat` for basic groups (`chatParticipantCreator`/`chatParticipantAdmin`); statuses `userStatusRecently`, `userStatusOffline` (`was_online`), `userStatusLastMonth`. Probe `scripts/telegram-client.py members --peer …`. Swift: `TelegramClient.members(peer:)`, `TelegramChatViewModel.loadMembers()`, `TelegramChatView`/`TelegramMembersView` (online first, then by last seen). Chats opens Telegram chats via `telegramChatFactory` (returns `TelegramChatViewModel`; `ChatsView.opened` is generic)
- Mentions (observed 2026-10-29): received text carries `messageEntityMention` (`@username`, common) and `messageEntityMentionName` (a name tied to `user_id`, for people without a username). Telegram marks `@username` in sent plain text by itself, so only name mentions are sent as entities (`inputMessageEntityMentionName` with an `InputUser`). The history usually includes the mentioned users (49 of 58 in a sample), so `TelegramHistoryPage.usernames` resolves most `@username`s to an id; the rest open as `@username` and resolve on tap with `contacts.resolveUsername`, which can also name a channel (shown as "not a person"). Probe: `resolve --username`, `profile --user`, `mentions --peer` (no message text), `send --peer self --mention-user … --mention-range OFFSET:LENGTH`
- Mentions reach the shared chat as `ChatMessage.mentions` (UTF-16 ranges with a user id or `@username`), not through `ChatMentioning.mentionPattern`, which is `nil` for Telegram: a pattern for `@<digits>` would also highlight ordinary text such as "@2024". The composer offers group members (loaded once per chat with `channels.getParticipants`); a pick inserts `@username`, or `@<user id>` for someone without one, which `TelegramChatAdapter.resolvingMentions` turns into their name and a name mention on send (usernames can't start with a digit)
- Group details at the top of the member list (`TelegramMembersViewModel.info`, `TelegramClient.groupInfo`): `channels.getFullChannel` / `messages.getFullChat` give the title, `about`, photo and `participants_count`; they show even when the member list is hidden. Edit shows when the account is the creator, an admin with `admin_rights.change_info`, or a member whose `banned_rights` and the group's `default_banned_rights` don't withhold `change_info` (probe `chat-info`, observed 2026-11-01 on groups with each case). Saving sends only what changed: `channels.editTitle` / `messages.editChatTitle` (posts a service message in the group) and `messages.editChatAbout`. The mutation request shapes come from the schema and probe `edit-chat`, which hasn't been run against a live group. Photo editing isn't offered
- In a private chat the title opens the other person's profile (Saved Messages: your own), like a group's title opens its details. A profile opened from a chat doesn't offer Message when that chat is already your chat with them (`TelegramProfileView.openChat`)
- Full-screen profile photos: `inputPeerPhotoFileLocation` with `big` downloads the 640 px photo instead of the 160 px one (probe `avatar --big`), as `TelegramProfile.fullAvatarURL` and `TelegramGroupInfo.fullAvatarURL`; the viewer shows the cached small photo until it loads
- Profiles (`TelegramProfileView`/`TelegramProfileViewModel`): `users.getFullUser` gives the bio (`about`, optional) and the user's photo and status. `TelegramClient` remembers an `InputUser` for every user it sees (history, members, lookups): `inputUser` with the access hash, or `inputUserFromMessage` for a "min" user. A profile opens from a mention, a sender's avatar or a member list row. Message sets `pendingChat = .telegram(peer:)`; a private chat you've never messaged has no Chats row, so `TelegramClient`'s `ChatListResolving` stands in for it, titled by the name the profile showed, when the peer has an access hash (`messages.getPeerDialogs` returns an empty dialog for it, so the chat loads). Notification taps carry no access hash and still wait for the listed row
- Spoilers: `messageEntitySpoiler` (UTF-16 offsets) → `TelegramMessage.spoilers` / `ChatMessage.spoilers`. Chat draws them transparent under rounded dotted patches (laid out like quote bars); a tap reveals that message's spoilers for the visit; VoiceOver reads "spoiler". Chats row, reply quotes and composer reply preview mask with ⠿ (`SpoilerText.mask`)
- The official Telegram account (user 777000) sends login codes without spoiler entities, both "Login code: 12345." and my.telegram.org's "This is your login code:" (alphanumeric) (observed 2026-10-28); the token after "code:" in its messages is treated as a spoiler everywhere incl. notifications (`SpoilerText.loginCodeRanges`). Other spoilers can't be masked in notifications since pushes carry no entities

### Media, reactions, sending
- Videos, GIFs, round videos, voice messages, audio files and other documents of up to `maxWholeDownloadSize` (200 MB; downloads are held in memory) get a whole-document `tgfile:` URL: key `document-<id>/<file name>` (the name from `documentAttributeFilename`, else one with the MIME type's extension, so the player and Quick Look know the type) and a `max` query raising the per-URL limit from 20 MB. Videos and GIFs map to `.video` (poster: the largest thumbnail ≤ 640 px; GIFs often only have the inline `i` thumb, so they start blank), voice messages to `.voice` and audio files to `.audio`, both played through `ChatAudioPreviewing` from a `ChatMediaFiles` copy, everything else (PDF, zip, SVG…) to `.file`. Larger documents stay cards. Validated with the probe (`download --size full`): MP4 and Ogg Opus voice play with AVFoundation; WebM (video stickers) doesn't, so those stay thumbnails (observed 2026-10-30)
- Stored records are parsed `TelegramMessage`s, so after a parsing change older stored messages keep their old shapes (videos as cards): raise `SharedChatStore.recordFormat` whenever any adapter's record parsing changes, which clears stored history once
- Voice waveforms: `documentAttributeAudio.waveform` is 5-bit levels packed least significant bit first, 100 levels in 63 bytes (observed 2026-10-30), decoded by `TelegramMediaParser.waveform` and packed by `packedWaveform`
- Forwarding (`ChatForwarding`, probe `forward`, observed 2026-11-07 in Saved Messages): `messages.forwardMessages {from_peer, id: [ids], random_id: [one per id], to_peer}` answers `updates` with an `updateMessageID` per random id, then the new messages; a forwarded voice note stays a voice note with its waveform. Forwarding the user's own message into Saved Messages adds no `fwd_from`, so it shows as a plain message. Received forwards' `messageFwdHeader` (155 surveyed by the probe's `forwards`): channel posts `from_id peerChannel` + `channel_post`; users `from_id peerUser`, sometimes `saved_from_peer`/`saved_from_msg_id`; senders hiding their account only `from_name`. `from_id`'s user or channel is in the same response's `users`/`chats`, so `TelegramForward.name` resolves there
- Sending voice messages (`ChatVoiceSending`, probe `send-voice --peer self`, observed 2026-11-05): upload the file, then `messages.sendMedia` with `inputMediaUploadedDocument {mime_type: "audio/ogg", attributes: [documentAttributeAudio {voice, duration (whole seconds), waveform}]}`; Telegram answers with `messageMediaDocument` flagged `voice`, keeps the waveform as sent and serves the file back byte for byte. Ogg Opus is what Telegram's apps send and what the app already plays. Core Audio encodes Opus (`AVAudioConverter`, 20 ms packets, 32 kbps, pre-skip from `primeInfo`, 312) but writes no Ogg, so `OggOpusEncoder` (`Sources/OggOpus.swift`) builds the pages itself (RFC 7845: `OpusHead`, `OpusTags`, ~1 s of packets a page, final granule trimmed to the recorded length); ffmpeg decodes its output to the exact length. Encoding runs off the main actor in the adapter
- Link previews (`messageMediaWebPage`, probe `webpage-preview --url U [--download-dir D]`, fxtwitter posts observed 2026-11-09): a `webPage` with `has_large_media` becomes `.linkPreview` with its media (`TelegramMediaParser.webPageMedia`): its `document` (a post's video: `type: video`, the whole MP4, with the page's `photo` as the poster, since the document's thumbnail is 320 px), else its Instant View `cached_page` photos and videos in block order (a multi-photo post: `type: photo`, whose `photo` is fxtwitter's collage of them), else its `photo`. The page's blocks stop at the first `pageBlockDivider`: fxtwitter's page ends with an "About author" footer holding the author's photo. Without `has_large_media` (articles with a small image) it stays a card with a 56 pt thumbnail. `cached_page` photos download with their own file references like message photos. A preview can be `webPagePending` until Telegram fetches the page; the probe asks again. Snapshot version 5 for the new embed
- Downloads run at a few hundred KB/s per DC connection: a 3.4 MB video took 8 s in 512 KB parts and 10 s in 1 MB ones (observed 2026-10-30), so parts stay 512 KB
- Albums: Telegram sends each item as its own message with a shared `grouped_id`. `TelegramChatAdapter.albums` merges a run from one sender into the message with the caption (else the first), with every item's embeds; replies to the other items point at it (`albumIds`). Reactions on the other items aren't shown
- Custom emoji: `messageEntityCustomEmoji` (UTF-16 range over a fallback emoji, `document_id`) → `TelegramMessage.customEmojis` → `ChatMessage.customEmojis`; custom emoji reactions are keyed `:<document id>:` and sent back as `reactionCustomEmoji` (Premium only). The adapter (`ChatCustomEmojis`) asks `TelegramClient.customEmojiImages` for ids it hasn't asked for: `messages.getCustomEmojiDocuments` (≤ 200 per call, remembered per client), the whole WebP for static emoji, else the largest thumbnail ≤ 320 px, a WebP still of an animated TGS/WebM emoji (observed 2026-10-30, probe `custom-emoji --peer`). They're hidden from the picker, since Telegram's emoji sets aren't loaded. Paid reactions (Telegram Stars) show as ⭐️
- Photos sent via `upload.saveFilePart` + `messages.sendMedia`. Images load through a `tgfile:` URL scheme served by a `URLProtocol`, so `CachedAsyncImage`, bubbles and the viewer are unchanged. `URLProtocol` already has `task` (ours is `loadTask`); its client is called through a small `@unchecked Sendable` wrapper. "Min" users' avatars via `inputPeerUserFromMessage`
- Which reactions are allowed (probe `reactions --peer K`, observed 2026-10-31): `messages.sendReaction` refuses an emoji outside `messages.getAvailableReactions` with `REACTION_INVALID`, including 😂, which is listed but `inactive`; ❤️ with U+FE0F is accepted for the listed bare ❤. The list holds 74, 73 of them active and none `premium`. Groups narrow it with `available_reactions` on `messages.getFullChat` / `channels.getFullChannel` (`chatReactionsAll`, `chatReactionsSome`, `chatReactionsNone`); every group checked had All, and private chats have none set. In Saved Messages reactions are Premium tags, so every emoji fails with `PREMIUM_ACCOUNT_REQUIRED` (also observed 2026-10-27). `TelegramClient.reactionChoices` combines these (Premium from `users.getUsers inputUserSelf`, the available list remembered per client), and the adapter loads it once with the chat as `ChatReactionChoices`; an empty list (Saved Messages without Premium, `chatReactionsNone`) turns `acceptsReactions` off
- Editing (probe `telegram-client.py edit`, observed 2026-10-31): `messages.editMessage {peer, id, message, entities?}` answers `updates` with `updateEditMessage` (`updateEditChannelMessage` in supergroups) carrying `edit_date`; resending the same text fails `MESSAGE_NOT_MODIFIED`. An edit keeps only the entities sent with it (a bold entity was dropped), but Telegram marks URLs, hashtags, `@username`s and emails again by itself. So `TelegramMessage.isEditable` (parsed) allows text, link previews, photos and non-sticker, non-round documents, not forwarded or via a bot, whose entities are all of those kinds or name mentions; the adapter writes name mentions as `@<user id>` in the editable text and resolves them back to entities on save, as sending does. `help.getConfig` `edit_time_limit` is 172800 (48 h); Saved Messages have no limit (as Telegram's apps). A text message the app just sent is added as editable at once (it carries only name mentions), so Edit doesn't wait for Telegram's copy. `editDate` (none with `edit_hide`) → `ChatMessage.isEdited`. The adapter applies the edit locally, then refreshes

- Inline keyboards (probe `buttons`/`press`, observed 2026-11-02 in a bot group): `message.reply_markup` `replyInlineMarkup {rows: [keyboardButtonRow {buttons}]}` → `TelegramMessage.buttons` → `ChatMessage.buttons` (ids `<row>.<column>`). `keyboardButtonCallback` is `.press`: `messages.getBotCallbackAnswer {peer, msg_id, data}` answered in ~2 s with an empty `messages.botCallbackAnswer` (`native_ui`, `cache_time: 0`), and the bot's change came as an edit of the message without "edited" (`edit_hide`); a non-empty `message` shows as a toast, or an alert with `alert`, and `url` opens. A bot that doesn't answer fails with `BOT_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT`. `keyboardButtonUrl` and `keyboardButtonUrlAuth` (whose `url` also opens without the site sign-in) are `.link`, shown before opening since the label hides it; `keyboardButtonCopy` is `.copy`. The rest (callbacks with `requires_password`, games, payments, mini apps, switch-inline, request-peer/phone/location/poll, user profile) show dimmed. `replyKeyboardMarkup` (a bot's keyboard under the composer) isn't shown. Snapshot version 3 for the new field.

### Stickers
- Formats (probe `stickers`, observed 2026-11-03): `application/x-tgsticker` (TGS, ≤ 64 KB), `video/webm` (VP9 with alpha, 512 px, 24–30 fps, ≤ 256 KB), `image/webp`; thumbnails `j`/`m`. `TelegramMediaParser.sticker` maps a document to `ChatSticker` (whole-file `tgfile:` URL keyed `document-<id>-full`, the largest thumbnail ≤ 320 px as poster for animated ones) and a message's sticker to `ChatEmbed.sticker`. Snapshot version 4
- Sending (`ChatStickerSending`): `messages.getRecentStickers`, `getFavedStickers` (with documents) and `getAllStickers` (sets only, with `count`; a set's stickers load with `messages.getStickerSet` when its section scrolls into view, remembered per client). Stickers for an emoji: `messages.getStickers {emoticon, hash: 0}` returned 91 for 😂, 9 from installed sets, and only animated ones (none of the user's many static sets; probe `stickers-for`, observed 2026-11-04), so `TelegramClient.stickers(forEmoji:)` puts the loaded sets' stickers whose `alt` matches (ignoring U+FE0F) first, then installed, then suggested; suggested ones are remembered so they can be sent. Sets' own thumbnails (`inputStickerSetThumb`, type `a` TGS, `v` WebM, else still) are the tabs; most static sets have none and take their first sticker's. `messages.sendMedia` with `inputMediaDocument` (id, access hash, file reference of a document the client listed), validated with probe `send-sticker --peer self`. The account had 157 installed sets; the panel's preload fetches them all in the background, three at a time, and `TelegramClient` reuses the set list for 10 minutes (cleared by a sticker send, since Recent changes)
- Lottie renderer (`Stickers/Lottie*.swift`): parity with Telegram's rlottie fork, not upstream rlottie: upstream's 2026 safety limits blank some animated emoji entirely, and the fork differs in layer out-points (exclusive), shape-layer opacity (multiplied into each paint, not composited), gradient stops and frame count (`op - ip`). Things matched on purpose, each found by `scripts/lottie-compare.py`: `VLine::length` is "alpha max plus beta min" (`max + 3/8 min`), which positions on motion curves and trims follow, with `VBezier::tAtLength`'s search (up to ~100 steps); points with `to`/`ti` move along the curve for any 2D property; path morphs between different point counts cut the longer; easing uses each handle's first dimension; colours pack as clang compiles `uchar(255 * x)` on arm64 (signed saturating int, low byte, ORed into the word unmasked), so overshooting easings (`y: 10`, used by some animated emoji) wrap colours and opacity; gradient stops are premultiplied and blended premultiplied (given to Core Graphics as eight steps per pair). Skew, merge paths, expressions, effects, masks, repeaters, polystars and rounded corners are ignored, as in the fork (census of 1,134 stickers: no masks, text or images; merge paths 9 %, expressions 7 %, skew 3 %)
- Result (observed 2026-11-04, 1,134 TGS from 20 animated sets and the animated emoji set, 6 frames each at 256 px): 1,098 (96.8 %) within 2 % differing pixels of rlottie. The rest: dash phases on a few dotted strokes (the fork's dasher skips dash pieces under 1 px), and effects of out-of-range values that rlottie draws as glitches (a giant ring, stray blobs). Telegram's iOS app has since moved to its own renderer, so those glitches aren't what every Telegram user sees either
- Playback (`ChatStickerView`): 98 % of the corpus is 60 fps, and rendering takes 1–7 ms a frame at 480 px on an M-series Mac. The first version rendered each frame on demand, capped at 30 fps and picked frames by the clock, skipping unevenly when a render ran late; it looked less smooth than Telegram. Now each view has a serial queue that prepares the next three frames in order, the display link runs at the sticker's own rate (≤ 60) and shows each frame on time (a late frame delays playback instead of skipping), and rendered frames are LZ4-compressed into a shared cache keyed by file and pixel size (6–20× smaller, 0.1 ms to decompress; 8–26 MB per 180-frame sticker; 150 MB `NSCache`), so later loops and other views only decompress. Measured in the simulator (observed 2026-11-04): two stickers at 60.0 fps, frames 16.7 ms apart (worst 19 ms), about 9 % of a core for both once cached. Only on screen; the picker shows first frames
- WebM (`Stickers/WebMSticker.swift`): EBML parse of `Segment`/`Tracks`/`Cluster`; frames from `SimpleBlock`s or `BlockGroup`s (keyframe when no `ReferenceBlock`), alpha from `BlockAdditions`/`BlockMore` with `BlockAddID` 1. Sticker tools write a placeholder `Duration` (1 ms), so the frame rate comes from block timecodes. VP9 via `VTRegisterSupplementalVideoDecoderIfAvailable` (iOS 26.2+) with a `vpcC` atom; colour as BGRA, alpha as 4:2:0 full-range luma, premultiplied together. Each view has its own decoder (VP9 frames depend on earlier ones); looping restarts from frame 0. Matches ffmpeg's libvpx decode within ~1.6 % of pixels (chroma upsampling at sharp edges). The iOS 27 simulator has no VP9 decoder (`-12906`), so video stickers show their poster there; on Mac the decoder works (5 ms a frame at 512 px). Not yet checked on a device
- Panel (`ChatStickerPanel`, a `UIInputView`): the composer's sticker button sets it as the text view's `inputView` and reloads input views, so it replaces the keyboard and the composer stays on `keyboardLayoutGuide`; it takes the last on-screen keyboard's height (from `keyboardWillChangeFrame`, 336 before one has shown). A tap on the text, or the text view ending editing, puts the keyboard back. A search field was left out: typing into a field inside the input view would bring the keyboard over the panel, so a row of emoji filters instead, as in Telegram's panel. No loading state: when a chat that offers stickers appears, `preload` fetches the sets, every set's stickers, the emoji row's results, and draws first-frame stills at the grid's pixel size (the first 60 and every tab's into memory, the next 100 and each emoji's first 15 to disk), and the grid prefetches stills for rows about to scroll in. Stills (`ChatStickerFiles`) are kept in memory (80 MB) and as PNGs under `Caches/sticker-stills`, keyed by the URL's host and path (a Telegram URL's query carries a changing file reference); stills under 150 px (a tab's own render) stay in memory, and a larger still always wins. A still view with a sharp enough still skips rendering frame 0. One grid of every set with a header each; a section holds `count` empty cells until its set loads, should the preload not have reached it; the tab bar follows the topmost header and a tab scrolls to its set. Press and hold shows a blurred overlay inside the panel (the keyboard window is above the app's, so a preview can't go over the chat) with the sticker playing and Send. After a send the sticker leads Recent locally. On a Mac (`isiOSAppOnMac`), where no on-screen keyboard shows, the panel opens as a sheet and closes after sending. The composer's sticker button is a fixed 32 pt wide and swaps face and keyboard outside the input view's animation, which otherwise squeezed the icon. iOS 27's SF Symbols swapped the faces: `face.smiling` is filled and `face.smiling.inverse` the outline (observed 2026-11-07), so the outline name depends on the OS. The recording row is shown and laid out without animation, since a row shown for the first time grew from the pill's corner
- `UIImage.preparingForDisplay()` fails on some WebP stickers in the simulator (`-17102`); `ChatMediaImageCache` falls back to the undecoded image

### Push
- `account.registerDevice` with Web Push (token type 10, Telegram Web's VAPID key `BHEbKOXt-…KPM`) answers `APP_PUSH_APIKEY_MISSING` for this api_id (observed 2026-10-27). Instead an APNs SSL (Sandbox & Production) certificate for `tech.stupid.StupidSocial` (valid to 2027-11-26, key kept outside the repo) was uploaded as certificate + key PEM to both slots in my.telegram.org/apps Push credentials (`apps/apns_cert`); token type 1 registration then returns `true` (`TelegramPushService`)
- APNs alerts are `loc-key`/`loc-args`; `scripts/telegram-push-strings.py` writes 190 templates to `Resources/en.lproj/Localizable.strings` (`{1}` → `%1$@`); `Info.plist` has `CFBundleDevelopmentRegion = en`

### UI
- `TelegramBadge` PNGs (blue circle, white paper plane) drawn with ImageMagick

### Open issues
- Write/ping-failure reconnect is unconfirmed on device (the simulator doesn't suspend sockets)
- Video and file downloads show a spinner, not progress; custom emoji show stills (the sticker renderers could draw them); `messageMediaUnsupported` (media newer than layer 225) shows as "Attachment"

## Discord

### Auth & requests
- User tokens only (no OAuth for user clients). `DiscordLoginWebView` loads `https://discord.com/login` in a non-persistent store with a document-start script that reports the first `Authorization` header the web client sets (XHR or fetch) or `localStorage.token`; Discord hides `window.localStorage` from later scripts, so the script keeps its own reference. Closing the sheet doesn't log out, so the token stays valid. Dev mode takes a token by hand. The WebView login hasn't been run end to end yet; the manual token path was (2026-11-07).
- Requests send discord.com's web client headers (`DiscordClient.headers`): Chrome 156 macOS user agent, `X-Super-Properties` with `client_build_number` 667580, `X-Discord-Locale`, `X-Discord-Timezone`, `X-Debug-Options: bugReporterEnabled`, Origin/Referer discord.com (captured from the web client 2026-11-07). 401 → `SourceError.notConfigured`; 429 waits out `retry_after` once when it is 5 s or less.
- Endpoints: `GET /users/@me`; `GET /channels/{id}/messages?limit=50[&before|after|around]` (newest first); `POST /channels/{id}/messages {content, nonce, tts, flags, message_reference}`; `PATCH`/`DELETE /channels/{id}/messages/{mid}`; `PUT`/`DELETE /channels/{id}/messages/{mid}/reactions/{emoji or name:id}/@me?location=Message%20Reaction%20Picker&type=0`; `GET …/reactions/{emoji}?limit=100&type=0` (reactors, loaded when the reactor list opens); `POST /channels/{id}/messages/{mid}/ack {"token": null}`; `POST /channels/{id}/typing` (at most every 8 s while the draft changes).

### Gateway
- `wss://gateway.discord.gg/?encoding=json&v=9`, `Origin: https://discord.com`. Hello (op 10) → heartbeat (op 1 with the last sequence) every `heartbeat_interval`, the first at half; identify (op 2) with the token, `capabilities: 0`, the super properties plus `client_app_state`, `compress: false`, `client_state.guild_versions: {}`. Op 7 and op 9 reconnect and identify again (no resume).
- `READY` with `capabilities: 0` (observed 2026-11-07, about 430 KB for 9 guilds and 84 DMs): `guilds[]` each with `channels`, `roles`, `members` (always including the user's own member), `emojis`; `private_channels[]` with `recipients`, `is_message_request`, `is_spam`; `read_state[]` `{id (channel), last_message_id, mention_count}`; `user_guild_settings[]` `{guild_id, muted, channel_overrides[{channel_id, muted}]}`. Guild channels in `READY` and `GUILD_CREATE` have **no `guild_id`**; `DiscordGuild.withGuildIds` sets it, since titles, permissions and mutes depend on it.
- Every guild in `READY` was `lazy: true` (observed 2026-11-07). Opening a guild channel in the web client sends `op 37 {subscriptions: {<guild id>: {typing, threads, activities: true, members: [], member_updates: false, channels: {<channel id>: [[0, 99]]}, thread_member_lists: []}}}`; the adapter sends it without the member-list ranges. Whether unsubscribed lazy guilds still deliver `MESSAGE_CREATE` wasn't observed (nothing arrived in a quiet 45 s), so open chats also poll every 30 s.
- `URLSessionWebSocketTask.maximumMessageSize` is 64 MiB; the default 1 MiB is close to a modest `READY`.

### Chats list & chat
- Rows: DMs and group DMs (not message requests or spam) and guild text (0) and announcement (5) channels whose computed permissions include `VIEW_CHANNEL`; channels without a `last_message_id` are skipped. Permissions: owner → all; else the `@everyone` role and the member's roles; `ADMINISTRATOR` → all; then the channel's `@everyone` overwrite, the member's roles' overwrites (deny then allow, combined), and the member's own overwrite.
