- Unread: a channel is unread when its `last_message_id` is past the read state's. Discord's `mention_count` counts every DM message but only mentions in guilds, so the count is the larger of it and others' messages past the read position in the fetched page; without a page, at least one.
- Text: `<@id>` and `<@!id>` → `@id` (framework mention pattern `@(\d{15,21})`, names from authors, mentions and guild members, guild nicknames first); outgoing `@id` → `<@id>`. `<:name:id>` and `<a:name:id>` → `:id:` custom emoji (`cdn.discordapp.com/emojis/<id>.png|gif`), also as reaction keys; toggling one sends `name:id`. `<#id>` and `<@&id>` read as `#channel` and `@role`; `<t:unix:style>` as a formatted date. Messages are Markdown.
- Media: attachments by `content_type` (images open the viewer, videos play, the rest are files); `gifv` embeds play as GIFs, `image` embeds as images; other embeds with large media become `.linkPreview`s: Discord sends a link's several images as consecutive embeds sharing its `url` (all but the first carry only `image`), merged by `groupedByURL`; a `video` with a `proxy_url` (a file Discord proxies) plays, posted by `image` or `thumbnail`; a video without one (YouTube's player) shows its `thumbnail` large, opening the link; `image` opens the viewer. The rest with a title or description are link cards, their `thumbnail` beside the text. This follows Discord's documented embed shape and is covered by `DiscordChatTests` against recorded-shape JSON, but hasn't been checked against a live fxtwitter message (no token at hand, 2026-11-09); PNG, APNG and GIF stickers from `media.discordapp.net/stickers/<id>.png|gif`, Lottie stickers as cards. Attachment URLs are signed and expire (about 24 h), so stored messages may show broken images until history reloads.
- System messages (types other than 0 and 19) show as centered notices. Replies quote `referenced_message` when the original isn't loaded; `null` marks it unavailable.
- Permissions gate the composer (no `SEND_MESSAGES`, or `SEND_MESSAGES_IN_THREADS` in a thread, or a locked thread → `ChatReadOnlyAccess`), reactions (`ADD_REACTIONS`) and attachments (`ATTACH_FILES`).
- The badge (`Resources/DiscordBadge*.png`) is Discord's mark from Simple Icons (CC0, `simple-icons@13.21.0/icons/discord.svg`) in white on `#5865F2`, rendered in a browser (ImageMagick's and CoreSVG's SVG renderers both mangle its compact path syntax) and scaled to 16, 32 and 48 px.

### Threads & forums
- Threads (types 10, 11, 12) are channels with a `parent_id`. `READY` and `GUILD_CREATE` carry each guild's active threads in `threads` (none of the user's guilds had any, observed 2026-11-07), with `member` set when the user has joined; `withGuildIds` moves them into `channels`. `THREAD_CREATE`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`, `THREAD_LIST_SYNC {guild_id, threads, members}`, `THREAD_MEMBER_UPDATE` and `THREAD_MEMBERS_UPDATE {added_members, removed_member_ids}` keep them and the user's membership current; an update without `member` keeps the one known (`DiscordGateway.remember`).
- A thread's permissions are its parent's; a private thread (12) also needs membership. Sending in a thread needs `SEND_MESSAGES_IN_THREADS` (1 << 38).
- Listed: joined threads, plus each visible forum's (15) or media channel's (16) 10 newest posts from `GET /channels/{forum}/threads/search?archived=true&sort_by=last_message_time&sort_order=desc&limit=10&tag_setting=match_some&offset=0` (the forum view's call), fetched again when the forum's `last_message_id` (its newest post) changes. Its `members` mark the posts the user joined. A thread with no read state counts as read, as in Discord. Rows are titled with the thread's name under "Guild / parent". The only forum among the user's guilds answered 403 Missing Access (it is hidden from the user), so the search's response shape is from Discord's documentation, not observed.

### Profiles & starting DMs
- `GET /users/{id}/profile?with_mutual_guilds=true&with_mutual_friends_count=true`: `user` (with `bio`), `user_profile {bio, pronouns}`, `mutual_guilds[{id, nick}]`, `mutual_friends_count`, `premium_type`, `badges` (observed 2026-11-07 on the user's own profile).
- `DiscordChatView` opens `DiscordProfileView` from avatars and mentions (`showProfile`), and from the title in a DM; in a server channel the title opens `DiscordChannelDetailsView` (`DiscordChannelDetails`: name, server, topic, server icon) with Show All Channels. Message opens the DM the gateway knows with that user, else `POST /users/@me/channels {recipients: [id]}` (creates or returns the DM; sends nothing), remembers it in the gateway and sets `NotificationRouter.pendingChat`. A DM without messages isn't listed (`last_message_id` is null), so `DiscordChatList` resolves it from the gateway state (`ChatListResolving`). Message isn't shown on the profile of the DM it was opened from, or on your own.

### Sending files & stickers
- Forwards (probe `forward`, validated in a DM the user named for testing, observed 2026-11-07): `POST /channels/{to}/messages {content: "", nonce, tts: false, flags: 0, message_reference: {type: 1, channel_id, message_id, guild_id (server channels only)}}`. Received forwards have empty `content`, `flags` 16384 (`HAS_SNAPSHOT`), `message_reference.type` 1 and `message_snapshots[0].message` {type, content, mentions, attachments, embeds, timestamp, flags, …}, with no author or id, so the app shows "Forwarded" without a name
- Voice messages (probe `send-voice`, same DM, observed 2026-11-07; the web client's user token may send them): upload `voice-message.ogg` (Ogg Opus) as for files with `Content-Type: audio/ogg`, then `{content: "", nonce, channel_id, type: 0, flags: 8192 (IS_VOICE_MESSAGE), sticker_ids: [], attachments: [{id: "0", filename, uploaded_filename, duration_secs, waveform: base64 of 0–255 levels}]}`; the app sends 64 levels, as the probe did. Received: `flags` 8192, the attachment with `content_type: audio/ogg`, `duration_secs` (Discord measures the file: 2.02 for 2.0 sent) and `waveform`
- Files: `POST /channels/{id}/attachments {files: [{filename, file_size, id: "0", is_clip: false}]}` → `attachments[{id, upload_url, upload_filename}]`; `PUT` the bytes to `upload_url` (a signed Google Cloud Storage URL: no Discord headers, the token stays with Discord); then the message's `attachments: [{id: "0", filename, uploaded_filename}]`. This is the web client's flow; the probe's `upload` does the same.
- Stickers: `sticker_ids: [id]` on a message with empty content. Without Nitro (`user.premium_type` 0) a guild's stickers can only be sent in that guild, so the panel offers the current guild's (every guild's with Nitro, the current one first), then `GET /sticker-packs` (14 standard packs, loaded once per gateway, observed 2026-11-07). Search by emoji matches `tags`, which for a guild sticker is the emoji it stands for.
- Sticker files: PNG and APNG (1, 2) from `media.discordapp.net/stickers/<id>.png?size=320`, GIF (4) as `.gif`, Lottie (3) from `discord.com/stickers/<id>.json` with the PNG as poster. Discord's Lottie is plain JSON, so `ChatStickerFiles` tries TGS (gzip) then plain JSON. APNG shows its first frame (the image cache decodes stills).

### Probe
- `scripts/discord-web-client.py` (stdlib only) reads `DISCORD_TOKEN` from the environment or `.env.local` and never prints it. Commands: `me`, `guilds`, `channels --guild`, `dms`, `forward --from-channel --message --to-channel --expect-name` (own messages only), `send-voice --channel --expect-name --file F.ogg --duration S`, `history` and `around` (summaries: author id, length, attachment types, reactions; `--show-text` prints only the user's own text), `ready [--shape]`, `follow [--shape]` (dispatch names and ids), `raw GET <path>` (JSON shape only), `threads` (each guild's channel types, forums and READY threads), `forum-threads --channel`, `stickers` (standard packs and guild stickers by format), `profile --user [--shape]`. `send`, `edit`, `delete`, `react`, `typing`, `upload --file` and `send-sticker --sticker` require `--expect-name` matching the channel's name; `ack` moves the read position; `open-dm --user` creates or returns a DM without sending. The mutations follow the web client's request shapes and are covered by `DiscordChatTests` against a stubbed session, but haven't been run live: the agent's permission classifier blocks writes to the account.

## LinkedIn

### Auth & requests
- Sessions are the `li_at` and `JSESSIONID` cookies. `LinkedInLoginWebView` loads `https://www.linkedin.com/login` in the persistent web store (`CookieLoginWebView`, deleting the previous login's two cookies first) and finishes once `li_at` is set; `JSESSIONID` exists before login too. Dev mode takes both by hand. Only the two values are stored (`LinkedInCredentials`, Keychain per account). The WebView login hasn't been run end to end yet; the manual path was, with the cookies of a signed-in browser (2026-11-09).
- Every request sends `Cookie: li_at=…; JSESSIONID="ajax:…"`, the JSESSIONID without quotes as `csrf-token`, `x-restli-protocol-version: 2.0.0`, `x-li-lang: en_US` and an iPhone Safari user agent (`LinkedInClient.userAgent`), which the login page sends too. A Mac Chrome agent on a session created by the iPhone login page saw the session revoked soon after connecting: LinkedIn answered 302 and cleared `li_at`, `li_a` and `liap` (observed 2026-11-09); one agent for the whole session is the first fix tried, not yet confirmed. Bodies are JSON sent as `text/plain;charset=UTF-8`, as the web client's `fetch` sends them. A bad `li_at` answers 302 to the login page (observed 2026-11-09), so `LinkedInClient` doesn't follow redirects and maps 3xx/401 to `SourceError.notConfigured`; a mismatched JSESSIONID still reads fine.
- `GET /voyager/api/me` (normalized JSON: the mini profile in `included` with `dashEntityUrn` = `urn:li:fsd_profile:<id>`, the account's `remoteId` and messaging mailbox).

### Notifications
- `GET /voyager/api/voyagerIdentityDashNotificationCards?decorationId=com.linkedin.voyager.dash.deco.identity.notifications.CardsCollectionWithInjectionsNoPills-25&count=20&q=filterVanityName&start=0`, Accept `application/vnd.linkedin.normalized+json+2.1`: `data.*elements` names `included` cards (`headline.text`, `publishedAt` ms, `read`, `cardAction.actionTarget` relative path, `headerImage.attributes[].detailData.*profilePicture` → an included Profile's `vectorImage`, `headerImage.accessibilityText` "View <name>’s profile"). The card kind is the first part of the urn (`fsd_notificationCard:(JOB_CHANGE_PROP,…)`). The Notifications page itself is now server-driven UI (`flagship-web/rsc-action/…notificationsList`), but this endpoint returns the same cards (observed 2026-11-09, with only network-update cards on the account, so other kinds' fields aren't observed and the type mapping from the kind name is a guess).
- Items keep LinkedIn's sentence as their text, minus " View more network updates." The feed shows the network badge before text that doesn't start with the actor's name.
- The app never marks notifications seen. The web client does it with the SDUI action `com.linkedin.sdui.requests.notifications.markNotificationsSeen`; the older `voyagerNotificationsDashBadge?action=markAllItemsAsSeen` is in the bundle too.

### Messaging (GraphQL and Rest.li)
- Reads are `GET /voyager/api/voyagerMessagingGraphQL/graphql?queryId=<id>&variables=(…)`, Accept `application/graphql`; values inside `variables` are percent-encoded URNs (`LinkedInClient.restli`). Query ids come from the messaging SDK in the web bundle (`kind:"query",id:"messengerMessages.<hash>",name:"get-messages-by-timestamp"`, observed 2026-11-09): `messengerConversations.9501074288a12f3ae9e3c7ea243bccbf` (by category: `query:(predicateUnions:List((conversationCategoryPredicate:(category:PRIMARY_INBOX)))),count:20,mailboxUrn,nextCursor?` → `messengerConversationsByCategoryQuery {elements, metadata.nextCursor}`), `…fb3eab135970d880d59c5fad1bc695f9` (by id, needs `count`), `messengerMessages.5846eeb71c981f11e0134cb6626cc314` (sync: newest messages, newest first, up to 20) and `…d8ea76885a52fd5dc5c317078ab7c977` (`deliveredAt,conversationUrn,countBefore:20,countAfter:0` → older messages, oldest first, `metadata.prevCursor` null at the start).
- The sync conversations query (`…0d5e6781…`, what the web client loads first) returns the newest 20 of every category, spam included; the Chats list reads the primary inbox, two pages (40).
- Writes are Rest.li actions on `voyagerMessagingDashMessenger*` (the SDK's `Qa("restli", …)`), all validated with the probe in a test conversation (2026-11-09): `Messages?action=createMessage {message: {body: {attributes, text}, renderContentUnions, conversationUrn, originToken}, mailboxUrn, trackingId (16 raw bytes as characters), dedupeByClientGeneratedToken: false}` → `{value: {entityUrn, deliveredAt}}`; a reply adds `renderContentUnions: [{repliedMessageContent: {originalSenderUrn (participant entityUrn), originalSendAt, originalMessageUrn, messageBody}}]`; `Messages?action=reactWithEmoji|unreactWithEmoji {messageUrn, emoji}`; `Messages/<encoded urn> {patch: {$set: {body}}}` (edit; the message then reads `EDITED`); `Messages?action=recall {messageUrn}` (stays in the history as `RECALLED`, reactions dropped); `Conversations?ids=List(<encoded urn>) {entities: {<urn>: {patch: {$set: {read: true}}}}}`; `Conversations?action=typing {conversationUrn}` (202).
- Messages: `sender {hostIdentityUrn, entityUrn}`, `actor` (the full participant), `body.text`, `deliveredAt` ms, `messageBodyRenderFormat`, `reactionSummaries [{emoji, count, viewerReacted}]` (no reactor list; the SDK fetches one per emoji, not ported), `renderContent` (a union with every kind as a key: `vectorImage`, `externalMedia` (GIFs), `video`, `audio`, `file`, `hostUrnData` (shared posts), `forwardedMessageContent`, `repliedMessageContent`, `callMessageContent`, `videoMeeting`, `awayMessage`, `unavailableContent`, ads). Only GIFs, shared posts and replies were observed; the image, video, audio, file and forward shapes follow the SDK's field names and decode leniently. File and audio URLs may need the session cookie, which `URLSession` doesn't send for them.
- Read state is per conversation (`unreadCount`, `read`, `lastReadAt`), so the adapter marks a chat read only when the newest message from others has been seen.
- The badge (`Resources/LinkedInBadge*.png`) is a white "in" (Arial Bold) on `#0A66C2`, drawn with ImageMagick at 16, 32 and 48 px; it must be listed in `stupid-app.yml`'s `resources` to ship.

### Realtime stream (`LinkedInLiveFeed`)
- `GET /realtime/connect?rc=1`, `Accept: text/event-stream`, with the usual cookies and `csrf-token` plus `x-li-realtime-session` (a UUID the client makes up per connection), `x-li-query-map` (`{"topicToGraphQLQueryParams": {<topic>: {queryId, variables: {}, extensions: {}}}}`: the messaging topics' `voyagerMessagingDashMessengerRealtimeDecoration.<hash>` decorations, `LinkedInClient.realtimeQueryMap`), `x-li-accept`/`x-li-recipe-accept` normalized JSON, `x-li-query-accept: application/graphql`, and `x-li-track` with `mpName: voyager-web`; without `mpName` it answers 400 "Could not map to valid appName for supplied mpName: null" (all observed 2026-11-09).
- Each event is one `data:` line of JSON: first `com.linkedin.realtimefrontend.ClientConnection {id, personalTopics (29)}`, then `Heartbeat {}` every 15 s and `DecoratedEvent {topic, payload.data.<decoration>.result}`. Messaging topics arrive without subscribing; the web client only `PUT`s `realtimeFrontendSubscriptions` for presence and its AI coach's streaming topic. Its `realtimeFrontendClientConnectivityTracking?action=sendHeartbeat` posts are presence tracking, not needed to keep the stream (a 55 s stream delivered without them).
- Observed with the probe in the test conversation: `messagesTopic` (the full message plus `conversation.entityUrn`; also for edits and unsends, as `RECALLED`), `messageReactionSummariesTopic` (`{message.entityUrn, reactionSummary {emoji, count, viewerReacted}, reactionAdded, actor}`), `conversationsTopic` (the full conversation, after marking read) and `tabBadgeUpdateTopic`. Typing (`typingIndicatorsTopic`: `{conversation.entityUrn, typingParticipant}`, per the web bundle; shown 5 s) and seen receipts only come from the other person, so they weren't observed; seen receipts aren't handled.
- The web client treats 30 s without a heartbeat as a dropped connection (`heartbeatGapThreshold`); `LinkedInLiveFeed` allows 45 s, then cancels the stream and `LiveConnection` reconnects. A signed-out session stops it (`SourceError.notConfigured`).
- Open chats apply messages, reaction totals, typing and conversation changes, and keep a 60 s poll as a fallback (10 s without a live feed). Chats rows update from conversation events directly; a message event refetches its conversation (debounced 500 ms), since the event doesn't carry the unread count.

### Probe
- `scripts/linkedin-web-client.py` (stdlib): `me`, `notifications`, `badges`, `conversations`, `find --name`, `messages [--before]`, `stream --seconds` (realtime events' topics and shapes, the user's own text only), and `send [--reply-to]`, `react [--remove]`, `edit`, `recall`, `read [--unread]`, `typing`, which need `--expect-name` to match a participant. Credentials from `--credentials-file` (the app's `liAt`/`jsessionId` or the browser's `li_at`/`JSESSIONID`) or `--simulator`. It never prints others' message text.

## WhatsApp

### Approach & constraints
- Linked device on WhatsApp's multi-device protocol (see `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` § WhatsApp). References cloned into the ignored `third-party/`: whatsmeow (Go), its libsignal port `libsignal-go` (cloned from `tulir/libsignal-protocol-go`, module `go.mau.fi/libsignal`) and Baileys (TS). The probe ports their logic; byte-level questions are answered from those sources, not by guessing
- Unofficial clients are against WhatsApp's terms and accounts can be restricted; the probe only sends what WhatsApp Web sends, and sends test messages only to the self chat (`send --to self`; other recipients need `--allow-others`)
- No push for linked third-party devices: messages arrive only while connected (the server queues them offline and delivers on the next login, ending with `<ib><offline count=…/>`)

### Probe (`scripts/whatsapp-client.py`)
- uv inline script (cryptography, protobuf, grpcio-tools, websockets, qrcode). Protobufs are compiled from `third-party/whatsmeow/proto` and libsignal-go's `WhisperTextProtocol.proto` into `logs/whatsapp-proto` on first run
- Commands: `link [--open-qr | --phone N]`, `connect [--listen S]`, `appstate [--full]` (also fetches joined groups), `chats [--archived]`, `send --to self --text …`, `read --chat … IDS`, `send --reply-to ID`, `react --id ID`, `typing`, `forget-session --device N` (to test retry receipts; the probe sends them on decryption failures), `send-media --file F --kind image|document|video|audio`, `resend --chat JID --id ID` (placeholder resend request), `media [--kind K] [--limit N] [--id ID] [--retry]` (downloads from saved history; prints kind, type, sizes and age, never content), `history --chat JID [--count N]` (on-demand history), `version`, `selftest` (codec, XEdDSA, Signal round trips, offline)
- State in `logs/whatsapp-session.json` (0600): device keys, Signal sessions and sender keys, identities, one-time prekeys, LID map, push names, app state keys, app state collection versions/hashes/MACs, groups. History syncs are saved raw under `logs/whatsapp-history/`, app state summary in `logs/whatsapp-appstate.json`. Never prints others' message text
- XEdDSA in the probe is pure-Python Edwards arithmetic for signing; verification converts the Montgomery key and uses `cryptography`'s Ed25519 (libsignal-go `ecc/SignCurve25519.go`)

### Protocol observations (observed 2026-10-30)
- `web.whatsapp.com/sw.js` (current `client_revision`, as Baileys reads it) answers 400 without `Sec-Fetch-Site: none`. The version used was 2.3000.1052379569 (whatsmeow's built-in was 2.3000.1050728964); linking sends the fetched version
- The socket must send keepalives (`w:p` iq) from the start: without them WhatsApp dropped the connection while the first QR code (60 s) was showing
- After `pair-success` the server closes the stream (stream error 515) and the device logs in on a new connection; the first login found 0 prekeys on the server and uploaded 812
- Right after linking the phone sent, as `pkmsg` without a one-time prekey: history syncs NON_BLOCKING_DATA, INITIAL_BOOTSTRAP (413 chats, 785 messages, 361 PN→LID mappings), INITIAL_STATUS_V3, PUSH_NAME (2000 names) and RECENT (413 chats, 4730 messages, 644 mappings), each downloaded from the media hosts (`md-msg-hist`), plus 20 app state sync keys. The phone's sender is the user's LID (`<lid>@lid`), so sessions with the phone are under the LID address
- App state: all five collections synced from snapshots with value/index MACs and LTHash snapshot/patch MACs verifying. Contact names (`contact` index, 202), `mute`, `archive`, `pin_v1` and `markChatAsRead` are the ones chats need; most mutations are `deleteMessageForMe` and `call_log`
- History sync conversations lack names for most groups; `<participating>` returns all joined groups' subjects (136) and participants (with `phone_number`/`lid` pairs)
- A self-chat text sent by the probe (LID addressing, `msg` to the phone over the session it opened, `pkmsg` to another linked device from a fetched bundle, `device-identity` attached) was acked by the server and shown decrypted on the phone

### Push (investigation)
- WhatsApp Web registers Web Push: `<iq xmlns="urn:xmpp:whatsapp:push" type="set"><config platform="web" endpoint=… auth=… p256dh=…/>` (whatsmeow `push.go`, `WebPushConfig`). `<settings/>` (type get) answers `webserverkey`, WhatsApp's VAPID public key (`BIt4eFAVqVxe4yOA5_VLbZTbOlV-2y1FYJ_R4RlxWoyYazAq4glIxI7fh_xLbob1SNv7ZtTWn9mmZCsk2YNXYeY`, observed 2026-10-30), which a push.stupidtech.net registration needs as its `vapidKey`
- WhatsApp Web (`WASmaxPushConfigSetRPC`) sends the config with `lg` and `lc` too, and subscribes only when Background sync is on and notifications are allowed; its service worker treats a push as a wake-up and reconnects in the background ("offline resume", behind client and server gating) rather than reading content from it. mautrix-whatsapp registers the same way (same VAPID key) and parses pushes as `{"data": {"pn", "enc_iv", "enc_p", "enc_t", "enc_c"}}`, then reconnects with `connectReason: PUSH`, waits for the offline sync and answers `<iq xmlns="urn:xmpp:whatsapp:push" type="get"><pn>` with an `<ib><cat>` (observed 2026-10-30 in WhatsApp Web's bundles and mautrix `pkg/connector/client.go`)
- On the test account no push arrived while every companion was offline (messages stayed at one tick until a device connected): not to the probe registered with a tunnel endpoint (with and without `lg`/`lc` and `presence unavailable`), and not to genuine WhatsApp Web in Chrome with an `fcm.googleapis.com` subscription and its tab closed (observed 2026-10-30). WhatsApp isn't sending Web Push to web companions on this account, so push isn't available as a route; it may be rolled out per account. Helium can't test it at all (`pushManager.subscribe` fails: no push service)
- AB props for a web companion (probe `abprops`, `<iq xmlns="abt" type="get"><props protocol="1"/>`): `web_background_sync_v2` and `web_pwa_background_sync` aren't sent (client default off), `enable_call_links_push_notification` is 1 (observed 2026-10-30). Five capture runs got no push for messages; the call test was inconclusive because the phone wasn't reachable for calls either (its own VoIP push had lapsed until WhatsApp was restarted)
- WhatsApp Web's notifications for unseen chats when its tab opens come from the same mechanism the probe sees: the new connection receives the messages queued while every companion was offline (ending with `<ib><offline count=…/>`) and the page notifies for them. They aren't pushes, so they don't change the finding above
- Decision (2026-11-22): no WhatsApp notifications for now, chats only. The one remaining route, logging in from a background task to drain the offline queue and posting local notifications, was set aside
- `push-clear` removes a device's registration (`<clear platform="web"/>`, as WhatsApp Web does); the probe's was cleared after the tests
- How others do it (research 2026-10-30): desktop wrappers (ZapZap, whatsapp-for-linux, Ferdium) and whatsmeow/Baileys/purple-gowhatsapp setups stay connected; only Beeper pushes to a closed app. Its server runs beeper/push-receiver, which registers with Google's push service as Chrome (`c2dm/register3`, `app=org.chromium.linux`) for WhatsApp's FCM sender id `293955441834`, keeps the MCS socket open and re-pushes to Beeper's app, whose Notification Service Extension reconnects to fetch and decrypt (blog.beeper.com 2025-10-01; mautrix `pushCfg` lists `FCM{SenderID: "293955441834"}` without `Native`). The push's `enc_*` fields have no known key; it is a wake-up. Doing the same here needs an always-on receiver (not the stateless relay) and is unverified for this account given the web gating
- A Cloudflare version was considered and set aside (2026-10-30): checkin and `register3` are plain HTTPS; the MCS connection could be a Durable Object with an outbound `connect()` socket, kept open or reopened on an alarm every 30–60 s (Google queues pushes for offline devices). One connection can carry many registrations, so users could be sharded onto a few connections. It would be the app's first stateful backend, depends on impersonating Chrome to Google, needs the WhatsApp client to run inside the Notification Service Extension (state in the App Group), and was never shown to deliver on this account. Notifications stay foreground-only for now

### Restriction risk
- WhatsApp publishes no limits; reports (whatsmeow #810, #1074, Baileys #1869, #2441, #2707, mautrix docs) point at outreach to non-contacts (error 463, a time lock; don't retry), bursts and bots in groups, bulk number or picture lookups, and reconnect storms. Personal use of mautrix-style clients is described as low-risk
- So the app: sends only what the user types, to existing chats; looks up pictures lazily and slowly; keeps the web client version current (fetched at link and after a 405); backs off reconnects up to 60 s; stops on 402 (temporary ban), `replaced` and logouts instead of reconnecting
- Privacy tokens (whatsmeow tctoken.go; validated with the probe's `send`, 2026-11-22): contacts' tokens come from history sync conversations (`tcToken` 21, `tcTokenTimestamp` 22, `tcTokenSenderTimestamp` 28; 85 of 396 conversations had one, 32 unexpired) and `privacy_token` notifications (`<tokens><token type="trusted_contact" t>`), stored by LID user in `keys.json` (`privacyTokens`, optional so older key files decode). A token is valid while its time is at or after the start of the week bucket three before the current one (604800 s buckets). 1:1 sends, presence subscriptions and picture requests carry `<tctoken>`; after a 1:1 send our own token is issued (`<iq xmlns="privacy" type="set"><tokens><token jid t type="trusted_contact">`) once per weekly bucket. Tokens in this device's history sync from before this was built weren't kept; they come again with on-demand syncs and notifications
- Not yet ported from whatsmeow: `cstoken` (needs the NCT salt, which history sync didn't carry on the test account, observed 2026-11-22; it comes through app state `nct_salt_sync`), `unified_session`, reporting tokens
- Research (2026-11-22): whatsmeow's maintainers call restrictions "mostly a behavioral issue" (whatsmeow #810); warnings have hit low-volume accounts that only replied, or only stayed connected. Missing or expired privacy tokens make 1:1 sends fail with 463 (#1074) and picture lookups return random 401s (#950, which added the token). So `tctoken` comes before any new sending feature
- Rules for building the remaining features: media downloads only when a message is shown, one at a time per file and cached; on-demand history only when the user scrolls past what the device has, one request in flight; typing indications only while the user types in an open chat, throttled like WhatsApp Web; retry receipts capped per message; probe runs short, sequential and limited, and test sends, reactions, typing and receipts only in the self chat

### Swift client
- Layers: `WhatsAppProtobuf` (wire codec) → `WhatsAppProto`/`WhatsAppBinary` (models, node codec) and `WhatsAppCrypto`/`WhatsAppSignal` (XEdDSA on 5×51-bit limbs, Signal) → `WhatsAppSocket` (Noise) → `WhatsAppClient` (actor). `WhatsAppCodecTests` and `WhatsAppSignalTests` hold vectors generated by the probe: node bytes, client payloads, ADV re-encoding, messages built by Python, and sessions and sender keys encrypted on one side and decrypted on the other, in both directions. XEdDSA verify takes ~0.2 ms in release, ~3 ms in debug (simulator)
- Where the probe and whatsmeow differ on edge cases (agent suffix in signal addresses of `s.whatsapp.net` AD JIDs, empty strings), Swift follows the probe; none occur in observed traffic
- The client's reader resolves `<iq>` results and message `<ack>`s by id at once and queues everything else; stanzas are handled one at a time in arrival order (Signal state must advance in order), while handlers await requests such as `media_conn`
- Linking keeps reading after sending `pair-device-sign` until WhatsApp closes the stream (515). Closing the socket right after sending it made the phone report "Couldn't link device" while the app thought it was linked (observed 2026-10-30)
- Chat adapter: the store is the chat's whole history, so `fetch(.newest)` returns every stored message as records (`WhatsAppMessage` encoded, sort key the time in ms, version the last edit's time) with no earlier cursor; any other request throws `unsupported`. When the live connection stores something for the chat (or asks for a full reload) the adapter fetches again and applies it as the newest page. Names, avatars, the title and the read position are its saved state. Read receipts go for others' messages rendered as unread up to the one read, and the read position moves once WhatsApp accepted them
- Live connection: `WhatsAppLiveFeed` runs `WhatsAppClient.run` on `LiveConnection`, calling `opened()` on the login's `success` (so reconnects tell chats and the list to catch up) and stopping on `loggedOut`, `banned`, `replaced` and `notLinked`
- Device keys: Keychain item `whatsapp` (not synchronizable) per account. Signal state, prekeys, app state keys and hashes: `keys.json`; chats, names and up to 1000 messages per chat: `directory.json` and `messages/<chat>.json`, all under `Application Support/WhatsApp/<account id>/` (excluded from backup, complete until first unlock). Settings links with its own `WhatsAppClient`; the account's client picks the link up from the Keychain on its next run or list load
- `AccountServices` builds components before the Chats list exists; X and WhatsApp components without a live feed are rebuilt once it is set (`lacksLiveFeed`)
- Chats: history sync conversations seed chats (unread, archive, pin, mute, name) and messages; app state mutations update names (`contact`), mute, archive, pin and read; `<participating>` gives group subjects. A LID chat and a phone-number chat for one person merge into whichever exists (`canonicalChat`). Unread counts come from history until the app sees a read position (`readUpTo`, set by reading here, `read-self` receipts, `markChatAsRead` or writing from another device)
- Receiving sends `inactive` delivery receipts; reading sends `read` receipts per sender, or `read-self` when the account's `readreceipts` privacy setting is `none`
- Sending: one-to-one text only. Group chats show their messages but sending to them throws `unsupported` until group sending (sender key distribution to every participant device) is validated in the probe
- Media (validated with the probe's `media`, 2026-11-22): each message keeps its file reference (`WhatsAppMedia`: kind, MIME type, direct path, media key, both hashes, size, pixel size, length, voice flag, GIF/animated flag, file name, waveform, inline thumbnail) in the store. Downloads use the kind's HKDF info ("WhatsApp Image/Video/Audio/Document Keys"; stickers use the image keys) and `mms-type` (whatsmeow download.go). `wamedia://media/<account>/<chat>/<message id>?ext=` URLs (`WhatsAppMediaFiles.swift`) name no key; `WhatsAppMediaURLProtocol` asks the account's client for the file only when a bubble, viewer, player or Quick Look loads it, and caches it decrypted under Caches/whatsapp-media (purgeable). Photos are `.image`, videos `.video` (the inline thumbnail as poster, `gifPlayback` as GIF), voice messages and audio `.voice` with WhatsApp's 64-level waveform (0–100), stickers `.sticker` (WebP), documents `.file`; a message without a direct path (never uploaded) shows a "No longer available" card. Observed: files from history sync carry no inline thumbnails; voice messages are `audio/ogg; codecs=opus`
- Expired media: the media hosts answer 403 (also 404/410) for old files (a 77-day-old voice message, observed 2026-11-22). The client then sends a media retry (`<receipt type="server-error">` with the request AES-GCM-encrypted under HKDF(mediaKey, "WhatsApp Media Retry Notification"), the message id as associated data, and `<rmr jid from_me [participant]>`), waits up to 30 s for the phone's `mediaretry` notification, decrypts its `MediaRetryNotification` and keeps the new direct path with the message. Error code 2 means the phone no longer has the file. One request per file, only when it's opened
- History on demand (validated with the probe's `history`, 2026-11-22): a `PeerDataOperationRequestMessage` (`HISTORY_SYNC_ON_DEMAND`, chat JID, oldest message id/fromMe/timestamp in seconds, count) as a peer message to the user's own phone (`category="peer"`, `push_priority="high_force"`, `privacy_sensitive="1"`, encrypted for the phone's device only). The phone answers with an `ON_DEMAND` history sync of the messages before the anchor (exclusive); those leave the chat's unread count, archive and mute alone. The adapter pages back with it (cursor = the oldest stored message id), stops when a request brings nothing, and the client spaces requests at least 5 s apart. The store keeps up to 5000 messages per chat so fetched pages stay. Messages stored before media was kept are recovered by a request anchored on the newest message, once per anchor: the newest itself only comes back once a later message gives a newer anchor. The phone ignores `PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE_RESEND` for a message this device didn't fail to decrypt (no answer twice, probe `resend`, observed 2026-11-22), so it can't fetch one message by key. A media message without a direct path (one whose upload never finished: no pixel size either) gets media retry error 3 from the phone (observed 2026-11-22) and shows as unavailable
- Replies (validated with the probe's `send --reply-to`): `extendedTextMessage` with `contextInfo` (`stanzaID`, `participant` the quoted sender's phone JID, `quotedMessage` its text). Received replies keep `quotedId` and `quotedSender`; the chat draws them from `replyToId`
- Reactions (probe `react`): `reactionMessage` (key with the chat, `fromMe`, id and the sender as `participant` in groups; text the emoji, empty to remove; `senderTimestampMS`), sent like a message. Received ones (and history sync's `WebMessageInfo.reactions`) are folded into the target's `reactions` by reactor JID; WhatsApp keeps one per person, so a different emoji replaces the user's and the same one takes it back
- Typing and presence (probe `typing`): while a WhatsApp chat is open the client sends `<presence type="available" name>` (the first open chat; `unavailable` when the last closes, as WhatsApp Web is online only while focused) and subscribes to the person's presence with their token. `<chatstate to><composing/>` goes at most every 10 s while the draft changes and `<paused/>` 5 s after the last change, when the draft empties or a message is sent. Incoming `<chatstate from [participant]><composing/>|<paused/>` show as typing for up to 25 s; the user's own typing comes back in their self chat under their LID and is dropped
- The account's row in Settings: the user's WhatsApp name (the device's push name, else the name the store has for their own JID from history sync and app state) and their own profile picture (`WhatsAppClient.ownProfile`), refreshed whenever Settings loads, since the picture's signed URL expires; the number names the account until then
- Member lists: `<participating>` already returns each group's participants (`type` admin or superadmin) and description (`<description><body>`); the store keeps them (`groupMembers`, `groupDescriptions`, optional so older directories decode). The adapter adopts `ChatMembersListing` (in `ChatDestination.swift`, for any network without its own details screen), and `ChatInfoView` shows the description, member count and a searchable member list, owner and admins first; the first 30 members' pictures are looked up like chat pictures
- Retries (whatsmeow retry.go; validated 2026-11-22 by making the probe forget its session with the app's device, `forget-session`, then sending from the app to the self chat: the probe's receipt #1 got a resend on the old session, #2 with keys got a prekey message it decrypted). A message this device can't decrypt gets `<receipt type="retry">` with `<retry count id t v="1">` and `<registration>`, plus from the second time `<keys>` (type 5, identity, a fresh one-time prekey, the signed prekey, `device-identity`); at most four per message. The first time, the phone is also asked for it after 5 s (`PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE_RESEND` peer request with the message key); its answer (`peerDataOperationRequestResponseMessage` results' `placeholderMessageResendResponse.webMessageInfoBytes`) is stored like a live message. The newest 256 messages sent from this device are kept in memory, so a retry receipt for one is answered by encrypting it again for the requesting device (a `DeviceSentMessage` for the user's own devices), from the receipt's keys or a new session when there's none, `<enc count>`, `device_fanout="false"`; at most ten per device and message
- Sending media (whatsmeow upload.go; validated with the probe's `send-media`, 2026-11-22, and a photo from the app to the self chat that the probe received): a fresh 32-byte media key, AES-CBC under its HKDF keys for the kind, the first 10 bytes of HMAC-SHA256(iv + ciphertext) appended, POSTed to `https://<first media_conn host>/mms/<mms-type>/<base64url enc SHA-256>?auth=<media_conn auth>&token=…`; the JSON answer gives `url` and `direct_path`. The message carries both hashes, length, mediaKeyTimestamp, pixel size and a 100 px JPEG preview for photos and videos (ImageIO, AVAssetImageGenerator), the file name for documents, and for voice messages `ptt`, the length and 64 levels (0–100) with the Ogg Opus file (`OggOpusEncoder`, as for Telegram). Photos go as photos (GIFs and SVGs as files), videos as videos, anything else as a document; a caption goes with the media. The sent file goes straight into the media cache, so the sender's bubble doesn't download it again
- Group sending (whatsmeow sendGroup; the sender-key crypto is checked against the probe in `WhatsAppSignalTests`, and live on 2026-11-22: a message from the app to a test group with only the user, which the probe, as another of the user's devices, decrypted): the message is encrypted once with the user's sender key for the group (`skmsg`, addressed by the user's LID device), and every participant device (from `<participating>` members' devices, the user's own included, minus this one) gets the `SenderKeyDistributionMessage` in a 1:1 message on every send, as whatsmeow does. The node carries `phash` ("2:" and base64 of the first 6 bytes of SHA-256 over the sorted device JIDs) and `addressing_mode="lid"` for LID-addressed groups. A retry for a group message goes as a 1:1 message with the distribution alongside. Groups where only admins send (`<announcement/>`) are read-only unless the user is an admin; groups without stored members are read-only until `<participating>` has listed them
- Every send's node `type` and `enc` `mediatype` follow whatsmeow's getTypeFromMessage/getMediaTypeFromMessage: `text`, `reaction`, or `media` with image, video, gif, audio, ptt, document or sticker
- Groups created or joined in the last 14 days that history didn't include are listed from their `creation` time ("No messages yet"), as WhatsApp lists a new group; older empty groups aren't. Known gap: the Chats list showed such a group only after a relaunch, not when `<participating>` returned it
- Profile pictures: `<iq xmlns="w:profile:picture" type="get" target=JID><picture query="url" type="preview"/>` (whatsmeow `GetProfilePictureInfo`) returns a signed `pps.whatsapp.net` URL that loads without auth and expires per its `oe` parameter (hex Unix seconds, ~10 days); 404 `item-not-found` means no picture and 401 `not-authorized` a hidden one (observed 2026-10-30, probe `avatar`). Looked up for the 20 newest chats at login and for a chat and its senders when it opens, one per second, cached in `directory.json` (none-found for 7 days), refreshed a day before expiry or after a `picture` notification. whatsmeow attaches the contact's privacy token (`tctoken`) to picture requests; without it some return 401 (whatsmeow PR #950) — not sent yet
- Status updates arrive as messages from `status@broadcast`; they aren't stored as chats. LID chats fold into the phone-number chat once a mapping is known (history, message attributes, group participants, and app state `contact` actions, which carry `lidJID`)
- One `WhatsAppClient` per account (`WhatsAppClient.client(accountId:keychain:)`), shared by Settings and the live connection: two instances once saved their own directory over each other's and lost chats and names
- Badge: `Resources/WhatsAppBadge*.png` is WhatsApp's mark from Simple Icons (CC0, `simple-icons@13.21.0/icons/whatsapp.svg`) in white at 60% of the badge on `#25D366`, rendered in headless Chrome as Discord's is (ImageMagick and CoreSVG mangle the compact paths) and scaled to 16, 32 and 48 px; listed in `stupid-app.yml` like every resource

## Chats & chat screen

### Live connections (`LiveConnection`)
- Every pushed-update connection runs on `LiveConnection<Event>` (Services/LiveConnection.swift): X Chat's socket (`XChatLiveFeed`), the Discord gateway (`DiscordGateway`), LinkedIn's realtime stream (`LinkedInLiveFeed`) and each Apron server's (`ApronLiveFeed`). It owns the foreground lifecycle, reconnecting (exponential backoff from 1 s to 60 s, ±50% jitter, reset once a connection opens, or after it stays up `stableAfter`, 120 s for Apron, never sooner than a delay the server asked for), stopping on errors that won't heal (`LiveOutcome.stop`: Discord close 4004, a signed-out LinkedIn session, a denied or ended Apron sign-in), the observer list, and `LiveUpdate.catchUp` when a later connection opens or the network asks (X's pull instruction).
- A network supplies only its session: open, call `opened()` once up, `deliver(_:)` events, return or throw when closed. Protocol keep-alives stay in the session (X's 30 s keep-alive, Discord's heartbeat, LinkedIn's silence check).
- Accounts expose `AccountComponents.liveConnection`; `AppContainer.startLiveFeeds`/`stopLiveFeeds` start and stop every one without naming networks, and `shutDown` stops it when the account goes.
- Telegram stays on its own MTProto session (reconnecting and resending live in its protocol layer), and Apron chats still observe the server's shared connection (`ApronService`) directly; only `ApronLiveFeed`'s retry loop moved.
- Chats: `ChatLiveUpdating.onCatchUp` is set by the framework; adapters call it on `.catchUp` and the framework loads the newest messages (`loadNewest`), instead of each adapter refetching.

### StupidChatKit
- Every network-free chat file (models and view models, drafts, snapshots, media files, stickers, the chat screen and its UIKit views, `Emoji.json`) is the `StupidChatKit` package in its own public repository, `https://github.com/stephancill/stupid-chatkit`, which other apps can use too. The app depends on it from 0.4.0 (`Package.swift`) at the version pinned in `Package.resolved`; `stupid-app` builds package dependencies from 0.0.27. Its resource bundle in the app is `StupidChatKit_StupidChatKit.bundle`. It can't import the app, so the rule that shared chat code never names a network is enforced by the compiler.
- Why its own repository rather than a product of this package: another app depending on stupid-social would clone this whole repository, and the kit gets its own versions and tests.
- What the app used to reach for directly goes through `ChatKit`, set once in `StupidSocialApp.init`: `onEvent` (`ChatEvent`s, which `AppMetrics.chat` maps to the same event names as before), `networkIcon` (`NetworkBadgeIcon`) and `openAppLink` (`NotificationRouter`, for Apron links).
- `ChatNetwork` is a string (`RawRepresentable`, so it encodes as `SocialNetwork` did and cached rows still decode). `ChatDestination.swift` (Core) gives it a static member per `SocialNetwork` (`chat.network == .telegram` still reads), `socialNetwork` (traps on an unknown id: the app only makes networks from `SocialNetwork`) and `displayName`. A `ChatListProvider` names its network as `chatNetwork`, since several providers are also `NotificationSource`s with `network: SocialNetwork`.
- The Chats list types are generic over the app's destination (`ChatSummary<Destination>`, `ChatListPage`, `ChatListSource`, `ChatListViewModel`, `ChatListProvider<Destination>`). Core declares `ChatDestination` (the old `ChatSummary.Destination`, same cases, so cached lists and reminders decode) and typealiases with the kit's names; Core re-exports the kit (`@_exported import`), and its aliases shadow the kit's generic types in files that import Core. A file that imports both explicitly gets "ambiguous for type lookup", so app files and tests import only Core (or only the kit, as `ChatDetailsHeader` does).
- Reminders reach the chat through the `ChatReminders` protocol (`ChatViewModel.reminders`), which `ChatReminderTarget` conforms to; `ChatReminderDelay` holds the presets. A `ChatListProvider` that isn't connected throws a `ChatNotConnectedError` (`SourceError` conforms; `.notConfigured` lists nothing).
- `StupidSocialPush` depends on the kit too (for `ChatUserColor`, `InitialsAvatar` and `SpoilerText.mask`), so the notification extension links it.
- Framework tests and the sticker comparison scripts live in the kit's repository. App tests run against a simulator destination (`-destination id=<udid>`): the generic simulator destination also links x86_64, which the vendored Juicebox XCFramework lacks.

### Chat store
- Every chat reads from the kit's `ChatStore` (design in the kit's `docs/DESIGN.md` § Storage). Records per network: Telegram `TelegramMessage` (sort key the message id; cursor `offset_id`), Apron `ApronMessage` and reaction sets (above), Discord `DiscordMessage` in Discord's JSON (snowflake; `before`/`after`/`around`, so Discord jumps to old messages), X Chat the event's Thrift with its decrypted `MessageEntryHolder` (`XChatAdapter.StoredEvent`; reactions, edits and deletes related to their message; stored decrypted so chats open without keys), LinkedIn `LinkedInMessage` (`deliveredAt` ms; earlier pages by anchor time), Instagram `InstagramDirectMessage`, Spotify `SpotifyChatMessage` and Bluesky `BlueskyChatMessage` (sent time in ms; opaque cursors), demo by id from its catalog.
- Rows name their latest message (`ChatSummary.latestMessageId`): Telegram `top_message`, Discord `last_message_id`, X the newest message event, LinkedIn/Instagram/Spotify/Bluesky the inbox's last message, Apron the page's greatest `message_id` (any kind, not just the latest with text, which could leave a newer image-only message unstored). Live row updates set it too.
- Telegram sends updates to one connection per authorization: a simulator with copied credentials gets none while the original app runs (the open chat then only updates on its 30 s poll), observed 2026-11-12. Quit the original app to test pushed updates on a copy
- `stupid-app run --simulator` builds get the App Group container on the simulator (the inbox is there), though `simctl get_app_container … groups` lists none (observed 2026-11-12)
- The store is in the app's own container (`ChatStore.defaultURL()`). Builds 204–216 kept it in the App Group so the extension could write to it, and iOS killed the app when it was suspended during a store read (`0xdead10cc`, build 214, observed 2026-11-20): the rule in § Storage. The extension now writes each pushed message as its own JSON file to `chat-inbox/` in the App Group (atomic, `completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`, named by time so they apply in order) and posts `tech.stupid.StupidSocial.chatStoreDidChange`. `ChatInbox` (app) moves the inbox into the store at launch, on `willEnterForeground` (before a tapped notification opens its chat) and on that Darwin notification; a file is removed even when its write fails, and failures report `chat_inbox_import_failed`. `openForApp` deletes the old App Group store files once (removing a file takes no lock); their history loads again from each network
- Opening from a notification: the extension leaves pushed messages in the inbox before the tap. Apron pushes are whole messages (`ApronPushNotification.record`). Telegram pushes carry `custom.msg_id`, the sender and, for text, the text, which become a provisional `TelegramMessage` (`TelegramPushedMessage`) in every stored chat with that peer, since a push doesn't name its account; the chat shows it at once and Telegram's copy replaces it.
- Every inbox entry is imported provisional (`ChatStore.writeProvisional`), Apron's too: a push says nothing about what came before it. Apron's were written as history, which after a trip to the background started a newest chunk of their own holding the row's latest message, so the chat counted as current: loading ahead skipped it, opening didn't fetch, and it opened on the pushed messages alone with earlier history filling in above (a jump). Provisional records never make a chat current, so loading ahead fetches these chats when the app opens. Only chats already stored take a provisional message
- A pushed message can end up provisional under newer messages the network stored (observed once 2026-11-22 on the simulator: two of three pushed Apron messages stored, the third left provisional; not reproduced in seven runs, and neither the live socket, which closes in the background, nor the server, which replays nothing, explains it). The row's latest message was then stored, so the chat counted as current and nothing ever fetched it. Since stupid-chatkit 0.10.2 a chat holding a provisional message is never current, so loading ahead fetches it, and a newest page keeps pushed messages older than it reaches as stored
- Build 205 made the feed scroll slowly after a fresh start: the store heard its own Darwin notifications and redrew every chat model on each write, including the ~30 the Chats list loads ahead (23% of the main thread during a cold start on the simulator, 3% after the fix in the kit). Chats loaded ahead now never read or observe the store
- Only the app opens the store and checks the record format (`SharedChatStore.openForApp`, in its own defaults).
- Records clear when `SharedChatStore.recordFormat` changes; the store survives app updates otherwise, and Settings → Storage shows its size and clears it

### Shared chat architecture
- `ChatViewModel` (StupidChatKit) is network-agnostic: published state and display models (`ChatMessage`, `ChatUser`, `ChatEmbed`, `ChatReaction`, `ChatReactor`, `ChatAttachment`), overridable actions, capability flags (`canReply`, `canReact`, `offersAttachments`, `mentionPattern`, `showsSenderNames`). Its only subclass is `AdapterChatViewModel`, which drives a network's `ChatAdapter`; the base's no-op actions are what an adapter without that ability gets. `ApronChatAdapter` renders stored `ApronMessage` snapshots as `ChatMessage`s (embed mapping incl. the allowed-media-origin rule lives in the adapter)
- `ChatUIKitView`/`ChatViewController` (cells/helpers named `Chat*`) take any `ChatViewModel`; cells re-render when their `ChatMessage` or group layout changes, not on `log_id`. `ChatView` = SwiftUI screen (title pill, connect/disconnect, reconnect on activation); `ApronChatView` adds members/profiles; `NetworkChatView` hosts other networks. `ChatViewModel.network` puts `ChatKit.networkIcon` (`NetworkBadgeIcon` here) before the subtitle in `ChatTitlePill` (before the title when there is no subtitle either; subtitles never name the network, since the icon already shows it), and `titleAvatar` puts a `ChatAvatarImage` before the title: the one-to-one partner when the adapter sets `avatarUserId` (from `ChatInfo`), else the chat's own picture from its Chats row (`showRowAvatar`, called by `ChatsView.opened` with the row's `avatarURL`/`avatarColorKey`), so groups, rooms and every network show what their row shows
- `publish(history:reactions:)` sets reactions before messages, and the screen keeps totals for messages not yet shown, so first render includes reactions (separate subscriptions rendered bare bubbles that then grew). Apron applies reactions first itself; Telegram, Instagram, Spotify, X Chat use `publish`
- Adapters (`AdapterChatViewModel.swift`): `ChatAdapter` is one conversation with no UI state (info, `fetch` of pages as records, `render` of stored records, `send`, read position, per-chat state as `Data`); optional abilities are separate protocols (`ChatReactionSending`, `ChatReadReceipts`, `ChatAudioPreviewing`, `ChatLiveUpdating` for pushed updates, `ChatReferenceResolving` for quoted messages outside the loaded ones, `ChatReadOnlyAccess` for chats the user can only read, as `ChatViewModel.isReadOnly`, `ChatReactionChoices` for networks that take only some emoji, as `ChatViewModel.reactionChoices`), so `canReact`, audio previews, following and quotes follow from conformance rather than a flag that could disagree with the code. `AdapterChatViewModel` (final) drives one: restores its state, reads the stored window and places the divider before the first render, connects (fetching only when the store isn't current), polls at `pollInterval`, sends `markRead`'s returned send, and republishes when the adapter calls `onChange` (a profile or track loading later). Every network uses it (`*ChatAdapter`, next to their clients). `AdapterChatViewModel<Adapter>` is generic so a network's own screens reach their adapter typed (`ApronChatView` reads `viewModel.adapter.room`). Apron also implements `ChatWindowing` (older windows, jump, latest), `ChatTyping`, `ChatMentioning`, `ChatCommanding` (a command waits for the server instead of showing a sending copy, since it may post nothing) and `ChatLinking`; live adapters report a closed connection through `onConnectionLost` (an error shows with Reconnect; without one the app closed it and reconnects when active) and background failures, such as an upload after its message posted, through `onError`. Apron's members, profiles and notification level are `ApronRoomViewModel`, which the adapter keeps current and the members and profile screens observe. `connect()` is guarded while connecting and while open, so the `.task` and app-activation calls can't start following twice. Telegram's member list has its own `TelegramMembersViewModel` (member state is screen state, not adapter state), built by the same factory as the chat; its 30 s poll reloads the dialog with the page, which also re-reads "last seen". Spotify's "seen" goes through the shared read-marker sending too
- Shared behavior lives in `ChatViewModel`, and networks use it instead of their own copies (the target shape and migration status are in TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md § Chat Framework):
  - `chatKey` (`<account id>|<network>:<id>`; Apron's is `apron:<server>|<room>`, since each server is its own account) names the chat for drafts and in the store. The account is part of it because two accounts can be in the same chat, such as a Telegram group
  - drafts: `ChatDraftStore`, `UserDefaults` `chatDrafts` by `chatKey`, for every network. It replaced Apron's `apronDrafts`, whose drafts weren't migrated
  - polling: `startPolling(every:)` / `stopPolling()` call the network's `poll()` (Spotify 10 s, Instagram 5 minutes behind its realtime socket, Telegram 30 s as a fallback to pushed updates); `isPolling` doubles as "already connected"
  - unread: networks override only `isAfterReadPosition(_:)`; `isUnread`, `updateUnreadCount()` and `placeUnreadDivider()` build on it. Apron overrides `updateUnreadCount()`, since it counts its kept snapshots rather than a possibly older `history` window
  - `scheduleReadMarker` sends the read position as soon as it moves (Apron, Telegram, Instagram, X); while one mark is in flight only the newest read waits for it, and a failed mark is retried after 3 s and 15 s unless a newer one replaces it. A 600 ms delay used to come first, and leaving the chat within it dropped the mark, so the chat stayed unread on the network. Spotify marks a whole chat seen at once and doesn't debounce
  - read positions move only once the network accepts the mark: every adapter's `markRead` send moves its cursor (Telegram `readInboxMaxId`, Discord `readId` and the gateway's read state, Bluesky/Instagram/LinkedIn `readAt`, Spotify `seenUpTo`, X Chat `readUntil`, Apron's server cursor in `ApronReadStore`) after the request succeeds, never before, and the framework keeps what the user read itself so the count drops at once. Moving it first lost reads for good: a failed or never-sent mark left the saved position past it, so later visits had nothing to send while the network, and the Chats row built from it, still counted the chat unread. Apron servers without `activity` have no cursor to tell, so the stored one moves at once
  - a message seen before the chat connects is read once it has (the kit's `readOnConnect`), so a quick look at a loaded-ahead chat still reads it
  - links: `link(toMessage:)` (Copy Link), `linkTarget(for:)` (links to this chat's messages or members) and `linkBaseURL` (relative links), so `ChatUIKitView` never casts to a network's view model
- Chats load ahead: `AppContainer` sets `ChatListViewModel.prefetcher` to build a row's chat the way opening it does (`unfollowedChatViewModel(for:)`, shared with `chatViewModel(for:)`), every network included. History loads don't move the read position on any network (only `markRead`, which the chat screen calls, does), so loading ahead sends no read receipts; checked in the simulator 2026-11-10 (unread counts unchanged).
- Stored messages show in the first frame, at the divider when they reach it; history the chat fetches joins them without moving the scroll position, and the divider moves when history replaced the message it sat on. Deleted messages never count as unread. Covered by the kit's tests
- `sendOptimistically` (all networks): message shows at once with a clock by its time; the network's copy replaces it; networks set `history`, not `messages`. A sending photo uses the media layout; the confirmed message keeps the picked image file and aspect ratio so only the time badge changes
- Several attachments (`ChatViewModel.attachments`, up to `maxAttachments` = 10): Photos multi-select (`PHPickerConfiguration.selection = .ordered`, limit = room left), Files (`allowsMultipleSelection`), drops and pastes all append to the composer, which shows them as a sideways strip of tiles with a remove button each. Each file sends as its own message through `send(_:)` (`ChatOutgoing` keeps one attachment), the first with the text and the reply; all show as sending at once and go in order, and a failure stops there and puts the unsent files back. Telegram albums (`grouped_id`) aren't sent: that would be an optional ability
- Read receipts (`ChatReadReceipts`): an adapter that conforms says per message whether it is `.sent`, `.seen` (someone else read it; in a group, anyone) or neither (`nil`, such as Telegram's Saved Messages). `AdapterChatViewModel` collects them for the user's messages into `receipts` on each change, and `ChatViewModel.receipt(for:)` leaves out sending, deleted and notice messages. The bubble shows the status after the time, Telegram-style: in chats with receipts (`showsReadReceipts`) the clock while sending, then one check once sent, then two once seen (`checkmark` at the time's size, the second overlapping the first; SF Symbols has no double check). They sit in one fixed-width slot (`statusSlot`, as wide as two checks), so the time never moves and the bubble never resizes between states; becoming seen only reconfigures the cell (`applyReceipts`). On the same row (keyed by the item key, since the sending copy and the confirmed message have different ids) a change animates with layer animations, which survive `apply`'s `performWithoutAnimation`: the clock fades, a check pops in (the reaction chips' `popIn`), and the second check pops in beside the first; a row that loses its status (Saved Messages) slides the time into the slot's space. Chats without receipts keep the clock before the time, which fades once sent. The text reserves the time row's width with a clear image attachment (an attachment without an image draws a placeholder line). Networks:
  - Telegram: the dialog's `read_outbox_max_id`, at load and on the 30 s poll, plus `updateReadHistoryOutbox` / `updateReadChannelOutbox` pushed on the open session (`addReadOutboxObserver`, which carries `max_id`; the general update observer only reloads history, not the dialog). Kept in the adapter's saved state. Not in Saved Messages (`TelegramDialogState.isSavedMessages`). Probe `telegram-client.py receipts`
  - X Chat: the furthest read marker from anyone but the viewer, from `latest_read_events_per_participant` and live/paged read-marker events. People with read receipts off send none, so their chats stay at sent (as on X). Probe `xchat-client.py read-receipts` (observed 2026-10-31: 1:1 chats with two markers show the newest own message seen)
  - Instagram: the latest `slide_read_receipts` watermark of anyone but the viewer, against the message's time, refreshed on the 20 s poll and kept in the adapter's saved state. Probe `instagram-web-client.py direct-receipts`; not yet run live (the probe's session was logged out)
  - Demo mode: Telegram, Instagram and X demo chats show earlier messages seen and new ones sent
  - Apron: the furthest read cursor of any member but the user (any `ownUserIds`). `ApronConnection.readCursors` keeps every cursor the connection receives, per room and user, forward only, since the ones sent after `room_list` arrive at sign-in before any chat observes the connection; the adapter reads them on load and on each other user's `activity` read. A room shows receipts only once another member's cursor for that room has arrived, since servers choose the rooms: the Go server shares cursors in every room, `ext.settings.read_receipts: "private"` (EXTENSIONS.md; apron-server-cloudflare, private rooms of at most 10 members) only small private rooms, older servers none, and elsewhere one check on every message would read as nobody having read them. The rule needs neither the setting nor the server's member limit. Kept in the snapshot (`othersRead`). No receipt in a room with only you. Covered by `ApronReadReceiptsTests`
  - Bluesky and Spotify have none: Bluesky's convo API exposes no read state, and Spotify's chat API has no per-person seen state the app reads
- Editing (`ChatEditing`: `editableText(of:)` says which of the user's messages can be edited and from what text, `edit(_:text:)` saves): `ChatViewModel.startEditing` puts the composer's draft, attachments and reply aside (`composerBeforeEdit`) and fills it with the editable text; `draft` isn't written to `ChatDraftStore` while `editingMessage` is set, and typing isn't sent. `send()` in edit mode calls `editOptimistically`: unchanged text just ends editing; otherwise `pendingEdits` shows the new text (`ChatMessage.editing(to:)`, `isEdited`, ranges dropped) until `history` changes the message from the version when the edit began (`editBases`), since the network's copy may read differently; a failure drops it and, if the composer is empty, starts editing again with the edited text. The UI reuses the reply bar ("Edit Message", Cancel Editing), shows a checkmark Save button, and turns off attaching (button, drops). `ChatMessage.isEdited` shows "edited" before the time in the bubble. Tests: `AdapterChatViewModelTests` (`FakeEditingAdapter`)
- Deleting (`ChatDeleting`: `canDelete(_:)` says which of the user's messages can be deleted, `delete(_:)` deletes for everyone): `ChatViewModel.canDelete` also needs an own, confirmed, not deleted message in a connected chat that isn't read-only or saving an edit. The long-press menu's last item is a red Delete, confirmed with a centered alert ("Delete Message?", Delete Message / Cancel) as the stupid iOS design rules ask for destructive actions. Not optimistic: the message goes once the network confirms, and a failure shows the error. Apron (servers with `edit`) sends a save with only `message_id`, `room_id` and `deleted: true` (§4.4) and shows the broadcast tombstone as "Message deleted" (probe `apron-web-client.py delete`; not yet run live). Telegram deletes the user's outgoing, non-service messages, an album's other messages with it, with `messages.deleteMessages` `revoke` (validated in Saved Messages with the probe's `delete`, observed 2026-11-02: `messages.affectedMessages`, `pts_count` 1) or `channels.deleteMessages` in supergroups and channels (validated 2026-11-02 in a supergroup, from the app and with the probe), and drops them from `loaded` at once. Messages deleted anywhere else leave the chat too: `TelegramClient.addDeleteObserver` reports the ids in `updateDeleteMessages` (per account, so the adapter drops only ids it has) and `updateDeleteChannelMessages`, and each page of the newest messages (`loadNewest`, the refresh after updates) drops messages loaded before the fetch that fall in its id range (all of them when there are no more) but aren't in it, which covers deletions while the chat was closed and stale snapshots. Messages loaded during the fetch, such as one just sent, stay. Validated 2026-11-02 in Saved Messages: a probe `send` showed in the open chat and its probe `delete` removed it within 4 s, and in a supergroup a probe-deleted message left the open chat within 1 s (`updateDeleteChannelMessages`); opening the chat dropped messages deleted earlier, matching the probe's `history`. Other networks don't offer Delete yet. Tests: `AdapterChatViewModelTests` (`FakeDeletingAdapter`)
- No jump on send confirmation: a pushed update can bring the sent message into history before send returns its id. `composeMessages` matches a "sending" message to a new own message with the same text not in history at send time; the view keeps the confirmed message under its "sending" item key (`sentLocalIds`, `itemKey(for:)`) so the cell is reconfigured, not replaced; a swap adding no row settles at the bottom without animation

### Chat screen behavior
- Voice messages (`ChatVoiceRecorder`, `ChatVoiceNote`): the composer's microphone shows where the network's adapter is `ChatVoiceSending`, or takes attachments (`canAttach`), and there's nothing to send. Without `ChatVoiceSending` the recording goes through `send(_:)` as `Voice Message.m4a` (`audio/mp4`), converted by `ChatVoiceFile` with `AVAssetExportPresetAppleM4A`, as the user asked for networks without voice messages (Apron); the sending copy still shows as a voice message; holding it records 48 kHz mono 16-bit PCM to a temporary `.caf` with `AVAudioRecorder` (`.playAndRecord`, after `AVAudioApplication.requestRecordPermission`; `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` in Info.plist), and the text row gives way to a pulsing dot with the time, "Slide to cancel" and a red microphone. Release sends; sliding left `min(120 pt, 40 % of the pill)` cancels, and the label follows the finger as far as the time, fading. The long press (0.15 s, unlimited `allowableMovement` so a finger already sliding still records) is on the composer, not the button: the button hides with the text row while recording. Releasing before the microphone starts (the permission prompt) records nothing. A plain tap shows a hint; with VoiceOver it starts recording and the row shows delete and send buttons instead. Hold-to-record replaced an earlier tap-to-record at the user's request. `stupidsim swipe` with slow steps (100 × 40 ms) exercises hold-and-slide and `tap --hold-milliseconds` hold-and-release `ChatVoiceNote(recordingAt:)` reads the length and 100 peak levels scaled to the loudest; the sending copy shows that waveform and `audioPreviewURL` plays the recording until the network's copy arrives (`sendingVoiceFiles`). A voice message keeps the draft, like a sticker, and a failed one is lost. In the simulator, `simctl privacy grant microphone` relaunches the app
- The initial layout and scroll (`performInitialScrollIfNeeded`, from `viewDidLayoutSubviews`) run in `UIView.performWithoutAnimation`: they happen inside the navigation push's animation, which otherwise slid the cells in from their zero frames
- Reactions: `applyReactionTotals` reconfigures changed bubbles in one 0.3 s ease-out animation pinned to the bottom (`applyKeepingBottom(_:animated:)`); not before the initial scroll or while anchored to the unread divider. `apply(animatingDifferences: false)` lays out with animations off even inside that block, so the moves it makes are carried into the animation: visible cells' frames are put back and set again in the block (cells below a grown one jumped down), and a short chat's top inset (`updateTopInsetForShortContent`, deferred during the apply) follows in the block (the list jumped up before the bubble grew). `animatingDifferences: true` instead slid the bubble's text sideways. The reaction row's bottom constraint is low priority: configure runs at the cell's old height, and a squeezed container squeezed the chips to nothing, so they stretched open from the bubble's left edge. While a message's reactions change, the chip row and its container take their frames at once (their layers' delegates return no action for `position` and `bounds`): configure lays the row out early, so its size was new while its position still animated, and a widened row started half its growth to the left and slid back, and first chips slid in from where the hidden container sat. A chip new to the shown message pops in like Telegram's (`ChatMessageReactionsFooterContentNode`: scale from 0.01 on a spring, fade over 0.2 s) and a removed one shrinks out (to 0.01 and transparent over 0.2 s), both with layer animations: configure runs inside `apply`'s `performWithoutAnimation`, which finishes UIView animations at once. The removed chip leaves the row, so a still image of it (`drawHierarchy`) shrinks over the bubble; a `snapshotView` mirrors the live chip and went blank. Scrolled up, reactions grow upward (lowest visible message stays put, `lowestVisibleItem()`). Other growth (quotes, mention names) applies without animation
- Reaction chips always get their tap action; `ChatViewController.toggleReaction` checks `canReact` at tap time. `canReact` is set once the chat connects, after the snapshot and the first `adapterDidChange()` have configured the visible cells, and nothing reconfigures cells when it changes, so chips that took it at configure time stayed inert until their message was reconfigured (noticed in Apron, whose rooms open from a snapshot)
- Changes while a context menu shows wait until its dismissal ends (`willDisplayContextMenu` / `willEndContextMenuInteraction`): the dismissal preview kept the old bubble size and cut the grown bubble
- Every configure rebuilds media, embed and reaction views; the cell lays out those stacks once without animation after rebuilding (else zero-frame views animated in from the corner inside `applyKeepingBottom`)
- Context menu: three quick reactions + More Reactions smiley icon at the end; a fifth small element wraps onto a full-width row. The quick ones are `ChatViewModel.menuReactions`: 👍 ❤️ 😂 🔥 😮 🙏 in order, or for a network with `ChatReactionChoices`, those it takes, written as it writes them (Telegram's ❤ without U+FE0F, so the chip's on state matches), then its own first choices (Telegram: 👍 ❤ 🔥)
- More Reactions on a lifted message grows the reaction row into a grid in place, as Telegram does (`ChatMessageFocusController.expandReactions`, `ChatReactionGridView`): a search field (sized like the emoji keyboard's: 40 pt tall, 9 pt side insets, measured on iOS 27) over the chat's non-hidden custom emoji (by category, uncategorized as "Custom") and then every emoji by category, or for a network with `ChatReactionChoices` just its choices (`ChatReactionGrid.sections`, tested in `ChatReactionGridTests`). The user's reactions are marked; a pick closes the overlay and toggles it. The grid goes where there is more room beside the message (above when it fits 280 pt), so the message stays in view, over it only when neither side has 200 pt, and shrinks above the keyboard while searching. The actions panel fades out. Search matches a custom emoji's key and tags, and Unicode emoji whose name or alias is the query, then names with it as a word, then words starting with it. The native context menu (a right-click beside a bubble on a Mac) still opens the `ChatReactionPickerViewController` sheet
- Emoji data (`EmojiCatalog`, `Resources/Emoji.json`, 252 KB): gemoji-format entries (emoji, name, category, aliases, tags, `ios_version`) from Elegant Emoji Picker's Unicode 17 list (MIT, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). Emoji newer than the running iOS are left out, since they draw as empty boxes. The picker itself was tried and dropped: its sheet didn't look native, and it can't show custom emoji images
- Tapping the message list dismisses the keyboard (`cancelsTouchesInView = false` so links/cards/images/reactions still get taps)
- Quotes (Markdown messages only; Instagram/Spotify plain text unchanged): `ChatAttributedText.formatQuotes` drops `>`, indents 12 pt, dims to 70%, tags `.chatQuote`; `ChatLinkLabel` draws a 3 pt bar per run of quoted lines as sublayers in `layoutSubviews` because UILabel on iOS 27 rarely calls `drawText(in:)`. `ChatLinkLabel.layout(in:)` is shared by bars and hit-testing and returns the `NSTextStorage` too, since `NSLayoutManager` holds storage weakly
- Cards (`.card`: link previews and shared posts, places, contacts, polls), like Telegram's link previews: a 3 pt bar and a faint wash (10%, 18% on own bubbles) in the sender's `ChatUserColor` (white on own bubbles), then the link's host without `www.` beside the card's symbol, a semibold title (2 lines) and the subtitle (3 lines), with a 56 pt thumbnail top-trailing when there's an image. Tapping opens the link. Files (`ChatFileRow`), like Telegram's and WhatsApp's: a 44 pt rounded tile colored by type (PDF red, text blue, sheets green, archives and slides orange, media purple, else gray; white on own bubbles) showing the extension, or the file's thumbnail, then the name (middle-truncated) and size · extension; a spinner replaces the extension while it loads. Uploads in progress use the file row with "Uploading…". The card's text stack fills its column (`alignment = .fill`): with `.leading`, multi-line labels kept the width they last wrapped at, and since configure lays the embeds out once at the cell's current frame, a card configured before the cell had its width (a message arriving in an open chat) stayed one character wide. Bubbles with cards take `maxContentWidth`; preview images keep aspect ratio, cropped to at most 4:3 portrait (`max(ratio, 0.75)`). Uploaded images keep Telegram-style sizes
- Link previews with media (`.linkPreview`, any network): the card above, with its media under the text across the card (`ChatMediaPager`) instead of a thumbnail, so a post's photos or video can be looked through without opening the link. One page per item, sized by the first (4:3 portrait to 2:1), photos and video posters filling it; with several, a horizontally paging scroll view and a prominent `UIPageControl`. Pages keep their own taps (photos open the viewer, videos play, a player's thumbnail opens the link); only the card's text opens the link. `ChatMediaPagerScrollView` takes a horizontal drag only while there's a page that way, and the cell's swipe to reply doesn't begin on a pager that can still page forward, so a leftward drag pages, and on the last page swipes to reply (checked in the simulator on the demo chat "Trip Photos", 2026-11-09). The full-screen viewer still shows one photo
- Videos and files (`ChatEmbed.video`, `.file`): a video shows its poster with a play symbol and length, sized like an uploaded photo, and plays full screen in `AVPlayerViewController` (`ChatVideoPlayer`: the player opens at once with a spinner while the file loads). A GIF (`isGIF`) plays muted and looped in the bubble (`AVPlayerLooper`) while the cell is in a window. A file is a card whose icon turns into a spinner while it loads, then opens in Quick Look (`QLPreviewController`; its data source is weak, so `ChatViewController.filePreviewSource` keeps it). Both load through `ChatMediaFiles` (Core): `URLSession.download` (so network schemes like `tgfile:` work) into Caches/ChatMedia/`<sha256 of the URL>`/`<filename>`, because AVFoundation and Quick Look can't read custom URL schemes and need the file's extension. One load per URL; a failure shows as the chat's error notice
- Voice messages (`ChatEmbed.voice`, `ChatVoiceView`), like Telegram's: a 44 pt round play button in the bubble's accent (white on the user's blue bubbles, blue on others'), the waveform as 2.5 pt bars resampled to the width (the loudest level per bar; flat without levels), filling in as it plays, 120–190 pt long by duration; a tap on it seeks. Under it the time played, or the length when stopped. A message that is only a voice message fits the bubble to it, with the time beside the length instead of on its own line. `ChatAudioPreviewPlayer` posts `progressNotification` 20 times a second while playing, offers `position(for:)` and `seek(id:to:)`, and stops at the end so the waveform resets. `.audio` without artwork (audio files) shows a round play button straight on the bubble instead of a card with an empty artwork square
- Photos and videos that fill a bubble are 296 pt wide (portrait ones up to 400 pt tall, at least 220 pt wide): as wide as a bubble may be on a 375 pt iPhone (80% of the row, less the bubble's 4 pt inset), so they never overflow it. Albums: two or more media embeds (photos and videos) in one message form a grid that wide: an odd one out on a row of its own, then square pairs 2 pt apart; the grid takes the bubble's media corners
- Positional custom emoji (`ChatMessage.customEmojis`, for networks that mark them as entities over fallback emoji, such as Telegram): the chat replaces each range whose key is in the adapter's `ChatCustomEmojis` catalog with an image attachment, after spoilers and back to front, since each replacement shortens the text; inside a hidden spoiler the fallback stays. The text (copying, previews) keeps the fallback emoji
