- Bubble images (photos, track artwork, card icons, and the image viewer) load through `ChatMediaImageCache`: its own cost-limited cache (150 MB of decoded pixels), decoded off the main thread, with one shared load per URL. Every configure rebuilds a cell's image views, and reconfigures are frequent (reactions, read state, emoji and mention loads), so the cell also starts the new views on the images it showed by URL. In the 80-entry `StoryImageCache` the chat's avatars evicted photos, and each reconfigure blanked them until they reloaded
- Reply quotes: `ChatViewController` keeps each reply's rendered quote with its rendered message/layout and reconfigures the reply when the quote changes, because a quoted message often arrives in `messages` not `referencedMessages` and the diffable data source won't reconfigure an unchanged reply ("Loading…" stuck). `replyQuote(for:)` checks the view's own `messagesById` first
- Composer `ChatComposerTextView` handles hardware Return in `pressesBegan`: sends when the send button is enabled and swallows the press end; Shift-Return adds a line; on-screen Return arrives as text; while `markedTextRange` is set, Return confirms IME composition. Not a priority `UIKeyCommand`, which would steal the IME Return. On a Mac (`isiOSAppOnMac`) keys never reach `pressesBegan`: they arrive through `insertText`, Return as `"\n"` (observed 2026-10-31), so there `insertText("\n")` without marked text sends, and Shift-/Option-Return are `UIKeyCommand`s that insert the line. The send button is `arrow.up.circle.fill` with palette colors white on `.systemBlue`, so the arrow stays white in dark mode
- Drag and drop: a `UIDropInteraction` on the chat view, plus the message list's `dropDelegate` (a `UICollectionView` has its own drop interaction that takes drops over the messages, so the view's never sees them), take a single image anywhere on the chat (when `canAttach`) and stages it like a Photos pick (`stageImage(from:)`: GIFs as is, others as JPEG); a "Drop to Attach" highlight, added to the window so it covers the navigation bar too, shows while an image is over the chat. The composer's own text drop interaction is removed so it can't take drops over the composer; dropped text is inserted into the composer by the chat's handler instead. A staged image shows no filename, and tapping its thumbnail opens `ChatImageViewerController` on a temporary copy of the data (the viewer reads and saves from a URL)
- Paste: `ChatComposerTextView` overrides `paste(_:)` (and enables Paste in `canPerformAction`) so an image on the pasteboard, when the chat `canAttach` and isn't editing, is staged through `stageImage(from:)` like a drop; image wins over text when the pasteboard has both. Reading the pasteboard inside a user-initiated paste shows no paste prompt
- Swipe-to-reply pan sets `allowedScrollTypesMask = .continuous` (two-finger trackpad); `.discrete` mouse wheels excluded since each notch would start and end a swipe
- Typing: others typing show as the last list row (`Item.typing`, `ChatTypingCell`), an incoming bubble with three dots pulsing in turn (opacity, plus a 3 pt lift unless Reduce Motion), like Messages; chats with `showsSenderNames` put "Name", "A and B" or "A and N others" above it. Not shown in an older window (`hasLater`). It is added and removed through `applyKeepingBottom(_:animated:)`, so a pinned list follows it. The dot animations restart in `didMoveToWindow` (Core Animation drops them off-window) and use `isRemovedOnCompletion = false` to survive backgrounding. It replaced a floating "is typing…" glass capsule above the composer
- Date chips: the list has one section per calendar day (`Section.day(startOfDay)`, built by `ChatViewController.days(of:)`), each with a `ChatDateHeaderView` header from the list configuration's `headerMode = .supplementary`, made pinned (`pinToVisibleBounds`, `zIndex = 2`) in a compositional section provider. A message without a timestamp joins the day before it, and one dated to a day already shown (out of order) stays in the current section, since a diffable section id may appear only once. The typing row goes in the last section. Pinned headers fade out 1.2 s after scrolling stops (`showDateHeaders` / `hideFloatingDateHeaders`): a header is "floating" when its frame sits below its natural place above its section's first item; one in its natural place stays. Sender groups break at midnight. `scrollToDivider` lands the divider just under its day's header rather than under the pinned header
- Search: framework-level, so every network has it with no adapter code. `ChatViewModel` holds `searchQuery` (non-nil while searching), `searchMatchIds` (loaded messages, oldest first, matched by `ChatSearch`: case-, accent- and width-insensitive on `searchableText(of:)` = rendered Markdown with mention names, plus card/audio/quoted-message titles and text and image alt), `searchMatchIndex` and `isSearchingEarlier`. Matches recompute in `messages.didSet`, keeping the shown match. Typing jumps to the newest match after 300 ms; older-match and Return page `loadEarlier()` until an older match appears, at most `searchPageLimit` (10) pages per press. Jumping goes through `jump(to:)`, so windowed networks show a window. No network's server-side search is used yet. UI: long press on the title pill (`ChatView`; tap still runs `onTitleTap`) calls `beginSearch()`; the field is the app's shared `searchBar(text:isPresented:...)` in the navigation-bar drawer, attached on a `.background` view while searching so toggling search doesn't rebuild `ChatUIKitView` (an `if` around the chat would recreate the view controller and lose the scroll position). Its Cancel (`isPresented` false) calls `endSearch()`. `ChatViewController` swaps the input row for a glass results bar ("N of M", "+" while `hasEarlierMessages`, up/down arrows) and highlights query ranges in bubble text (yellow; white on own bubbles), with the query in the attributed-text cache key. Event `chat_search` {network}
- Member lists and profiles (Apron, Telegram) have no navigation title: the name is already under the photo. Member lists open on the chat's details (`ChatDetailsHeader`), then one Members section (`MembersSection`) whose first row is the member search, in the list rather than the navigation bar so it sits with the members. Members are in one list, online first, then most recently seen, ties in the network's own order (Apron: you, then by name); there are no Online/Offline subsections
- Tapping a large avatar on a profile (Apron, Telegram, the feed's `ProfileDetailView`) or a chat's details header opens it in `ChatImageViewerController` (`opensFullScreenPhoto`), with `imageIsPreview` so a smaller photo shows until the full one loads. Not offered without a photo or while names are redacted
- Redact names (`ChatRedaction.isEnabled`, `redactionEnabled` default) covers sender names, initials, reply quotes/preview, typing names, reactors, mention suggestions, mentions in text (`@Redacted`), Apron member list and profile. Titles redacted when they name people (`!showsSenderNames` or `ChatViewModel.titleNamesPeople`), plus `@username` subtitles. Plain-text names in bodies are not redacted
- Forwarding (`ChatForward.swift`): Forward in the long-press menu shows when the chat's `forwardHandler` is set (`ChatsView.opened` sets it) and the message is a sent user message. `startForward` builds a `ChatForward`: the message as shown, network, `accountId`, the adapter's `ChatForwarding.forwardReference` (an opaque string: Telegram the peer and the message's ids, an album's every one; Discord the channel, message and guild; X the text and `xchatmedia:` URLs, since X re-uploads media; Instagram the thread, message, whether it's the viewer's, and its text or one attachment fbid) and its media (`ChatForwardMedia.from`: images, videos, files, and voice messages through `audioPreviewURL`; cards, track shares and stickers have no file). `ChatsView` shows `ChatForwardPicker` (every row, archived too, searchable by name), then opens the picked chat; `ChatForwardHandoff`, a reference type since `opened` runs while SwiftUI evaluates the destination, stages the forward in the new chat's view model (`stage`), which shows it in the reply bar ("Forward from <name>") and makes Send available. `send()` sends typed text first, then the forward; on failure the forward stays staged
  - Native when `forwardsNatively`: same network and `accountId`, a reference, and a `ChatForwarding` adapter. Otherwise `sendCopy`: `ChatForwardCopy.parts` puts "Forwarded" (an italic range, `ChatOutgoing.italics`, and a link to the original from `ChatForward.link`, `ChatOutgoing.links`) and the text with the first file, then each further file alone; files come from `ChatMediaFiles` (which serves the networks' schemes), voice goes through `ChatVoiceSending` when `AVAudioFile` reads the file (Ogg Opus reads on iOS 18), else as a file. Files to a chat without attachments throw `ChatForwardError.noAttachments` before anything is sent. Italics and links: Telegram sends `messageEntityItalic` and `messageEntityTextUrl` entities (validated in Saved Messages, observed 2026-11-07), Discord and Apron (Markdown) write `[*Forwarded*](url)` (`ChatOutgoing.markdownText`), and plain-text networks send the word as it is; the copy names neither sender nor source network, at the user's request. The composer bar and the sending copy name the origin (`originName`: the message's own forward origin, else its sender, none for the user's own)
  - The optimistic copy shows the source message's text and embeds with its forward origin, until the id `deliver` returns (the last message sent) is in history
  - Incoming: `ChatMessage.forwardedFrom` (`ChatForwardOrigin`, name optional) shows "Forwarded from <name>" / "Forwarded" in italic footnote in the bubble's header (`ChatMessageCell.forwardLabel`)
- Message links (`ChatMessageLinking`, split from Apron's `ChatLinking`, which refines it): Copy Link in the menu and a forwarded copy's link. Apron: link.apron.chat; Discord: `https://discord.com/channels/<guild or @me>/<channel or thread>/<message>`; Telegram supergroups and channels: `https://t.me/c/<channel id>/<message id>` (members only), none for private chats and basic groups
- Link cards (`ChatMessageCell.linkCard`): the header row (symbol, site) ends with a flexible spacer. Without a site (a card with no link, such as Instagram's expired-story and bare post shares) the symbol, which hugs at required priority, alone set the row's width, and the card column's `.fill` alignment held the whole card to it at required priority, beating the bubble's width (999): those cards showed one letter wide. Required hugging inside a filled stack overrides the computed bubble width without any constraint-conflict log

### Home pager
- `ContentView`'s root is `HomePager`: `UIPageViewController` (`HomePagerController`) with two `UIHostingController`s — `NavigationStack` for `ChatsView` left, feed right. Not SwiftUI paging `ScrollView`/`TabView`: both lost hosted navigation bars' layout margins, and `scrollPosition` didn't apply initially
- The pager ignores every safe area region, the keyboard included, and keeps its size; `HomePagerController` gives both pages the keyboard's height (beyond the home indicator) as `additionalSafeAreaInsets.bottom`, animated with the keyboard's duration and curve from `keyboardWillChangeFrame`/`keyboardWillHide`. Its pages are hosting controllers inside the page controller's scroll view, which don't avoid the keyboard themselves, so without the inset forms pushed in either page (Settings → Apron's Save Token on an iPhone SE) sat under the keyboard. Shrinking the whole pager above the keyboard (`.ignoresSafeArea(.container)`) instead resized it at once while the keyboard was still moving: a black band under a chat as the keyboard rose, and the Chats list laid out at half height, its lower rows drawn late, after leaving a chat with the keyboard up. `ChatView` ignores the inset (it ignores every region; the list's bottom inset already subtracts the safe area) and places its composer on `keyboardLayoutGuide`. The inset doesn't move iOS 26's bottom-floating search field (`.searchable` `.automatic`): Search pushed from the feed kept its field under the keyboard, so Search uses the field under the title (`.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .always)`), which moves to the top while focused. Chats search is at the bottom, as in Messages, at the user's request, so it's a field of the app's own (`bottomSearchBar`, see Chats search). Search leaves out `searchBarKeepsFill()`: its fill, `secondarySystemBackground`, is the inset-grouped list's background, so the field lost its capsule. A floating or undocked keyboard that doesn't reach the bottom edge adds no inset
- A page set from outside (a tapped notification, a link) is swapped in on the next turn of the run loop, not inside SwiftUI's `updateUIViewController`. Swapped in during launch's first update, with the app left on the feed, the Chats page laid out without its top safe area: the chat's navigation bar and the Chats list's title sat under the status bar until relaunch, though the page's UIKit `safeAreaInsets` were right and laying it out at once didn't help. A manual swipe or a notification while the app was running was unaffected
- Pages wrap: the data source returns the other page both before and after, so swiping on past either page lands on the other. The title row repeats both ways for it (… Notifications, Chats, Notifications …), its position running from -1 to 2 mid-swipe. It measures a swipe from the page shown when the offset was last at rest, not `viewControllers.first`: the page controller switches that while the swipe is still settling, before the offset returns to the middle, which made the old page dots flick back and forth after each swipe
- Paging (`isScrollEnabled`) off whenever the visible side has a pushed screen, tracked from navigation state (`ChatsView` selection, feed detail selection and path), so back swipe and swipe-to-reply aren't taken over. Not via root `onAppear`/`onDisappear` (outgoing page's `onDisappear` fires as a swipe begins)
- `updateUIViewController` calls `setViewControllers` and updates the title row when the bound `page` differs from the shown page. An animated programmatic `setViewControllers` leaves cached neighbours stale, so the page is set again without animation after it finishes. Never while a swipe is transitioning (`willTransitionTo` until `didFinishAnimating`): the shown page switches before `page` catches up, so any SwiftUI update then would set the old page mid-transition, and `UIPageViewController` raises an exception (crashed build 190)
- The page dots were replaced by the title row (`HomeTitleStrip.swift`): each page's large title (`ToolbarItem(placement: .largeTitle)`, iOS 26+; plain titles before) is a `UIViewRepresentable` showing "Chats  Notifications" or "Notifications  Chats", the leading title in `label` colour and the next one gray, blending with the swipe. The row scrolls by a title's width while the pages scroll by a screen's, so a swipe moves both at once. `HomePagerController` observes the scroll view's `contentOffset` by KVO (the page controller stays its delegate) and sets `HomeTitleStripModel.position` directly, which relays out both strips; pushing the offset through SwiftUI state re-rendered both roots each frame and cancelled the pan. Each strip draws the row where it would be with the pager at rest (offset back by its page's window x) and is masked to its own page, so the two pages' strips meet as one row while sitting in their own navigation bars (and collapsing with them). Titles further than one step from the position fade out, so the previous one fades in from the leading edge on a swipe back. Tapping a title scrolls to its page (animated `setViewControllers`, `isScrollingToPage` keeps the resting position until it finishes, then the page is set again without animation). Nothing inside a large title receives touches (a UIKit tap recognizer on the strip and SwiftUI's `onTapGesture` on it both never fired, observed 2026-11-22 on iOS 27), so `HomePagerController` has a tap recognizer on its own view that only receives touches landing on a visible title (`HomeTitleStripModel.titleIndex(at:in:)`) and doesn't cancel them. Only strips whose row is showing take taps (`HomeTitleStripView.takesTaps`): nothing pushed on their page and the large title not collapsed. A pushed screen leaves the strip in the window under it, so tapping Search's field, where "Chats" sits on the Notifications page, switched to Chats with Search still open (seen on a device). Only the page mostly on screen takes them too, and none while paging is off: the other page stays loaded beside the visible one, its strip drawing the row back across the screen, so its titles still caught taps on Search's field. Accessibility is SwiftUI's on the representable: the leading title as a header and the next as a button, since UIKit labels inside the strip didn't reach the accessibility tree
- Scrolling a home page up turns the title row into the bar's inline title instead of sliding it under the bar: the leading title scales from large title to `headline` size and moves to the middle of the bar's top row while the others fade out by halfway. On iOS 27 the large title is a subview of the list's scroll view (`_UINavigationBarLargeTitleView`), not of the navigation bar, and collapsing it doesn't move the content offset relative to the inset: the top content inset shrinks by the large title's height (157 to 116 on an iPhone 17 Pro, observed 2026-11-22) with `contentOffset` following, and once it bottoms out the system sets the large title's alpha to 0 and shows its inline title. So the strip observes its nearest ancestor scroll view's `contentOffset` and takes progress as how much of that inset is gone (resting inset minus current, over the large title view's height), arriving at the inline title's place exactly when the system swaps to it. The scroll offset alone (offset plus inset) stays 0 through the whole collapse, and the inline title label's own frame is mid-animation at rest, so neither works as the measure or the target. The bar's inline title is replaced by the strip's own (`HomeInlineTitle`, a `.principal` toolbar item in the large title's bold face at `headline` size, so it matches the shrunk strip exactly): the system's came in blurred and sliding up from below while the large title faded out, so for about 8 frames the title showed twice, and it settled in a lighter, grayer semibold. Ours shows at once when progress reaches 1. On the way back it shows as much as the large title is still hidden, read from the large title view's presentation opacity frame by frame with a `CADisplayLink` while the bar fades it in: the bar starts that fade a frame or more after the title starts to grow, so hiding ours at once blanked the title for a frame, and fading it by progress showed it twice. The resting inset lives in `HomeTitleStripModel`, per scroll view: SwiftUI makes a new strip when a page comes back on screen, and one made while its page was collapsed took the collapsed inset for the resting one, so the bar showed no title. The shrink can't run in the bar's title view instead: the principal item gets almost no width while the large title shows, and SwiftUI resets its transform. Mid-swipe the two pages' strips draw the row from shared geometry: each reports its leading title's centre on screen and its collapse progress to `HomeTitleStripModel.pageTitles`, and both draw the blend, weighted by how much of each page is on screen and leaving out a page whose title has fully collapsed (the bar hides it). Each drawing its own, the halves met with a step whenever one list rested a few points scrolled, as the Chats list does on a device. The sideways part of the shrink uses the distance from the title's resting place to the bar's centre, which a swipe doesn't change; measured from where the swipe had moved the title, a slightly scrolled page's half slid sideways as the page moved. Each strip is cut to its own page only mid-swipe, when it lays out every frame: cut at rest too, a strip laid out while a popped screen's page was still sliding in kept that cut and clipped the start of its leading title until the list next scrolled (seen on a device after going back from Search). For the same reason the sideways target is the list's middle, which moves with a push or pop along with the strip, not the screen's
- Last page stored in `@AppStorage("homePage")` (`HomePage` is `String`-backed), restored at launch

### Media viewer
- `MediaViewer` (`MediaViewer.swift`) opens photos and videos full screen from anywhere: `present(timeline:kind:itemId:…)` for a profile's Media grid (pages from `ProfileTimelineViewModel.$timelines`, more loaded near the end), and `present(pages:startKey:network:engaging:updates:loadMore:)` for any list of `MediaViewerPage`s. A page's `post` gives the caption the post's `PostActionBar` when `engaging` is given (only X has a `PostEngaging`); without one, a page's `counts` show read-only in the bar's layout (replies, reposts, likes, then sharing the link: a chat post's `ChatPost.counts`, an Instagram grid post's likes); its `link` is what their share button shares.
- Videos (not GIFs, which loop like photos) have controls laid out as X's player: a bare play/pause icon beside a thin scrubber across the screen (a small dot, larger while held), a round sound button above its end (muting carries across the videos paged through), and the time ("0:11 / 0:45") in a pill only while scrubbing. The scrubber follows the player four times a second; dragging pauses, seeks exactly (one seek at a time, only the newest position waiting) and resumes if it was playing.
- The ••• button saves the shown photo, or the video as its MP4: into Photos on an iPhone or iPad, and into Downloads on a Mac (`isiOSAppOnMac`), where apps save files. An iPhone app on a Mac is sandboxed, so the first save asks the user to choose the Downloads folder once (the kit's `ChatDownloads`: a folder picker opening at the real `~/Downloads`, kept as a security-scoped bookmark) and later saves copy straight in, numbered like Finder's copies when the name is taken. The menu button shows a check once saved. `MediaDownloads.file(for:name:)` downloads the original to a temporary file named `<network>-<post id>-<n>` with the extension its type calls for. A Bluesky video's HLS playlist (`video.bsky.app/watch/<did>/<cid>/playlist.m3u8`) can't be saved as a file, so it stands for the uploaded MP4, the blob on the author's PDS: `com.atproto.sync.getBlob?did&cid`, the PDS from the DID document (plc.directory for `did:plc`, `/.well-known/did.json` for `did:web`), which answers `video/mp4` (observed 2026-11-21).
- It opens from: profile Media tiles; photos in `PostCardView` (notification detail, threads, a profile's Posts, which passes the timeline item's full media so videos play); photos in notification detail's `TargetPostView` and the photo in a message notification; the Instagram profile grid; and a post's photos and videos in any chat, through stupid-chatkit's `ChatKit.openPostMedia` (0.9.15), mapped by `MediaViewer.present(chatPost:at:xEngaging:)`: an X post gets its action bar through the first signed-in X account's `PostEngaging`, other networks link to the post. Notification details carry image URLs only, so a video there shows its poster.
- A chat's own photos and videos (those that fill a bubble) open in it too, through the kit's `ChatKit.openChatMedia` and `MediaViewer.present(chatMedia:)`: every one in the chat's loaded messages, oldest first, so swiping right goes back through earlier media, with the message's text as the caption and "n of m" within an album. Within four pages of the first, the viewer asks the chat for earlier messages (`ChatMediaGallery.loadEarlier`), and the new media arrives through the gallery's `updates` (the chat's `messages`); the pager resets its cached neighbors when pages arrive while it is on the first or last. Chat pages set `downloadsVideo`: the video plays from a `ChatMediaFiles` copy, as the chat's own player did, because their URLs can be schemes only `URLSession` loads (`tgfile:`, `xchatmedia:`), and the player attaches to the scrubber when the download finishes (`onPlayerReady`). A video without a poster (X Chat, some Telegram GIFs) has its own URL as `media.imageURL`, which `MediaViewerPage.imageURL` treats as no image. Link previews' and posts' media and stickers stay as before.

### Posts linked in chats
- `AppContainer` sets stupid-chatkit's `ChatKit.resolveLink` to one `LinkedPostResolver` (`LinkedPosts.swift`) with a `LinkedPostSource` per network: X (`XPost`, first signed-in X account), Instagram (`InstagramPost`, first signed-in Instagram account) and Bluesky (`BlueskyPost`, no account). A source answers a link with the post's key only when something can load it now, so without an X or Instagram account those links stay as the network sent them. Each post draws as `.post` (`ChatPost`) with its network's icon, media, counts and, for X and Bluesky, the quoted post.
- A resolved post's embed carries the post's own link, never the link the chat used. The kit shows equal embeds once per message, and one message can link a post several ways: Discord previews an `x.com` link in the text with a `twitter.com` link, which drew the post twice when each embed kept its own link.
- `LinkedPostCache` keeps every network's posts (`LinkedPost`, keyed `<network>:<key>`) on disk in `Caches/LinkedPosts.json`, read off the main thread at launch and written a second after the last change; the newest 1,000 loaded in the last 30 days. A chat draws a post it has shown before in its first frame on later launches, instead of growing when it loads. A kept post older than a day still shows and loads again once a session, for its counts and Instagram's expiring media URLs. A post the network has none of isn't asked for again that session (and drops a kept copy); a failed load is retried after 5 minutes.
- Posts linked in a Chats row's latest message load before the chat opens: the kit asks `resolveLink` for the links in each changed row preview (0.9.14). Loading ahead stores messages without rendering them (rendering an Apron page fetches linked messages, which spends history pages), so older messages' posts load when the chat opens, or come from the cache.
- Instagram: `/p/`, `/reel/`, `/reels/` and `/tv/` links (also after a username). The shortcode read as base 64 in Instagram's alphabet (`A–Z a–z 0–9 - _`, first 11 characters) is the media id; `InstagramClient.post(shortcode:)` reads `GET /api/v1/media/<id>/info/` through the signed-in web session (`webJSONRequest`), the same `InstagramMediaInfoItem` shape as profile posts: `user` (`username`, `full_name`, `is_verified`, `profile_pic_url`), `taken_at`, `caption.text`, `carousel_media` or the item itself (widest `image_versions2` candidate, `video_versions`, `original_width`/`height`, `video_duration`), `comment_count`, and `like_count` unless `like_and_view_counts_disabled`. Instagram has no repost count. The anonymous embed page (`/p/<code>/embed/captioned/`) carries no post data, so loading needs the session (observed 2026-11-20). Probe `instagram-web-client.py --simulator post-preview <shortcode>` (quit the app first: one client per session).
- Bluesky: `bsky.app/profile/<handle or DID>/post/<rkey>` links, loaded from the public AppView (`https://public.api.bsky.app/xrpc`) without signing in: `com.atproto.identity.resolveHandle` first, since `app.bsky.feed.getPosts` answers a handle in an at:// URI with a 500, then `getPosts`. A 400 means no such handle or post. Media from `images#view` (`fullsize`, `alt`, `aspectRatio`) or `video#view` (`playlist`, an HLS `.m3u8`, and `thumbnail`); the quoted post from `record#view` (`record` a `#viewRecord`; `#viewNotFound` and `#viewBlocked` show none) or `recordWithMedia#view` (`media` the post's media, `record.record` the quoted post). Link cards (`external#view`) aren't drawn. Verified is `author.verification.verifiedStatus == "valid"`. Reposts shown leave out quotes. Bluesky DMs' shared posts are cards linking to bsky.app, so they draw as posts too. HLS plays streamed: the kit's player downloads other videos whole (0.9.15). Probe `uv run --with cryptography python3 scripts/bluesky-web-client.py post-preview --url <link>` (observed 2026-11-20).

### Voice message transcripts
- Every chat's voice messages can be transcribed on the device (stupid-chatkit 0.9.22, iOS 26 and later): a button beside the waveform, placeholder lines while it works, the text under the waveform, and a long press to pick the language. It is the kit's, through the audio file each adapter already resolves for playback (`audioPreviewURL(for:)`), so no network has code for it and nothing leaves the device. The app sets nothing: `ChatKit.transcriptionLanguages` defaults to the user's preferred languages. The kit's `docs/DESIGN.md` § Voice transcripts has the design.
- Engines: `SpeechTranscriber` where the device runs it and knows the language, else `DictationTranscriber` (54 languages, including `en_ZA` and Dutch). Neither detects the language, so each of the user's languages is tried and the most confident transcript that `NLLanguageRecognizer` reads as that language is kept; confidence alone picked a Spanish model for English audio. Below a score of 0.55 the bubble says it couldn't make the message out: real English voice notes scored 0.71–0.88 in `en_ZA`, Afrikaans ones at most 0.45 in Dutch or English, as fluent but wrong text (observed 2026-11-22). The first transcription in a language downloads its model (30–90 s on a Mac); after that a note takes under a second. Telegram's and Discord's Ogg Opus decode through `AVAudioFile`.
- Afrikaans isn't supported: no Apple engine has it (`SpeechTranscriber`, `DictationTranscriber`, `SFSpeechRecognizer`, iOS 27). Whisper does, but its `small` model (WhisperKit) wrote a Dutch–Afrikaans mix and, left to detect, took the notes for Dutch or English; only the large models, over a gigabyte and slow on a phone, would be usable, so Whisper isn't used (observed 2026-11-22).
- Transcripts are kept in the chat store by message id: a Telegram voice embed's preview id is a `tgfile:` URL naming the file reference, which Telegram replaces, so keying by it lost transcripts.
- The simulator can't transcribe: `SpeechTranscriber` lists no languages there and `DictationTranscriber` offers no audio format (observed 2026-11-22, iOS 27); the button says the device can't. Check transcription on a device, or the layout with a temporary fake in the kit's `ChatVoiceTranscriber`.
- Each transcription is counted as `voice_transcribe` (see Usage metrics), from the kit's `ChatEvent.voiceTranscribed` (0.10.0).

### Chat info, mute and archive
- Every chat's title opens details: `InfoChatView` wraps `ChatView` for networks without a screen of their own (X, Instagram, Bluesky, LinkedIn, Spotify, demo) and pushes `ChatInfoView` (`ChatDetailsHeader` with `ChatSettingsButtons` in its actions). Mute and Archive are round `DetailActionButton`s with their names below, in a `DetailActionRow` under the header next to Message where a profile has it (like Telegram's profile buttons); every profile's Message button (Apron, Telegram, Discord) is the same button. A one-to-one X, Instagram or Bluesky chat opens `ProfileDetailView` instead, with the same buttons (see Profile detail). `ChatSettingsButtons` is also in `TelegramMembersView`, `TelegramProfileView` (only for the DM it was opened from, and on its "No Profile" screen too), `DiscordChannelDetailsView` and `DiscordProfileView` (the DM's own). Mute and Archive are optimistic: the button shows the new state at once, ignores taps while its change is in flight, and goes back with an alert when the change fails. A spinner on a `DetailActionButton` (Message, while it opens a chat) is small and inside the icon's 24-point frame, so the button keeps its size.
- The kit's `ChatMuting`/`ChatArchiving` (0.9.1): `ChatViewModel.isMuted`/`isArchived` come from the followed Chats row, so a chat without a row offers neither; `setMuted`/`setArchived` throw what the network answered and, once accepted, change the row at once (muted flag; moved between the list and the archive). The next load confirms it from the network.
- Telegram: `account.updateNotifySettings` with `inputPeerNotifySettings {mute_until: Int32.max}` to mute, `0` to unmute (which also overrides a muted default for its kind of chat); `folders.editPeerFolders` with folder 1 / 0 (probe `mute`, `archive`, observed 2026-11-13). Saved Messages isn't archivable.
- Discord: `PATCH /users/@me/guilds/{guild_id or @me}/settings {channel_overrides: {<channel>: {muted: true, mute_config: {selected_time_window: -1, end_time: null}}}}`; unmuting sends `{muted: false}` and Discord drops the override. The response is the guild's settings, which the gateway keeps so rows update (probe `mute`, observed 2026-11-13). A channel in a muted server offers no Mute, since its own setting can't unmute it. Discord has no archive.
- Bluesky: `chat.bsky.convo.muteConvo`/`unmuteConvo {convoId}` through the PDS with the chat proxy; both return the conversation with its new `muted` (checked 2026-11-13 with bsky.app's session against the user's PDS; the probe's `mute`/`unmute` commands use the app's OAuth session). No archive.
- X Chat: `MuteConversationMutation {conversationIds: [id]}` / `UnmuteConversationMutation`, `ArchiveConversationMutation {conversationId}` / `UnarchiveConversationMutation`, at `x.com/i/api/graphql/<id>/<name>` like the other X Chat operations, without the optional `action_signatures`; the inbox's `is_muted`/`is_archived` follow (probe `conversation-settings`, observed 2026-11-13). X Chat's operations aren't in x.com's `client-web` bundles: the chat is a separate module-federation app whose manifest is `https://chat.x.com/embed-v1.json` (`publicPath` `https://abs.twimg.com/x-web/xchat/embed/`); its `dm-core-<hash>.js` asset declares every X Chat operation (Kotlin/JS: the persisted id beside the operation name, then the document).
- Not yet: Instagram (account locked behind a recovery challenge) and LinkedIn (no valid session to check against; it also has an archive the list doesn't read).

### Chats list
- Networks take part through `ChatListProvider` (`network`, `chatList() -> ChatListPage`): `ApronNotificationSource`, `SpotifyMessagesSource`, `InstagramClient`, `TelegramClient`, `XChatClient`, each wrapped in a `ChatListSource` with its account id and error label; `AppContainer` passes `ChatListViewModel(sources:)` a closure, read on every load since accounts come and go. Providers load concurrently in a task group and merge in source order. `ChatListPage.archived` is `nil` when the archive didn't load (Telegram's folder 1 request failed, or X's archived rows after its inbox), which keeps that network's archived rows; networks without an archive return `[]`. The protocol is `Sendable`: every provider is `@MainActor` or an actor, and the task group needs it (Swift 6 region checking rejects capturing non-Sendable providers)
- Live updates go through generic calls: `updateChats(_:)` (rows by id, X Chat's socket and Apron's per-room rebuild) and `replaceChats(where:with:)` (a set of rows, such as one Apron server's rooms via `ChatSummary.isApronRoom(on:)`)
- `ChatSummary.presence` (`ChatPresence`: online, last seen, recently, within a week or month) is the framework's presence type; Telegram fills it for one-to-one chats. Every "last seen" time (Telegram, Apron DM subtitles, members and profiles) goes through `ChatPresence.lastSeenTime`: "just now" under a minute (and for a time ahead of the device's clock), else the named relative time, which would otherwise read "in 0 seconds" or "12 seconds ago"
- Telegram chat subtitles: "<n> members" in groups, "Bot" in a private chat with a bot (the user's `bot` flag, from `TelegramClient.dialogState`; bots have no last seen), else the person's last seen, else none (Saved Messages). A chat opened for the first time has no subtitle until its dialog loads; after that the snapshot restores the subtitle
- `ChatListViewModel` merges `ChatSummary`s per network (Apron: every joined room on every server regardless of age, unread via the shared read store). A failing network keeps previous rows, as does an account a page reports `isCurrent` (`ChatListPage.AccountResult`: its rows are kept current by a live connection, such as an Apron server's) and shows its error above the list; unconfigured networks contribute nothing; `CancellationError`/`URLError.cancelled` aren't failures (opening a chat cancels the list's `.task` reload). Reloads on appear and on return from a chat; live connections and background refresh keep it current between those. No pull-to-refresh. Rows open via `AppContainer.spotifyChatFactory` / `telegramChatFactory` etc.
- Saved to `Caches/chat-list.json` after each load, restored when `ChatListViewModel` is created; a disconnected network loads empty and drops out. Adding a `ChatSummary` field makes old saved lists undecodable; the first load rebuilds them
- `follow(_:chatId:)` wires an opened chat to its row: `ChatViewModel` reports its newest message (skipped while an older window shows) as a one-line preview (mentions by name, "Shared “title”" for cards/tracks, "Photo" for images) and unread count; `You: ` prefix for own messages, or sender name where `ChatSummary.namesSenderInPreview` (Apron rooms, Spotify groups). The reload on return still runs A load that started before an open chat's latest update keeps that update (`followedRows`, numbered by `followedUpdateCount`) instead of the counts it fetched: launching from a Telegram notification, the first load (slow while Telegram connects) finished after the chat was read and put its unread count back, and the reload on going back was skipped because that load was still running. Live row rebuilds (`updateChats`/`replaceChats`: `TelegramLiveRows`, Apron's live feed) keep an open chat's counts too when the rebuilt row names the latest message the chat already showed: a Telegram dialog reload triggered by a new message finished before the chat's read mark, so the row showed the message as unread. A rebuilt row with a newer latest message replaces them, and the open chat fetches when it doesn't hold that message. Rows from the cache at launch or from before the app was in the background don't hand their latest message to a chat opened from them (StupidChatKit 0.9.13): a Telegram chat read before the background, opened from a notification about a newer message, found the stale row's latest message stored, skipped fetching, never showed the new messages, and the row came back unread on returning to the list. A chat also reports its row only once connected and once per change, so the row no longer flips between counts (0 → N → 0) while it opens.
- `ChatSummary.titleNamesPeople`: Apron rooms named for members (`isNamedForMembers`), Spotify DMs, every Instagram thread (`thread_title` may be custom or members' names, indistinguishable). With redaction on, `ChatRow` uses `DebugRedaction.chatTitle`/`chatPreview`: titles "Redacted", initials follow title, photos stay; `@` mentions → `@Redacted`; in sender-named rows the part before the first ": " → "Redacted" except "You: "; in DMs the title and each of its words are redacted as whole words in previews. Search still matches real names
- A showing `navigationDestination(item:)` doesn't swap when its item changes, so `ChatsView` clears the selection and opens the new chat 450 ms later
- `ChatListViewModel.chat(for:)` turns an open request's destination into a chat: the row listed under exactly that destination, else the first `ChatListResolving` provider's answer, given only its own rows. `TelegramClient` matches on kind and id (a push has no access hash); `ApronNotificationSource` stands in for a room the list doesn't have yet (titled by its id) and a direct chat with no room yet, using the same row id builder as its list. `ChatsView` holds no network knowledge here
- Notification-tap open requests for Telegram chats are kept in `ChatsView` until a load lists the chat (opening needs the access hash from the list; not loaded yet on cold launch)
- `ChatView` leaves the navigation bar's background alone (iOS 26 bars have none; messages scroll under it): `.toolbarBackground(.hidden)` on the chat hid the Chats drawer search field's background while swiping back, until the pop finished
- `ChatsView` inserts rows 50 ms after appearing: rows present while the drawer search bar first lays out collapse the list to the inline title (`defaultScrollAnchor(.top, for: .initialOffset)` and first-`.task` insertion didn't help)
- Search filters by title and latest message (`localizedStandardContains`) via shared `searchBar(text:prompt:placement:)`; Search uses `.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .always)` with an inline title (it opened inline anyway, as the list has no top content margin, and ending a search then brought the large title back, sliding in under the field before jumping above it); its empty, loading and no-results state is an overlay pinned at the list's resting top safe area plus 15pt (where the inset-grouped list put its first row), not a row: as a row it showed twice as the search took focus (removed and reinserted beside the pills' section) and dropped, then jumped back, as the search ended, and an overlay that followed the safe area jumped as the field moved. Focused, the pills take the space the hidden title leaves, so the pinned state sits just below them. An empty list draws no background, so the list's is hidden and `systemGroupedBackground` drawn behind it; Chats has a field of its own at the bottom ("Search Chats", below). While Chats search is active, `AccountFilterBar` (the feed's pills, one per account with chats matching the typed text (any chats before typing) plus any selected one, Apron servers included since rows' `accountId` is the account id) narrows the rows by `ChatSummary.accountId`; ending the search clears it. The title row and toolbar stay while searching (with the system search field, iOS hid them as it took focus, without animating, and the list jumped by their height). The filter (`showsFilter`) follows the field's focus a run loop later, fading in and out; the dock's hosting controller doesn't share SwiftUI's transactions, so the fade is an `.animation` keyed to it inside the dock (observed 2026-11-22, iOS 27.0, checked with the on-screen keyboard via `stupidsim button <udid> software-keyboard`). The filter and the field float over the list in one bar docked on the keyboard (`bottomSearchBar` in `SearchBar.swift`): iOS 26's bottom search field (`.searchable`, `.automatic`) stayed under the on-screen keyboard inside the home pager (only the pager's inset avoids the keyboard there, and it doesn't move that field), so Chats has its own glass field (`BottomSearchField`, with a Close button while presented). The bar is hosted in UIKit (`KeyboardDock`, a pass-through container whose hosting view is pinned to `keyboardLayoutGuide`), so it moves with the keyboard's own animation: laid out by SwiftUI from the pager's inset it moved at once, ahead of the keyboard rising and after it fell, even inside a UIKit animation (SwiftUI lays the page out on a later update), and a SwiftUI spring standing in for the keyboard's curve lagged behind it. The list's bottom `contentMargins` make room for the field and, while shown, the filter, so the last row scrolls clear. It matches iOS 26's own bottom field, measured against it on iOS 27.0: resting 28 pt above the screen's bottom edge (in the home indicator's area), 28 pt side margins, 48 pt tall, a primary-coloured magnifying glass, and no shadow behind it, only the glass blurring the row under it (a `.bar` material fading in behind it read as a heavy shadow). With the keyboard up it sits 8 pt above it (`KeyboardDock`'s two `lessThanOrEqualTo` constraints, with `keyboardLayoutGuide.usesBottomSafeArea` off). Not in a `safeAreaBar(edge: .bottom)`: there the rows' edge shadow covered the filter too and, as the search ended, darkened in a single frame, then snapped back once it was gone. Not a list row either. A list row was rejected because inserting a row as the search field activated cancelled the search (observed 2026-11-22, iOS 27.0). With content scrolled under the bar (or the title collapsed to inline), iOS clears the field's fill and draws its capsule as glass; during an interactive pop UIKit hides the real bar and mirrors it into the returning screen with `_UIPortalView`s, which don't draw glass, so the field showed as bare "Search" text until the pop finished (observed 2026-10-31, iOS 27.0). `searchBarKeepsFill()` (`SearchBar.swift`) finds the drawer's `UISearchTextField` through the hosting controller's `navigationItem.searchController` and, by KVO on `backgroundColor`, keeps it on opaque `secondarySystemBackground` (the unscrolled look; opaque so rows don't show through mid-swipe, where the bar's blur isn't mirrored either). Rejected: a glass `TextField` pinned in `safeAreaBar(edge: .top)` (lost the navigation title transition); `UISearchTextField` appearance proxies (UIKit overrides them when scrolling)
- On a Mac (`isiOSAppOnMac`) no search uses the system search controller: presenting one whose field is in the navigation bar throws, so the app crashed as Chats search took focus (observed 2026-11-17, macOS 27.0.1, build 211). `_UISearchPresentationController` never excises a bar inside a `UINavigationBar`, and its layout calls `+[UINavigationBar defaultSizeForOrientation:]` → `-[UIScreen _mainSceneReferenceBounds]`, which for an app that adopts scenes but doesn't support multiple ones asks `_preferredFocusedWindow`, and that raises "screen-based focus unsupported" on a Mac. Every `searchBar` there is `PlainSearchField` (`SearchBar.swift`), a plain `TextField` in `safeAreaBar(edge: .top)`, with the title inline: a trackpad's overscroll stretches a large title down with the list, under the pinned field; `isPresented` focuses it (Cmd+K/F) and turns false when it loses focus. The forward picker and server chats go through `searchBar` too. Searching a chat uses the kit's own Mac field (`ChatPlainSearchField` in `ChatView`), pinned under the bar since it never took focus in the bar; Done or Escape ends the search. Rejected: `UIApplicationSupportsMultipleScenes` (UIKit then reads the screen's bounds, but iPad users could open a second window, each with its own `AppContainer`); swizzling the private `UIScreen` method; `hidesNavigationBarDuringPresentation = false` (same path, still crashed). Temporary: with each new iOS/macOS release, try the system search field on a Mac again (Chats search, then searching a chat) and drop both plain fields once it no longer crashes
- Rows: separator starts at text (`.listRowSeparatorLeading`), top separator hidden on the first row only (hiding on all rows hid shared separators), network badge on an opaque system-background tile (Spotify's round badge has transparent corners). `ChatRow.timeLabel`: time today, abbreviated weekday within 6 days, `.dateTime.day().month(.defaultDigits)` earlier this year, `.numeric` before
- Quick look: long-pressing (or right-clicking on a Mac) a Chats or Archived Chats row shows the system context menu with the chat as its preview and an Open Chat action, as in Messages. The preview is `ChatsView.destination(for:quickLook: true)` in a `NavigationStack`: the chat's own screen with `ChatViewModel.isQuickLook` set, which makes `AdapterChatViewModel.markRead`/`reactionsSeen` no-ops (every network's read and seen sends go through those two), hides the composer and turns off replies, reaction toggles and the message menu. It opens at the unread divider like the chat does, and is still `follow`ed, so new messages update the row while unread counts stay. A context menu preview takes no touches, so it doesn't scroll; a custom Telegram-style scrollable peek (blurred cover, card, menu) was built and dropped for the native look
- Server chats ("Show All Rooms" in `ApronMembersView`, "Show All Channels" in `DiscordChannelDetailsView`): `ChatFolderView`, the Archived Chats list generalised, shows the Chats list's rows (archived ones too) that a `ChatServerFilter` includes, searchable, titled with the filter's name or the rows' `titlePrefix`. Rows leave out that name (`ChatRow.hiddenPrefix`): a channel shows as "general" and a thread as "parent / thread". The filter comes from the network side (`ApronRoomViewModel.serverFilter`: the server's account id; `DiscordChannelDetails.serverFilter`: the account and the channel's `guild_id` from the gateway), so `ChatsView` doesn't branch on networks. Details screens get the list builder through the `serverChats` environment value, which `ChatsView.destination(for:)` sets on each chat (not in a quick look). A `navigationDestination`'s content doesn't inherit the environment of the view declaring it, so `ApronChatView` and `DiscordChatView` pass the value on to their details explicitly; set on `ChatsView` itself it never reached them

### Long-press menu and the focus overlay
- `ChatViewController.messageMenu(for:)` builds one `ChatMessageMenu` (quick reactions, actions, submenus as closures). Every user message's long press shows it in `ChatMessageFocusController`; the system context menu (`uiMenu`) is left for presses beside a bubble. Reaction chips keep their own context menu (`ChatMessageCell.isFocusTarget` excludes them)
- Why not a context menu: its preview takes no touches, so it can't scroll or select text (tried a `previewProvider` with a scrolling `UITextView`, observed 2026-10-30)
- Press: `messagePress` (0.1 s) shrinks the bubble to 0.96 while the finger rests, `messageLift` (0.45 s) lifts it. Both begin only on a bubble (`ChatGestureFilter`) and recognize alongside each other and the list's pan. `messagePress` is passive and recognizes alongside everything: once begun it otherwise made the bubble's image and link taps fail whenever the finger rested longer than 0.1 s; a began long press doesn't fail on movement, so `messagePress` lets go after 10 pt of drag, or the list wouldn't scroll
- Lift: `ChatMessageCell.focusSnapshot()` renders the bubble's layer (whole, even past the screen's edges, and without the press's shrink). Not `drawHierarchy(afterScreenUpdates: true)`: it forces a screen update, which stalled the lift by several frames; the overlay's image view clips to the bubble's corners and returns the body label's attributed text and frame. The overlay shows the image and lays a `UITextView` over the text with the same attributed text made clear (custom emoji attachments swapped for blank ones of the same size), so it lays out like the label and selection lands on the drawn words while quote bars, spoilers and emoji stay as drawn. The text container's size is set after the frame, or it keeps a stale height and drops the last lines. Selecting can include the label's invisible reserved-time run
- Layout: a bubble that fits between the panels stays where it was, moved only as far as the reactions above and actions below need, as a context menu moves its preview. A taller one runs past the screen's edges in a scroll view with the panels pinned top and bottom over it, keeping the part that was on screen in place. Panels align to the bubble's side
- Transition: presented without animation; the bubble starts at its frame in the chat at the press scale and springs into place, the blur fades in and the panels grow from the bubble's side. The real bubble is hidden only once the overlay has appeared (`didLift`), since hiding it at the press left several frames showing neither, and stays hidden (`focusedMessageId`, kept across reconfigures) until closing animates the lifted one back to wherever the bubble is then. Actions run after it settles, so what they present comes from the chat. Tapping beside the bubble closes it (the first tap clears a text selection)
- On a Mac a right-click on a bubble lifts it into the same overlay as a long press: the Mac delivers secondary clicks only to `UIContextMenuInteraction`s, never to gesture recognizers (a `buttonMaskRequired = .secondary` tap never fired, observed 2026-10-30), so the collection view's context menu configuration, when `isiOSAppOnMac` and the point is on a bubble, returns no menu and presents the overlay on the next run loop turn. A right-click beside a bubble still gets the native menu. There's no long press on a Mac (`messagePress`/`messageLift` aren't added): a click and drag selects a message's text instead, through an invisible `UITextView` over the bubble's `ChatLinkLabel` (the focus overlay's approach), tinted with the text's color so the selection shows on the user's own blue bubbles; links, mentions and spoilers stay on the label's tap. Every right-click on a bubble lifts it, selected text included: the text view (`ChatSelectableTextView`) returns nil from `hitTest` for secondary-button events, so its own text menu (Look Up, Copy, Writing Tools) never shows and the click reaches the list's context menu configuration; without that, right-clicks on text showed the text menu and right-clicks beside it lifted. Command-C copies a selection. `focusSnapshot` clears and hides any selection while drawing the lifted bubble. A lifted bubble is 5% larger (`ChatMessageFocusController.liftedScale`), growing away from its own side of the screen so it never runs off the edge, with the panels moved out by what it adds; `focusSnapshot` renders at that scale so the text stays sharp. One too tall to fit between the panels stays at its own size. On a Mac the lift doesn't bounce: with no press the bubble starts at full size (`pressedScale` 1, 0.96 after a long press) and the spring is critically damped (`liftDamping` 1, 0.78 on touch screens, where the overshoot reads as the bubble popping out of the finger). Closing fades the blur view's alpha as well as removing its effect: on a Mac removing a `UIVisualEffectView`'s effect doesn't animate, so the blur stayed until the panels and bubble had settled and then vanished. The list's menu skips reaction chips (`ChatMessageCell.isOnReactions(at:)`), whose own interaction lists reactors on long press or right-click; chips stay interactive where you can't react, so the reactors list is always there, and only tapping toggles when you can
- On a Mac the chat's title pill has the toolbar's hover outline turned off (`.hoverEffectDisabled()`), since the pill draws its own capsule (observed 2026-10-30)

### Message reminders (prototype)
- Long press → Remind Me → In 5 Minutes / 30 Minutes / 1 Hour / 3 Hours / Tomorrow (9 AM local) / Custom… (SwiftUI `ChatReminderDatePicker` sheet, graphical date and time, future dates only). Offered when the chat's `ChatViewModel.reminders` is set, which `ChatsView.opened` does for every chat with a `ChatReminderTarget` from its `ChatSummary` (destination, account, network, title), so the kit knows nothing of the store
- `ChatReminderStore` (`StupidSocialCore/Storage`) keeps `ChatReminder`s in `UserDefaults` and schedules a local `UNTimeIntervalNotificationTrigger` notification, id `reminder:<id>`, `userInfo["chatReminder"]` = id. Permission is requested on the first reminder; when denied the reminder still lands in the feed
- Reminders sync across devices (iPhone, iPad, the app on Mac) through iCloud KVS key `com.stupid.chatreminders`: one `SyncState` (live reminders plus removed ids with their removal time). Reminders never change once made, so `SyncState.merged` is a union of reminders minus a union of tombstones; tombstones are pruned after 90 days. `ChatReminderStore.startSync()` (from `AppDelegate` at launch) merges at launch and on `didChangeExternallyNotification`, then schedules notifications for not-yet-due reminders this device has none pending or delivered for (only when permission is already granted, never prompting) and clears those of removed reminders, so Remove Reminder or Mark as Done on one device clears the others
- KVS alone can't fire a reminder on a device whose app hasn't run since it was set (iOS delivers KVS changes only to a running app, and nothing wakes a suspended one), so reminders also go to the other devices as pushes the relay holds until they're due (`ChatReminderPush`, see TECHNICAL_DESIGN § Chat reminder pushes). `ChatReminder.pushedTo` lists the device ids a push will reach; those devices don't schedule a local notification from KVS, which avoids two alerts. A device the push couldn't be scheduled for (relay unreachable, not registered yet) still schedules one when it syncs
- Every device signs with one VAPID key in the iCloud Keychain (item `chatReminderPush`), so any device can schedule or cancel pushes to the others; each device registers its own relay endpoint for that key with its own Web Push keys (App Group file `chat-reminder-push.json`, which the extension reads) and publishes `{deviceId, endpoint, p256dh, auth, vapidKey, updatedAt}` in KVS under its own key `com.stupid.chatreminders.device.<device id>` (one key per device, so devices never overwrite each other). Registration runs at launch, on an APNs token change and after the first reminder grants permission, never prompting; entries refresh weekly and devices silent for 60 days are skipped. Only devices whose published VAPID key matches are pushed to, which covers two devices creating keys before the Keychain synced: they converge on one and re-register
- The push payload is the `ChatReminder` JSON with its text cut to 600 UTF-8 bytes (refused over 2048). The extension decrypts it with the reminder keys before trying Apron registrations and shows it as the local notification would (`ChatReminderNotification`), with `userInfo` carrying the id and the reminder JSON (`chatReminderData`); a tap or foreground arrival adopts the reminder (`ChatReminderStore.adopt(pushed:)`) when KVS hasn't brought it yet, so it opens and lists. Removing a reminder before it's due cancels its scheduled pushes (`DELETE /scheduled/<id>`), and clears delivered pushed notifications by the id in `userInfo`, since their identifier is the push's
- Failures to register, schedule or cancel are reported as `chat_reminder_push_failed` with a `step` property
- Not verified end to end yet: the simulator gets no APNs token, so it needs two real devices
- In the chat, a message with a reminder (due or not) is outlined in orange with "Due in …" (or "Reminder due") in its bubble header; `ChatViewController.reminderDates` comes from `ChatReminderStore.dueDates(in:)` and reloads on `ChatReminderStore.remindersDidChange` (posted on every save) and every minute. Its long-press menu offers Mark as Done in place of Remind Me, which removes the message's reminders, and with them their feed items
- Due reminders join the feed in `FeedService.loadCachedFeed` (demo mode too) as `NotificationItem`s with id `reminder:<id>` (an orange bookmark icon, "<sender> in <chat>" or just the sender in a DM, the message as the muted target text), sorted in by due date, outside the 24-hour cache, so they stay until the user removes them: Remove Reminder on the row's trailing swipe or long press (the home pager can take the swipe), which also cancels a pending notification. `FeedViewModel` reloads its items when reminders change (`remindersDidChange`), when one's notification arrives in the foreground (`reminderDidFire`), at the next reminder's due time (a task sleeping until then, rescheduled whenever `items` changes, so it's there even with notifications off), and from cache first thing on foreground activation, before the sources refresh. The view model does it rather than `FeedView`, whose page may be off screen in the pager
- Tapping the notification or the feed row runs `NotificationRouter.openReminder(id:)`: clears the delivered notification, keeps the reminder in the feed, then opens the chat with `pendingFocus.messageId`. `focusMessageId` moved from `AdapterChatViewModel` to `ChatViewModel`, and `ChatsView.opened` applies a focus to any network's chat (it was Apron only). The jump only lands when the message is in the loaded history; networks without `ChatWindowing` open at the newest messages otherwise
- Not yet: no list or cancel of reminders that aren't due yet, no snooze action on the notification

### Command line (`ChatControlServer`, `cli/`)
- The app listens on `127.0.0.1:47823` (TCP, Network framework, main queue) while Settings → Command Line → Command-Line Access is on; `ChatControlServer.isAvailable` is `isiOSAppOnMac` or the simulator (which shares the Mac's loopback). Setting and token are in `UserDefaults` (`chatControlEnabled`, `chatControlToken`, 24 random bytes as hex): device-local on purpose, not iCloud
- Why TCP with a token rather than a Unix socket: macOS protects other apps' containers, including the app group's; a terminal gets "Operation not permitted" listing `~/Library/Containers/tech.stupid.StupidSocial/Data` or the group container even unsandboxed (observed 2026-10-30, macOS 27). Every request carries the token (constant-time compare); a wrong one gets an error and the connection closes after the reply is out (`send(_:thenClose:)`, since cancelling an `NWConnection` drops unsent data)
- Protocol: one JSON object per line. Request `{id, token, command, chat?, text?, limit?, archived?, refresh?, markRead?, replyTo?, interval?}`; reply `{id, ok, result | error}`; `watch` then streams `{id, event: "message" | "chat", …}`. Commands: `status`, `chats`, `read`, `send`, `mark-read`, `watch`. `chat` is a row id, else an exact title, else a unique title substring (ambiguity lists the ids); a miss reloads the list once
- Chats open through `AppContainer.chatViewModel(for:)`, a switch over `ChatDestination` beside the per-network factories `ChatsView` uses (the composition root, not shared chat code), followed by the Chats list so rows stay current. Each command connects an `AdapterChatViewModel` headless and disconnects after; `send` goes through the composer (`draft` + `send()`), restoring the chat's draft after, and fails loudly if the composer didn't take it. `mark-read` and `read --mark-read` await `sendPendingReadMarker()` so the debounced marker goes before the view model is released
- `watch <chat>` reconnects 5 s after a lost connection without an error (the app closes live connections in the background), and ends with the error on a disconnect with one. `watch` without a chat emits row changes, deduplicated on the encoded row (timestamps to the second, so a sending copy and its confirmed message are one change), and reloads the list every `interval` seconds (default 30, minimum 5). A connected client holds a `ProcessInfo` activity against App Nap
- Message text has mention names and masked spoilers, as previews do; local file URLs of sending images are omitted
- Release: `scripts/release-cli.sh <version>` (version must match `let version` in `cli/Sources/stupid-social/main.swift`, `cli/` committed and pushed) builds on this Mac, tags `cli-v<version>` and creates the GitHub release with `stupid-social-macos-arm64.tar.gz` and its `.sha256`. arm64 only, since iPad apps run only on Apple silicon Macs; the binary is the linker's ad-hoc signature, and curl downloads carry no quarantine attribute, so it runs without notarization. `cli/install.sh` (what `skills/stupid-social/SKILL.md` runs when the binary is missing) finds the newest `cli-v*` release through the GitHub API, verifies the checksum and installs to `~/.local/bin`
- CLI: a separate SwiftPM package (`cli/`, macOS 14, no dependencies, `swift build -c release` → `cli/.build/release/stupid-social`) since the app's package is iOS-only. Token in `~/.config/stupid-social/token` (0600, `stupid-social pair <token>`) or `STUPID_SOCIAL_TOKEN`; port override `STUPID_SOCIAL_PORT`. On connection refused it runs `open -g -b tech.stupid.StupidSocial` and retries for 30 s. `--json` prints the result (watch: the raw lines)
- Verified on the Mac app (observed 2026-10-30): `chats`, and `read` on X, Bluesky, Spotify and Apron chats, answer while the app is hidden, and `watch` (one chat and all chats) stays connected for a minute hidden. Sending was verified only in the simulator with demo chats; demo adapters keep sends in memory per view model, so a demo `watch <chat>` never sees a send from another command. Telegram signed in on another device must sign in on the Mac too before its chats list

### Open issues
- Row preview update via `follow` unverified with a message arriving while the chat is open
- Swipe-to-reply trackpad behavior unverified on Mac

## Push notifications

### Relay
- Why Web Push to a relay: native apps can't get a browser push subscription and the demo server lacks `relay`; registering `webpush` everywhere gives one format (RFC 8291 `aes128gcm`) and one decryption path. The relay seals device token, environment and the server's VAPID key into the endpoint id: no database, and pushes signed by another key are refused.
- `workers/push-relay` (Worker `stupid-push-relay`, `push.stupidtech.net`), APNs key `U7W6CW4B57` (team `6JKMV57Y77`). Valid-key check: a fake device token gets `BadDeviceToken` with the right key, `InvalidProviderToken` otherwise. Secrets `RELAY_SECRET` and `APNS_PRIVATE_KEY`; changing `RELAY_SECRET` invalidates every endpoint. Responses: unsigned push 403, APNs device rejection 410, refused provider token 502. Tests: `bun run push-relay:test`.
- APNs provider token cached in `caches.default` (key `https://push.stupidtech.net/internal/apns-provider-token/<key id>`, 49 minutes) as well as memory, because new isolates each signed a fresh token and APNs answered 429 `TooManyProviderTokenUpdates` (refreshes more often than every 20 minutes) (observed 2026-10-27). That URL isn't publicly served (Worker answers 404).

### App & extension
- `StupidSocialPush` module and `StupidSocialNotificationService` extension (`tech.stupid.StupidSocial.NotificationService`; an extension bundle ID must start with the app's, so it keeps `tech.stupid`). App has `aps-environment` and App Group `group.tech.stupid.StupidSocial` APNs environment comes from the embedded profile's `aps-environment` (absent in App Store/TestFlight → production).
- `ApronPushService.register` chains registrations per server so each reuses the relay URL the previous saved; concurrent runs created duplicate registrations for one `push_id` (one push each). Stale ones can be removed with probe `push-unregister`.
- Apron notification: title "<sender> in <room>" (sender alone in DMs), subtitle the server host only, no mention/reply labels; donated as one-to-one from a person named like the title (no group name/recipients) so iOS keeps that title; person identifier per sender. Plain text body with named mentions, avatar via Communication Notifications, inline Reply action (category `apron.message`). Undecryptable payloads fall back to "Apron / New message". Details in `docs/TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` § Apron Push.
- `CommunicationNotification` (shared by extension and Debug): sets the body before `updating(from:)` and returns the updated content untouched (a mutable copy may lose the communication context). Extension Info.plist declares `NSExtensionAttributes.IntentsSupported = [INSendMessageIntent]`; app `NSUserActivityTypes` has `INSendMessageIntent`. Entitlements: communication, Siri, App Group.
- The extension logs each run to `push-extension-log.json` in the App Group (no names/text), including Telegram pushes it leaves alone. After a real push: no entry = extension didn't run (no `mutable-content`), `no avatar` = sender photo not saved, an error names the failing step.
- `AppDelegate` notification delegate methods use completion-handler forms called from a `@MainActor` task: the `nonisolated async` `userNotificationCenter(_:didReceive:)` returned off-main and UIKit aborted (SIGABRT) on cold launch from a notification.
- Replies to a notification when the app is terminated: iOS launches it in the background and a standalone `ApronService` connects; failures surface as a "Reply not sent … unknown room" notification.

### Telegram notifications
- The extension has no Telegram session, so the app writes each user avatar loaded through `tgfile:` (`avatar-user:<id>-…`) to `telegram-avatars/<id>.jpg` in the App Group (`TelegramAvatarStore`), and group photos (`avatar-chat:`/`avatar-channel:` keys) to `telegram-avatars/group-<id>.jpg` (named by id alone, since history avatar keys call supergroups `chat:` too).
- A Telegram sender or group without a saved photo gets `InitialsAvatar.png` (first letters of up to two words, white on `ChatUserColor` of the user's or group's id, as the Chats row and chat avatars draw it), so the notification never falls back to the app icon. The extension log's `hasAvatar` still means a saved photo
- Telegram notifications the extension rewrites (messages from a person) get category `telegram.message`, with the same inline Reply action as Apron's (`apron.reply`). `NotificationRouter.sendTelegramReply` finds the chat's access hash and account from the Chats list row (`ChatListViewModel.chat(for:)`); when the app was launched in the background for the reply and has no list, each Telegram account's own client looks the peer up in its newest 100 dialogs of both folders (`TelegramClient.listedPeer`). A private chat gets a plain message; a group reply quotes the pushed `msg_id`. Failures post "Reply not sent". Channel posts and pushes the extension leaves alone have no Reply. Checked on the simulator with `stupidsim push` (which runs the extension) and `notification expand` (a reply to a Saved Messages push landed there, 2026-11-22); the background-launch path is untested.
- Notifications carry no picture of a photo message: Telegram's push names only the message (`loc-key` `MESSAGE_PHOTO`, `msg_id`), and the extension has no MTProto session to download it. Whether type-1 pushes carry Telegram's `attachb64` (a serialized `Photo`/`Document`, which Telegram's own app downloads in its extension) is unchecked; Settings → Debug's payload keys of a real photo push would tell.
- `TelegramPushChat` recognizes a Telegram push without `loc-key`: `channel_id`, `chat_id` or `from_id`, in `custom` or top level, as strings or numbers; never an Apron push.
- `TelegramPushNotification` handles private messages (`MESSAGE_*`, `from_id`) and group messages from a person (`CHAT_*`, `chat_from_id`, sender in `loc-args[0]`); channel posts and messages sent as a channel/group are left as Telegram sent them. Group reaction pushes (observed 2026-11-20: top-level `chat_id`, `msg_id`, `react_emoji`, `aps.alert {title: group, body: "<name>: 👍 to your \"…\""}`, no `custom`, no `chat_from_id`) don't name the reactor's id, so the group is the sender, with its photo, and the body is kept; other group pushes without `chat_from_id` are left alone. Text messages show the text alone; other kinds keep Telegram's line, formatted from the app bundle's `Localizable.strings` (the extension has none). Also accepts `aps.alert` `title`/`body` without `loc-key`, and sets title and body itself.
- Group messages are donated as a group conversation: `INSendMessageIntent` with the group name as `speakableGroupName` (carrying the group photo), two recipients (sender plus a placeholder for the user, since iOS needs more than one for a group); the sender keeps their own photo.
- `title`/`body` group pushes: `TelegramPushNotification.senderName` is optional; the sender comes from `alert.subtitle` when present (title "<sender> in <group>"), as Telegram-iOS's `NotificationService.swift` reads title/subtitle/body. Otherwise `CommunicationNotification.telegram` shows the group as the sender (name, photo, one recipient, no group name) with the body unchanged — using the group title as sender in a group conversation showed the group twice. Covered by `TelegramTests`.
- Opening a Telegram chat removes delivered notifications naming it (matched by chat in `userInfo`, not the thread, which is Telegram's own when the extension doesn't run), and the open chat's notifications aren't presented.

### Simulator limits
- `stupid-app run --simulator` signs with only the App Group entitlement: it strips `aps-environment` (simulator refuses team-less signatures carrying it, POSIX 163), so no APNs token, and without the communication entitlement iOS ignores the intent (app icon drawn). Ad hoc re-signing with either makes the simulator refuse to launch, so avatars can only be checked on a device.
- `simctl push` doesn't run Notification Service Extensions; `stupidsim push` runs the real `.appex` for `mutable-content` payloads and prints its output. `stupidsim notification open|reply` drive banners.

### Open issues
- Whether Telegram's APNs pushes carry `mutable-content` (needed for the extension, hence any avatar) is undocumented and unconfirmed on device; avatar drawing (Telegram and Apron, including group photos) has never been seen on a device.
- iOS's communication layout may not show `subtitle`; if the Apron server host is missing on device, move the line into the body.

## Build, signing & release

### Signing & provisioning
- Adding a capability (iCloud KVS, App Groups, associated domains such as `applinks:link.apron.chat`) means enabling it on the App ID and regenerating both development and App Store profiles; a profile created before the capability doesn't authorize it (`run --network` / archive fail signing)
- Saving App ID capabilities invalidates but keeps old profiles; with the default name `signing setup` then reuses them or fails HTTP 409 on duplicate names (e.g. two `tech.stupid.StupidSocial Development`). Pass a new `--profile-name` (e.g. `"iCloud"` → `iCloud tech.stupid.StupidSocial Development`), or delete the duplicates in App Store Connect to reuse the default name
- stupid-app ≥ 0.0.23 replaces profiles that don't authorize the source entitlements; the App Store profile is now `tech.stupid.StupidSocial AppStore 2`. Development profile: `signing setup --kind development --bundle-id tech.stupid.StupidSocial --profile-name <name> --udid …`
- The notification extension needs its own distribution profile: `signing setup --kind distribution --bundle-id tech.stupid.StupidSocial.NotificationService`. `signing setup` once hit HTTP 500 on the extension step; its existing profile stays valid if its App ID didn't change
- App Group must be created and assigned to both App IDs in the developer portal: the API can enable `APP_GROUPS` but not create/assign a group (profiles made before that carry an empty group list)
- `ubiquity-kvstore-identifier` needs a stupid-app whose `EntitlementDeriver` substitutes `$(TeamIdentifierPrefix)`/`$(CFBundleIdentifier)` and matches concrete values against trailing-`.*` profile grants; older CLIs fail with "ubiquity-kvstore-identifier value mismatch"
- `stupid-app run --mac` needs this Mac in the development profile

### Build & packaging
- `Info.plist` sets `CFBundleName` ("stupid social", and "stupid social notifications" for the extension): stupid-app fills it, the executable and the display name from the product (`StupidSocial`) unless the project's plist overrides them, and macOS shows the bundle name. The executable stays `StupidSocial`, which is the process name. `stupid-app run --mac` installs to `.build/…/.MacInstall/<display name>.app` from stupid-app's change of 2026-10-30 (previously `StupidSocial.app`, which LaunchServices listed as "StupidSocial")
- `StupidSocialCore` has SwiftPM resources (`.process("Resources")`); stupid-app ≥ 0.0.21 copies them as `StupidSocial_StupidSocialCore.bundle` (older versions omitted them and `Bundle.module` crashed). Juicebox (static-library XCFramework) needs stupid-app ≥ 0.0.22
- stupid-app's packer sets deployment target 18.0 and the active SDK via linker `-platform_version`; otherwise `LC_BUILD_VERSION` gets `sdk 18.0` and iOS 26 Liquid Glass disappears
- App icon comes from `iconPath` in `stupid-app.yml` (`Resources/AppIcon.appiconset/Icon-1024.png`); the root `Assets.xcassets` doesn't affect packaging
- Background fetch needs only `UIBackgroundModes = fetch` in Info.plist; no custom entitlements file for background modes
- `/.release` (archives, profiles, upload logs) and `__pycache__/` are gitignored

### Install & test
- `stupid-app build` emits the simulator app to `.build/arm64-apple-ios/debug/`; a stale `.build/arm64-apple-ios-simulator/debug/` copy exists and `simctl install` of it silently installs an old binary. Always install with `stupid-app run --simulator`
- Phone not on USB: `stupid-app run --network --udid <udid>`; fails if the iPhone is locked (developer disk image can't mount)
- `xcodebuild test` runs pass `-collect-test-diagnostics never`; otherwise any failure triggers `simctl diagnose --timeout=600` (up to 10 minutes)
- Unit tests: the shared `StupidSocialTests` scheme (`.swiftpm/xcode/xcshareddata/xcschemes/`) builds only the test target and what it depends on (Core, Push, the kit, Juicebox), not the app's screens, which `StupidSocial-Package` compiles too. `xcodebuild` keeps its own DerivedData, apart from `stupid-app`'s `.build`, so a change to a model every file uses compiles twice. Build once, then run as often as needed:
  - `xcodebuild build-for-testing -scheme StupidSocialTests -destination "platform=iOS Simulator,id=<udid>"` (25 s after a model change, 5 s with none; measured 2026-11-17)
  - `xcodebuild test-without-building -scheme StupidSocialTests -destination "platform=iOS Simulator,id=<udid>" -collect-test-diagnostics never -only-testing:StupidSocialTests/<Class>` (about 40 s, nearly all of it launching the test runner in the simulator; the tests take well under a second)

### Release & TestFlight
- Internal TestFlight builds can also come from GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/testflight.yml`, manual trigger): stupid-app's composite action builds on Ubuntu and signs and uploads through a release session on the signing broker (vault.stupidtech.net), which holds the App Store Connect key and the distribution key; the run authenticates with its GitHub OIDC token and the owner approves it in the vault app. The broker's release policy for this repo names this workflow file, `refs/heads/main` and both bundle ids. `TelegramAPI.json` comes from the `TELEGRAM_API_JSON` repository secret. The action bumps `CFBundleVersion` past the latest upload and pushes that commit. External TestFlight and the `testflight/<build>` tag stay manual. `.swift-version` is 6.4 because imported-SDK (Linux) builds refuse a Swift whose major.minor differs from the Xcode 27 SDK's.
- Archive releases only from the main checkout. A git worktree or fresh clone lacks the gitignored `Sources/StupidSocialCore/Resources/TelegramAPI.json`, and the build then ships without Telegram ("Telegram isn't available in this build"). Before uploading, check the IPA has it: `unzip -l .release/StupidSocial.ipa | grep TelegramAPI.json`
- External TestFlight via ASC API (`scripts/asc_external.py`: groups, get-build, add-to-group, submit): add the build with `POST /v1/builds/{id}/relationships/betaGroups` (`POST /v1/buildBetaGroups` 404s), then `POST /v1/betaAppReviewSubmissions`. `stupid-app release external-beta` does this
- `release external-beta --whats-new` rejects a value starting with `-`; pass `--whats-new=<note>`
- The CLI's external-review poll reads `submissionState`/`state`, but the response exposes `betaReviewState: APPROVED`, so it keeps polling after approval (observed 2026-10-10); verify with `release status --live` or submit with `--no-wait`
